### Now, the Definition of the Farha Sequence.

Those [letters] born of the <term>Abjad</term> <note>Arabic: *Abjad*. A system where each of the 28 letters of the Arabic alphabet is assigned a numerical value.</note> system are twenty-eight in number. Among them, the sequence of figures is: **Lahyan**, **Ankish**, **Humra**, **Bayaj**, **Nusrat-dakhil**, and **Nusrat-kharij**. ॥ 28 ॥ 

Likewise, **Ataba-dakhil**, **Ataba-kharij**, **Farha**, **Naki**, **Kanzul-dakhil**, **Kanzul-kharij**, **Jama'at**, **Uqba**, **Ijtima**, and **Tarikh** follow in the <term>Farha</term> <note>Arabic: *al-Farah*, meaning "Joy" or "Glory." This sequence is specifically ordered for certain calculations.</note> order. ॥ 29 ॥ 

The letter <term>Fa</term> <note>The Arabic letter 'F', which has a numerical value of 80, but here refers to its position in a specific twelve-fold division.</note> is the source of the twelve divisions; thus, starting from **Lahyan**, there are twelve segments. Just as before, when the expansion <note>Sanskrit: *prastāra*. The formal layout or grid of geomantic figures.</note> is made on the dice, the "back" consists of the four <term>Ghair</term> <note>Perso-Arabic: *Ghayr*, meaning "other" or "external." In Ramala, these are the secondary figures or those not belonging to the primary elemental set.</note> letters. ॥ 30 ॥

**Commentary—** Now the **Farha** sequence is explained: the Persian letters in the **Abjad** sequence number 28. In the first element, because the <unclear>starting</unclear> <note>Sanskrit: *shūna*. Possibly referring to the 'zero' point or a specific starting vowel.</note> letter is 'A' <note>The letter Alif.</note>, **Lahyan** occupies the first house. In the second house is its opposite, **Ankish**. In the third house, due to the air-point of the letter 'V' <note>The letter Waw.</note>, is **Humra**; in the fourth is its opposite, **Bayaj**. Following this order: **Lahyan**, **Humra**, **Ankish**, **Bayaj**, **Nusrat-dakhil**, and **Nusrat-kharij**. ॥ 28 ॥ 

Then **Ataba-dakhil**, **Ataba-kharij**, **Farha**, **Naki**, **Kanzul-dakhil**, **Kanzul-kharij**, **Jama'at**, **Uqba**, **Ijtima**, and **Tarikh**. This is the **Farha** sequence. ॥ 29 ॥ 

Because the letter **Fa** is associated with the number 12, there are **Lahyan** and the others in the twelve houses; they are established according to the numerical order of their respective elements. After this, there are 4 figures from the **Ghair** <note>The "external" or "remaining" letters/figures.</note> letters. The expansion should be understood as being performed as before. ॥ 30 ॥

### Now, the Definition of the Assah Sequence.

This sequence, known as **Assah**, consists of sixteen divisions and was written by the ancients as the "seed" of the situation. I shall describe it exactly as it is, following the ancient traditions without hesitation. ॥ 31 ॥ 

[The sequence is:] **Lahyan**, **Ataba-dakhil**, **Ataba-kharij**, **Jama'at**, **Farha**, **Humra**, **Naki**, and **Uqba**...

...and those [figures] known as **Kanzul-dakhil** <note>Arabic: *Kanz al-Dakhil*, "The Inward Treasure."</note> and **Kanzul-kharij** <note>Arabic: *Kanz al-Kharij*, "The Outward Treasure."</note>. Likewise, **Ankish** and **Ijtima** are placed at the front, followed by **Tarikh**. These were spoken of by the <term>Yavanas</term> <note>Sanskrit: *Yavana*. Historically referring to Greeks, but in the context of Ramala, it refers to the Perso-Arabic practitioners from whom this system of geomancy was adopted.</note> in the **At-sah** sequence. ॥ 32 ॥

**Commentary—** In the **At-sah** <note>Likely derived from the Arabic *al-Tas'ah*, meaning "The Nine," or a specific rearrangement of the figures.</note> sequence, no specific reason was observed for the positioning of the segments <note>Sanskrit: *khaṇḍa*. Refers to the sixteen individual geomantic figures.</note>. Therefore, it is written here exactly as the previous teachers have recorded it. In this system, one considers the increase in age, the path of the figure, and the level of certainty. The sequence is as follows: **Lahan**, **Ataba-dakhil**, **Ataba-kharij**, **Jama'at**, **Farha**, **Humra**, **Naki**, **Uqba**, **Nusrat-kharij**, **Nusrat-dakhil**, **Kanzul-dakhil**, **Kanzul-kharij**, **Bayad**, **Ankish**, **Ijtima**, and **Tarikh**. This is the **At-sah** order as stated by the Yavanas. ॥ 31 ॥ 32 ॥

In this way, I have separately described seven meaningful sequences of rows. Now, the "Second Strength" is explained through the difference and unity of the rows and the houses. ॥ 33 ॥
If the houses of the <term>Shakl</term> <note>Arabic/Persian: *Shakl*, meaning "form" or "figure."</note>, the grid, and the segments are the same, they provide strength. If two, three, or more houses are in unity, the figure becomes even more powerful. ॥ 34 ॥
In the absence of unity between the row and the house, the figure being questioned becomes weak. ॥ 35 ॥

->**Now, the Strength of Sight (Aspect).**<-

Figures see one another in the seventh position, which is known as the "Sun" sign. This does not occur in the positions ahead. Now, the "Third Strength" born of this sight is described. ॥ 36 ॥

A segment <note>original: *khaṇḍa*; refers to one of the sixteen houses or figures in a geomantic chart</note> that is aspected by auspicious or friendly figures is endowed with strength. ॥

In this manner, one must quickly evaluate the strength of the house of the query <note>original: *pṛcchālaya*; the specific house related to the seeker's question</note>, the segment itself, its friends, and the witness <unclear>figures</unclear> <note>original: *sākṣī*; the "Witnesses" are specific houses in a geomantic shield that summarize the reading</note>. ॥
Success in an undertaking is achieved through figures that possess strength, not those that are weak; likewise, if the enemy’s side is powerful, success is hindered. ॥ 38 ॥

One aspected by a malefic or enemy is weak, and one aspected by a neutral figure is of medium strength. ॥ 37 ॥ In this query, first consider the strength of the house of the question and its friendly witness segments. From the powerful—

### III. The Requisites for a Query

Without the proper requisites for a query, success in this art cannot be achieved, even if one follows the words of the Guru. Therefore, I shall describe the six-fold requisites for a query, which are the cause of all success, in a concise manner. ॥ 1 ॥ The first is the *Kilavanch* <note>A technical method for calculating or arranging figures</note> strength and weakness; the third <term>is the</term> calculation of the *Masha-ativahayam* <note>Technical terminology for the flow or movement of the figures</note>. The fourth is the *Yajam*, the fifth is the *Sarakam*, and the sixth is the calculation of the *Takarar-martavam*. ॥ 2 ॥

<meta>This page transitions from the conclusion of the "Second Gem" (calculating the strength of figures) to the "Third Gem," which focuses on the practical tools and methods for generating a geomantic chart (Prastara), particularly when the traditional dice are unavailable.</meta>

<page-num>33</page-num>
<header>Third Gem 3.]</header>

**Commentary**—Now, in the Third Gem, the tools for the query are described. Without these tools, even with the teacher’s instruction, success is not achieved. Therefore, for the sake of success in all queries, I shall describe the six subtle types of tools useful for divination. ॥ 1 ॥
...the fifth is **Imtiyaz** <note>original: *imatiyāja*; refers to the "distinction" or "differentiation" between figures</note>, the sixth is **Nigamtasir** <note>original: *nigamatāsīra*; refers to the "influence" or "effective result" of the figures</note>, then **Takrar** <note>original: *takarāra*; the "repetition" of a figure in different houses</note>, and **Artav** <note>original: *ārtava*; relates to seasonal or periodic timing</note>. ॥ 2 ॥

> The root cause of all [divination] is the dice; however, if they are unavailable, ॥
> I shall now describe the method spoken by the **Yavanas** <note>original: *yavanokta*; referring to the Perso-Arabic practitioners who pioneered Ramala geomancy</note> for generating an omen. ॥ 3 ॥
> Restraining the breath, one should draw rows of dots within a circular shape; then, one should count them carefully. ॥
> A figure corresponding to that number should be established; if the count exceeds sixteen, divide by sixteen and take the remainder. ॥ 4 ॥
> From that, the [first figure] is placed in the houses, then the seventh from that [becomes the second], and the seventh from that the third, and the seventh from that the fourth. ॥
> Having thus established the four quarters, the expansion <note>original: *prastāra*; the full geomantic chart or "shield"</note> should be accomplished according to the previously stated method. ॥ 5 ॥

**Commentary**—The primary root cause of the expansion is the previously mentioned dice (*pashaka*). If, by chance, the dice are not available, I will now describe the method spoken by the **Yavanas** for generating an omen (*shakuna*). ॥ 3 ॥
The seeker should hold their breath and draw as many dots as possible in a circle until the breath is released. Then, count them. If they are more than 16, divide by 16. The remaining number (within 16) determines which figure from the geomantic sequence is taken as the first. ॥ 4 ॥
From that first figure, the seventh one becomes the second; the seventh from that becomes the third; and the seventh from that becomes the fourth house. After preparing the first, second, third, and fourth figures in this way, the rest of the expansion is prepared according to the previously described method. This completes the chart. ॥ 5 ॥

> Having somehow created the expansion, then **Inkilab** <note>original: *inkilāba*; meaning "inversion" or "transformation," a technique where dots and dashes are flipped</note> [is performed]. ॥
> I shall now describe that process concerning the first and fifth houses. ॥ 6 ॥
> By combining the pairs of the second and sixth, the third and seventh, and the fourth and eighth, and taking their halves [remainders], ॥
> One should first place those four and from them proceed with the expansion. ॥ 7 ॥ **Inki**...

<meta>catchword: Inki (beginning of the word Inkilab)</meta>

<summary>This page introduces the "Third Gem," focusing on the tools of Ramala. It details an alternative method for generating geomantic figures using breath control and random dots when traditional dice are unavailable, and begins explaining "Inkilab" (transformation) techniques.</summary>

<meta>The previous page concluded the introduction to the Third Gem, explaining how to generate a geomantic figure using dots and breath control when dice are unavailable. This page continues the specific technical transformations of the chart, specifically the "Inkilab" (inversion/transformation) process and the "Maratib" (ranks) of the figures.</meta>

<page-num>34</page-num>
<header>[ Gem of Ramala -- ]</header>

> In the chart generated through **Inkilab** <note>original: *lābotha-prastāra*; here referring to the transformed chart or 'Inqilab'</note>, within the fifteenth house <note>original: *tithigehe*; literally 'the house of the lunar day,' meaning the 15th</note>, the figure of **Jamaat** <note>original: *jamāta*; the geomantic figure of 'The Congregation,' consisting of four pairs of dots</note> always resides. One should always look to its root. ॥ 8 ॥
> From that foundation, the truth of the query is spoken; thus is the matter attained. Through this name **Inkilab**, the arrangement of the classes and colors of the figures is determined. ॥ 9 ॥

**Commentary**—First, after constructing a chart by any method, I shall describe the method for performing **Inkilab** <note>original: *inkilāva*; a Persian term for 'transformation' or 'inversion' used in geomancy</note>. First, combine the figures of the 1st and 5th houses; then the 2nd and 6th; the 3rd and 7th; and the 4th and 8th. Using the previously mentioned rules for addition, prepare four [new] figures. Then, construct a full chart from those four. ॥ 7 ॥

This transformative process is called **Inkilab**. In the fifteenth house of a chart made this way, the figure **Jamaat** <note>The figure of "The Congregation" represents stability or a gathering; its appearance in the 15th house acts as a mathematical proof of the calculation's accuracy</note> always appears. One must examine its root. ॥ 8 ॥

From the root (the foundation) of that figure, the truthfulness of the seekers' questions is confirmed. Only through this technical transformation called **Inkilab** can the arrangement of categories and colors be determined; this cannot be achieved through any other method. ॥ 9 ॥

> The consideration of strength and weakness is the second tool. The third is known as **Maratib** <note>original: *marātiva*; from the Arabic 'Maratib' meaning ranks or degrees</note>, which is of five types, categorized by the moment. ॥ 10 ॥
> These are named **Ruvai** (Fourfold), **Khumasi** (Fivefold), **Sudasi** (Sixfold), **Savai** (Sevenfold), and **Samani** (Eightfold), as spoken by the ancients. When pairs are formed from the preceding houses in sequence, if there is a conflict in the first few, it is considered a 'Great Enmity.' ॥ 11 ॥
> A slight conflict is called **Jihvati** <note>original: *jihvati*; likely referring to a 'tongue' or 'sharpness' in the reading</note>; in worldly affairs, this brings obstacles. However, the wise should know that there are three types here that are 'Without Enmity' (neutral). ॥ 12 ॥
> All sections of the chart situated in the house of the **Jihvaka** <note>The 'Tongue' or 'Indicator' house</note> should be known as creators of obstacles, following the sequence of omens. ॥ 13 ॥

**Commentary**—The discrimination of strength and weakness is the second tool previously mentioned. The third tool is given the name **Maratib**. From these five types of names, one can immediately understand from the chart—

<summary>This page explains "Inkilab," a transformation technique where house figures are combined to create a new chart that always results in "Jamaat" in the 15th house. It also introduces the "Maratib" (ranks) system for evaluating the strength and relationships between figures.</summary>

<keywords>Inkilab, Jamaat, Maratib, chart transformation, geomantic houses, calculation of strength, Ruvai, Khumasi, Sudasi, Savai, Samani</keywords>

<meta>The previous page discussed the mathematical transformation of geomantic charts called Inkilab and the significance of the figure Jamaat (The Congregation) appearing in the fifteenth house as a verification of the reading. This page continues detailing the technical nomenclature for figures based on their dot counts and introduces the next analytical tools, Imtiyaz and Siram.</meta>

<page-num>35</page-num>
<header>The Third Gem 3. ]</header>

...one should know. ॥ 10 ॥ 

Ancient masters have given specific names to the figures based on the number of dots they contain, from four dots up to eight:
*   A figure with four dots is called **Ruvai** <note>original: *ruvāī*; from the Arabic *rubāʿī*, meaning 'quaternary'</note>.
*   A figure with five dots is called **Khumasi** <note>original: *khumāsī*; from the Arabic *khumāsī*, meaning 'pentagonal' or 'of five'</note>.
*   A figure with six dots is called **Khudasi** <note>original: *khudāsī*; likely a corruption of the Arabic *sudāsī*, meaning 'hexagonal' or 'of six'</note>.
*   A figure with seven dots is called **Savai** <note>original: *savāī*; from the Arabic *sabāʿī*, meaning 'of seven'</note>.
*   A figure with eight dots is called **Samani** <note>original: *samānī*; from the Arabic *thamānī*, meaning 'of eight'</note>.

The relationship between the 1st and 5th houses, as well as the 2nd and 4th houses, is known as **Jidda** <note>original: *jidda*; from the Arabic *zidd*, meaning 'opposite' or 'contrary'</note>, which signifies a minor enmity. ॥ 11 ॥

Even though this **Jidda** is considered a "minor" enmity, it nonetheless creates obstacles in the timing of worldly affairs. Among these relationships, the third is "without enmity" <note>neutral or harmonious</note> and is considered auspicious; the wise should understand it as such. ॥ 12 ॥ 

In the chart arrangement <note>original: *prastāra*</note>, if all segments fall into the houses of **Jidda** (the enemy houses), they should be known as causes of obstacles. This method should be observed within the sequence of omens. ॥ 13 ॥

## On Imtiyaz <note>Distinction or Comparison</note>

> **Imtiyaz** is the fourth tool, which arises from combining two segments or figures. If it is produced from two auspicious figures, it grants success; if from two inauspicious ones, it brings great obstacles. ॥ 14 ॥
>
> If it is produced from one auspicious and one inauspicious figure, the result is good at first but becomes bad later. If it arises from one inauspicious and one auspicious figure, the result is bad at first but turns out well in the end. ॥ 15 ॥

**Commentary—**Now, the fourth tool called **Imtiyaz** <note>original: *imtiyāja*; an Arabic term for 'distinction' or 'preference'</note> is explained. This is the name given to the figure that results from the combination of two other figures <note>specifically, the mathematical "adding" of dots to form a new shape</note>. The consideration here is this: a result formed from the union of two auspicious figures grants the fulfillment of the task. One formed from two inauspicious ones creates excessive obstacles in the work. ॥ 14 ॥ 

If it is formed from one auspicious and one inauspicious figure, it gives an auspicious start but an inauspicious conclusion. If it is derived from an inauspicious figure followed by an auspicious one, it gives an inauspicious start but an auspicious conclusion. ॥ 15 ॥

## On Siram <note>Progression or Movement</note>

> The knowledge of the relationship between two houses is now called **Siram** <note>original: *sīram*; likely from the Arabic *sayr*, meaning 'traveling' or 'path'</note>. The first house belongs to the querent; the other houses are understood through their relationship to it. ॥ 16 ॥
>
> Regarding my children, fame, or the acquisition of wealth—whenever a question is asked, the first house shall represent the querent.

<vocab>Point (Vindu), Quaternary (Ruvai), Of Five (Khumasi), Of Six (Khudasi), Of Seven (Savai), Of Eight (Samani), Opposite/Enmity (Jidda), Chart (Prastara), Omen (Shakuna), Distinction (Imtiyaz), Figure (Shakal), Progression (Siram)</vocab>

<page-num>३६</page-num>
<header>[ In the Ramala Navaratna ]</header>

<meta>The text consists of Sanskrit verses followed by a commentary in a mix of Sanskrit and early Hindi/Braj Bhasha.</meta>

From that [the first house], the son; from there, the one called the wife; from there, the house of gain; from there, the fire [eighth house] is the body. ॥ 17 ॥ The second from the body is called wealth; in this way, one finds the wealth and happiness of the body. This is called **Tasir** <note>original: *tasīra*; from the Arabic *taʾthīr*, meaning 'influence' or 'connection'</note>, which is described through variable numbers; by this method, the treasury of the inquiry-house is distinguished. ॥ 18 ॥

**Commentary**—Knowing a specific place through its connection is called **Tasir**. It is said that for the one asking the question, the first house is established. By its connection, other houses are known. ॥ 16 ॥ For example, if someone asks, "When will my son's brother-in-law obtain wealth?" the querent is established at the first house. From there, the fifth figure <note>original: *shakal*; one of the 16 geomantic symbols</note> is that of his son. The seventh from that is the daughter-in-law. The third house from that [the daughter-in-law's house] belongs to the querent's son's brother-in-law. One should look at the second house from that particular house to see his gain of wealth. Alternatively, one looks from the house of that brother-in-law. This third method is the fifth instrument <note>original: *upakaraṇa*</note>. By this method, the specific location of the inquiry is extracted. ॥ 17 ॥ 18 ॥

->**Regarding Takrar (Repetition)**<-

When a figure is repeated, it is called **Takrar** <note>original: *takrāra*; from the Arabic *takrār*, meaning 'repetition' or 'reiteration'</note>. When a figure situated in a question-house occurs again, ॥ 19 ॥ the results should be declared based on that, following the same path previously described. One should know auspicious results in auspicious houses and speak of inauspicious results in inauspicious houses. ॥ 20 ॥ In the eighth house <note>original: *jihasthāne*, likely a corruption of *mṛtyusthāne* or referring to the 'place of the tongue/enemy'</note>, it is declared inauspicious due to the influence of death. Knowing this, the wise man should speak, so that the inquiry never becomes fruitful in vain. ॥ 21 ॥

->**Thus ends the Instruments of Inquiry, the Third Gem in the Sri Ramala Navaratna.**<-

**Commentary**—When a single figure <note>original: *shakal*</note> occurs through repetition, it is called **Takrar**. If a figure situated in the question-house is also present in another house, then the results should be stated according to the previously mentioned method. For example, if the figure **Lahyan** <note>original: *lahyāna*; the geomantic figure *Laetitia*, representing 'Greater Fortune'</note> is in the house of wealth...

<summary>This page explains 'Tasir', the method of 'turning the chart' to find houses for relatives (like a son's brother-in-law), and introduces 'Takrar', the interpretive significance of a geomantic figure appearing in multiple houses.</summary>
<keywords>Tasir, Takrar, derivative houses, Lahyan, geomancy, Ramala Navaratna, house relationships, divination tools</keywords>

...is the question, and if the same figure found in the first house <note>the Ascendant or "Lagna"</note> is also in the tenth house, then one should understand that wealth will be obtained from the royal side <note>meaning from the government, authorities, or a patron</note>. However, there is a specific consideration here: if that figure <note>original: *shakala*; one of the 16 geomantic symbols</note> is in a favorable position, it yields a good result; if in an unfavorable position, the result should be declared as bad. ॥ 19 ॥ 20 ॥ If it is in the *Jidda* <note>original: *jidda*; referring to the first house or the "essence" of the chart</note> position, it is unfavorable; in others, it should be judged as favorable according to the *Maratib* <note>original: *marātiba*; the hierarchical ranks or sequence of the houses</note>. In this way, the predictions made by a scholar using the method of repetition known as *Takrar* never go in vain. ॥ 21 ॥

->This concludes the Third Gem in the Mahidhara commentary on the *Ramala Navaratna*. ॥ 3 ॥<-

## Now the Fourth Gem.

The various analytical methods <note>original: *prakṣālaya*</note> are the root of all inquiries. Therefore, I shall now describe the different types of questions related to the First House <note>original: *tanu*; the house of the body</note> and the other houses. ॥ 1 ॥ From the First House, one considers: bodily happiness, birth, fruits/results, limbs, livelihood and lack thereof, lifespan, effort, strength, the power of an action, royal policy, and peace. From the Second House <note>the house of wealth</note>, one considers: the poor, the wealthy, the giver of wealth, the arrival of wealth, assistants, those sitting nearby, neighbors, business ventures (other than buying), the miser, the donor, and livelihood. ॥ 2 ॥

**Commentary**—The analytical methods are the foundation of all questions, therefore the divisions of the First House and others are described here. ॥ 1 ॥ Regarding the **first house**: body, happiness, sorrow, birthplace, bodily parts such as fingers, lifespan, death, effort, strength, the success of a task, royal policy, and peace—these many questions are to be judged from the first house. Regarding the **second house**: the destitute, the rich, the one who gives money, the arrival of money, an assistant, a person sitting beside one, neighbors, efforts to gain money (other than through purchasing), the miser, the donor, and livelihood—all these considerations belong to the second house. ॥ 2 ॥

Regarding the **third house**: one’s own people, servants, kin, friends, satisfaction, brothers, sisters, and great endeavors. Knowledge, prowess, entertainment, and the desire...

Also: underground treasures, trees, one’s own or foreign lands, the time limit for a task, the ultimate result, death, concerns about land, fields, health, crossing a distance, vehicles, and bodies of water. ॥ 4 ॥

**Commentary:** In the **third house**, one looks for: one's own people, servants, relatives, friends, satisfied brothers and sisters, specific efforts, the beginning of a task, knowledge, prowess, humor, short-distance travel, dreams, and the act of seeing. <meta>This page continues the analysis of the final houses in the Ramala astrological system, specifically focusing on the "Witness" houses (14th, 15th, and 16th). These houses act as a synthesis of the entire reading. The text then transitions from the house descriptions to a formal prayer to Ganesha, marking the beginning of a new section on the practical application of the science.</meta>

<page-num>41</page-num>
<header>The Fourth Jewel 4. ]</header>

> Truly, the fifteenth house stands as the witness, just as the moon acts as the witness to the lotus and the water. ॥ 15 ॥ In the sixteenth house, one must contemplate the final resulting fruit that arises from both happiness and sorrow. Like the soul that witnesses all physical forms, this house is the sole witness for all houses and figures associated with the earth element and beyond. ॥ 16 ॥

->Thus ends the analysis of the sixteen houses and their distinct objects of consideration.<-

**Commentary—** The action and its cause are the two primary things to be considered. By examining the mutual relationship between these two from the **fourteenth house**, the good or bad results are proclaimed. This house acts as a witness through the influence of the Fire element <note>original: "Agnitattva"</note> in the sequence. Its nickname is "The Mirror" because it shows the reflection of cause and effect. ॥ 14 ॥ 

The **fifteenth house** is the universal witness. For this reason, it is called the <term>Chief Justice|Prangvivaka</term>, the head clerk, or the judicial inquiry. In this house, the final determination of the good or bad results of a living being's actions is made. The determination of the intellect is also made here. It is certain that just as there is a witnessing relationship between the fruit and the lotus <note>original: "phala evam kamala," likely implying the moon's effect on the blooming of certain lotuses</note>, this fifteenth house is the witness for all other positions. ॥ 15 ॥ 

In the **sixteenth house**, one considers the final result <note>original: "parinam phala"</note> arising from joy and distress. Just as the primary <term>geomantic figure|shakala</term> and its multiplier are used to extract the <term>Witness Fragment|Sakshi Khanda</term> from the thirteenth house to complete a task, this fragment serves as a witness through the Earth element <note>original: "Bhumitattva"</note> and others. This concludes the discussion of the sixteen figures and the different objects to be considered within them. ॥ 16 ॥

---

### Prayer to the Chosen Deity for the Purpose of Questioning.

> Having placed the beautiful lotus feet of Lord Ganesha <note>original: "Ganapa-pada-pankeruham"; Ganesha is the elephant-headed deity of wisdom and the remover of obstacles</note> within my heart, and having churned the entire ocean of Ramala <note>original: "Ramalambudhi"; a metaphor for masterfully condensing the vast knowledge of geomancy</note> here on the surface of the earth, I shall now speak of that method which causes great wonder and adorns the assembly of the wise—the method proclaimed by all the experts of the Ramala science. ॥ 17 ॥

<meta>This page marks the transition from the theoretical definitions of the sixteen houses to the practical application of the science, titled "The Miracle of Inquiry." It begins with an invocation and describes the daily ritual for a practitioner to predict the day's visitors.</meta>

<page-num>42</page-num>
<header>[ The Nine Jewels of Ramala </header>

<term>Commentary|Tika</term>—Now the author, in order to describe the **Miracle of Inquiry** <term>Prashnachamatkar</term>, first offers salutations to his chosen deity. By holding the beautiful lotus feet of Lord Ganesha within his heart and churning the ocean-like expanse of the <term>Ramala|Geomancy</term> science, he extracts its essence. He says: "I now reveal this knowledge, which adorns the assembly of scholars and produces great wonders—a secret of the Ramala scriptures previously hidden from the world." ॥ 17 ॥

> Rising early, the expert in Ramala should bow to his Guru and meditate upon the pair of lotus feet of Lord Vishnu <note>original: "Murari"</note> while in a state of purity. To know how many seekers will come to him today, the wise practitioner should then cast the pair of pure dice. ॥ 18 ॥

<term>Commentary|Tika</term>—In the morning, the practitioner of Ramala, upon rising from sleep and becoming pure through the performance of daily rituals <note>such as bathing and morning prayers</note>, should bow to his teacher <term>Guru</term>. He must meditate upon the two lotus feet of Lord Vishnu and, to determine how many people will come to ask questions today, he should first cast the dice <term>Pasha</term> onto the divination board. ॥ 18 ॥

> One should create the spreadsheet <term>Prastara</term> as previously described. If figures appear in the "House of the Two Halves" <note>original: "Dvijadahalaye," referring to the 11th House</note>, then seekers will arrive; if not, they will not come. ॥ 19 ॥ The number of people arriving corresponds to the number of segments <term>Khanda</term> or points found there. Male figures indicate the arrival of men, female figures indicate women, and neuter figures indicate the arrival of those of a neutral gender. ॥ 20 ॥
> 
> The arrival from outside is spoken of through the segments of the first house <note>original: "Anjad-khandai"</note> in a similar manner. By taking the figures of the first, eleventh, eleventh, and thirteenth houses <note>original: "Adya-vishwa-rudra-shakra," using numerical codes: 1, 11, 11, 14</note> and reducing them to one, ॥ 21 ॥
> 
> If the resulting figure relates to the question, the seeker comes with a full hand <note>bringing a gift or offering</note>; otherwise, they come empty-handed. One should then contemplate: "What is in their hand?" ॥ 22 ॥

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for "The Miracle of Inquiry." While the previous page established the morning ritual and the basic check for whether visitors would arrive, this page provides the specific mathematical and elemental logic used to determine the number, gender, and even the hidden possessions of those seekers.</meta>

<page-num>43</page-num>
<header>The Four Jewels, Section 4</header>

> If the element of Fire is present, the seeker holds a metal object in their hand. 
> In the element of Air, it is a living being; in Water, a plant; and in Earth, a gem. ॥ 23 ॥
> Thus, having determined the upcoming numbers and details, one should analyze them separately in sequence. 
> Having created the layout as previously described, one should then examine the inquiry. ॥ 24 ॥

**Commentary**—According to the dice <note>original: "pashaka"</note>, create the layout <term>Prastara|A geometric chart used in Ramala</term>. If figures appear in the <term>Vijadaha</term> <note>a specific arrangement or sequence of the houses in Ramala, often referring to the 11th house as the house of gains or arrivals</note> houses, then seekers will come to visit. If they are not in those houses, they will not come. 

The calculation works like this: if the figure <term>Shakal|The four-line geomantic sign</term> in the first house of your layout matches the figure that belongs in the first house of the "Vijadaha" sequence, that counts as one seeker. Follow this for every house; however many figures match their proper "Vijadaha" positions, that is how many seekers will arrive. If no figure is in its correct place, no one will come. ॥ 19 ॥ 

There is a further specific consideration: the number of people who arrive matches the number of matching figures. Furthermore, the number of male figures represents the men who will come, the female figures represent women, and the neuter figures represent those of neutral gender who will come to ask questions. ॥ 20 ॥

This sequence can also be viewed through the <term>Abjad</term> <note>A system of alphanumeric calculation of Arabic origin used to determine the "hidden" meaning of figures</note> segments. To see if a seeker is carrying something, first take the 13th house and the 11th house, and then the 14th and the 2nd houses, to derive two figures. From those, create one final resultant figure. If that resultant figure appears anywhere within the inquiry's layout <term>Prastara</term>, the seeker will arrive holding something in their hand. If that figure is not found in the layout, the seeker will come empty-handed. Now, one must determine what exactly is in the seeker's hand. ॥ 21 ॥ 22 ॥

If the top position of that derived figure is the **Fire element**, say that the seeker has a metal object <term>Dhatu</term> in hand. If it is the **Air element**, it is a living being <term>Jiva|Such as a bird, insect, or small animal</term>. If it is the **Water element**, it is a root <term>Mula|Referring to plants, wood, or botanical items</term>. If it is the **Earth element**, say it is a gem <term>Mani|Referring to stones, jewels, or minerals</term>. ॥ 23 ॥

In this manner, having understood the upcoming numbers and other details, consider each aspect separately and in the proper order. Create the layout exactly as it has been described and then examine the specific question. ॥ 24 ॥

What is in the mind? What is the "seed" <note>original: "bija," referring to the root cause or origin</note> of the inquiry? What is the specific variation of the question, and will there be success or failure? To attain the goal of this investigation, I shall now explain the proper method. ॥ 25 ॥

**Commentary**—I will now describe the method for understanding what is in the questioner’s mind, the root cause of their visit, the specific category of their question, and whether they will achieve success or failure in their endeavor through deliberation and effort. ॥ 25 ॥

The first aspect is "What is in the mind"; the second is the "Cause" of that thought; the third is the "Variation" <note>the specific type or category of the problem</note>; and the fourth is "Success or Failure." In every type of question, these four factors are present. ॥ 26 ॥

In their proper sequence, these are derived from the omens <note>original: "shakun"</note> located in the four angles <note>original: "kendra," the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses of the chart</note>. These figures, which arise from the casting of the dice, are placed there. Their "Witnesses" <note>the figures in the 13th, 14th, 15th, and 16th houses</note> are multiplied by them in order, and the resulting components should be arranged separately. ॥ 27 ॥

Likewise, other central houses and their fifth houses act as witnesses to them. These should be multiplied by one another, and from that, four distinct components are to be created here once again. ॥ 28 ॥

Having multiplied the previously obtained figures in their proper order, four specific results are revealed. Every variation arising from the seeker's heart can be known through the planets and the omens in their sequence. ॥ 29 ॥

**Commentary**—The primary factors in a reading are: first, the thought in the mind; second, the cause behind that thought; third, the specific type of inquiry; and fourth, the success or failure of the work. These four factors are present in all types of questions. 

The method for determining these is as follows: in the <term>Shakun|A system of omens or the starting sequence of geomantic figures</term> order, one looks at the <term>Shakal|The four-line geomantic figures</term> present in the <term>Kendra|The cardinal houses or angles of the chart: the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</term> (1, 4, 7, and 10).

In the <term>Sakshi-Prastara|The Witness Layout, specifically the final four houses of the 16-house chart</term>, the figures in houses 13, 14, 15, and 16... <note>The text concludes mid-sentence, describing the multiplication of the primary houses by the witness houses to determine the four outcomes.</note>

<header>The Fourth Jewel 4.</header>

...and keep them separately. ॥ 27 ॥ Then, in the grid <note>original: "prastara," the chart of sixteen geomantic houses</note>, the figures <note>original: "shakal," the four-line geometric symbols</note> located in the four angles (houses 1, 4, 7, and 10) have their respective "fifth" houses—meaning houses 5, 8, 11, and 14—as their witnesses. By multiplying the figures in these angles with the figures in their witnesses, four new figures are produced. Then, using the previously derived separate segments, one must combine them to prepare exactly four final figures. ॥ 28 ॥

From the three previously obtained segments, by following the sequence (matching the first with the first, the second with the second, and so on), once these four figures manifest, one should use them to declare the aforementioned "matter of the mind" and other factors. Specifically: 
- From the first figure, tell the "hidden thought," which is called <term>Zamir</term> in <term>Persian</term> <note>the inner secret or conscience of the seeker</note>. 
- From the second, tell the "cause" of the inquiry. 
- From the third, tell its "specific nature" <note>original: "bhed," the variations or details of the problem</note>. 

These three aspects should be understood through the sequence of omens <note>original: "shakun"</note>. Finally, from the fourth figure’s consideration of auspiciousness or inauspiciousness, one must declare the "success or failure" of the undertaking. ॥ 29 ॥

## Now, an Example of These

> Someone arrives and asks: "O practitioner of <term>Ramala</term> <note>the art of sand-divination or geomancy</note>! Tell me quickly and clearly, from this single inquiry, the hidden thoughts of my heart, their cause, and their specific nature. Speak also of success or failure, if the purpose behind this is truly sound." ॥ 30 ॥

> By placing the pairs of points on the earth... in the first, second, third, and fourth houses... the figures such as *Humra* and *Kabhajulkhari* <note>Sanskritized names for the geomantic figures Al-Humra (The Red) and Al-Kabsh al-Khariji (The Outgoing Ram)</note> arise from these. The grid becomes clear according to the method previously stated. ॥ 31 ॥

> From the combination of the omens known as *Lahyan* and *Vishvakad* <note>Al-Lahan (The Tongue/Voice) and Al-Jama'a (The Gathering)</note>... and from the combination of *Farha*, *Humra*, and the figure in the fifth house... ॥ 32 ॥ From these combinations, the result is *Atbad*, and its *Farha* becomes *Takarvam*... in the third omen, its "house" resides in the perceived thought. ॥ 33 ॥

<term>Commentary</term>—This is an example of the four factors of an inquiry. Someone comes and asks: "O Practitioner! Tell me the hidden reasons of my mind and their variations within this single question, including success and failure, if the intent here is good." ॥ 30 ॥

When a question was posed in this manner, the dice were cast. In the first position, there appeared <gloss>—<br>—<br>⠶<br>⠶</gloss> <note>the figure 'Lahan' or 'Laian'</note>. In the first house is Lahan <gloss>⠶<br>—<br>—<br>⠶</gloss> <note>original: "Lahan," a geomantic figure</note>. In the thirteenth house of the chart is Kabzul-dakhil <gloss>—<br>⠶<br>⠶<br>—</gloss> <note>original: "Acquisitio" or Inward Capture</note>. When these were added together <note>original: "yoga," the process of combining the lines of two figures</note>, Farha <gloss>⠶<br>⠶<br>⠶<br>⠶</gloss> <note>original: "Laetitia" or Joy</note> was produced in the first house. This figure belongs to the third house in the sequence of omens. Therefore, one should declare that the question in the seeker's <note>original: "prashta"</note> mind is related to the third house. According to the previously stated considerations of the houses, in the third house, one should speak of a question regarding a brother or a short journey <note>original: "samip gamana"</note>. This is how the work of the first center <note>original: "kendra"</note>, the 1st house, is completed. ॥ 32 ॥ 33 ॥

> When the figure **Ankish** is placed in the house of the omens, and the figure **Ukla** is in the chart, their combined product is **Lahyan**, which resides in the "mountain" of the question. ॥ 37 ॥ If **Nakki** and **Lahyan** are combined at the end of that sequence, the resulting house in the omens must be spoken of; one should contemplate its meaning according to the rules of the science. ॥ 38 ॥

**Commentary**—In the seventh house of the omen sequence, **Ankish** <gloss>—<br>—<br>—<br>⠶</gloss> <note>original: "Iankish," the figure Cauda Draconis</note> is present. From their combination, **Lahyan** <gloss>—<br>—<br>⠶<br>⠶</gloss> <note>original: "Lahyan," the figure Fortuna Major</note> was produced. Now, in the seventh house (a central "pivot" house) of the question chart, **Nusrat-dakhil** <gloss>—<br>⠶<br>—<br>—</gloss> <note>original: "Nusrat-dakhil," the figure Fortuna Minor</note> is present. Because of this, in the fifth and eleventh houses, there is **Nakki** <gloss>⠶<br>—<br>—<br>⠶</gloss> <note>original: "Nakki," the figure Conjunctio</note>. From their addition, **Lahyan** <gloss>—<br>—<br>⠶<br>⠶</gloss> was formed. This geomantic figure is in the first house of the omens; therefore, the first house reveals the specific nature of the question. The nature of the question should be explained as relating to the first house <note>The 1st house concerns the self, the body, and the general state of the seeker</note>. ॥ 37 ॥ 38 ॥

> The house of success or failure is the "Sky" <note>the 10th house</note> in the omens; it is filled with the figure **Jamat**. Its witness in the "King" <note>the 10th house of the chart</note> is **Farha** <gloss>⠶<br>⠶<br>⠶<br>—</gloss>. From their addition, **Nakki** is produced, which is then multiplied by **Ankish**. ॥ 40 ॥ There, the portion of **Farha** is auspicious and brings about the fulfillment of the seeker’s goal. In the fifth house of the omen chart, success is achieved through its assistance. ॥ 41 ॥

O Seeker! You desire here to know of your brother’s constant happiness while he resides in another land. The accomplishment of all your intended actions—such as the pursuit of wealth and new ventures—will indeed come to pass for you through the companionship and help of your friends and sons. ॥ 42 ॥

**Commentary**—The success or failure of an undertaking is determined by the tenth "pivot" <note>the tenth house, traditionally governing career and success</note>. In the sequence of omens, the figure **Nusrat-kharij** <term>⁚⁚</term> <note>original: "Nusrat-kharij," the geomantic figure for External Victory or Acquisitio</note> is in the tenth position. Its "witness" is the figure in the sixteenth house of the chart, which is **Farha** <term>⁝</term> <note>original: "Farha," the figure Laetitia or Joy</note>. Its "witness" is the fourteenth house (which is the fifth house from the first), containing **Nusrat-kharij** <term>⁚⁚</term>. From their addition, **Nusrat-kharij** <term>⁚⁚</term> was produced again. Now, by adding **Ankish** and **Nusrat-kharij**, the figure **Farha** <term>⁝</term> was produced. This figure is auspicious. Because of this, the work will be successful. This figure is located in the fifth house of the omen sequence; therefore, one should say the work will be accomplished with the help of a son, as the fifth house relates to children. ॥ 40 ॥ 41 ॥ After revealing these four divisions, say to the seeker: "O Seeker! At this moment, you are asking about the well-being of your brother who resides in a foreign land, and the success of all your professional efforts and financial plans will be achieved through the assistance of a friend and a son." ॥ 42 ॥

-> Regarding another matter. <-

If the figure produced from the first and the thirteenth houses is found anywhere in the chart, then the question is certainly for one's own sake. If that figure is not present anywhere, the question is for someone else. ॥ 43 ॥
The house in the chart where that figure is found indicates the subject of the question, following the sequence of omens. This is described as the second method for answering "silent questions." ॥ 44 ॥

**Commentary**—Another method is described: add the first and thirteenth figures together. If the resulting figure exists in any house of the chart, the seeker is asking about themselves. If that figure does not appear in the chart at all, they are asking on behalf of another person. ॥ 43 ॥ The specific house where that figure is found tells you the topic of the question (based on that house's traditional meaning). This is the second method taught for "silent questions" <note>Muka-prashna, where the diviner must guess the seeker's hidden thought</note>. ॥ 44 ॥

Now the third method for silent questions is described. Sum all the points of the chart and divide the total by sixteen. The remainder... ॥ 45 ॥

<meta>This page continues the discussion on geomantic techniques from the previous page, shifting focus from a specific case study (about a brother's welfare) to general methods for "Silent Questions" (Mookaprashna). <page-num>41</page-num>
<header>The Fourth Gem [4]</header>

The silent question should be understood through the houses starting from the thirteenth to the fourth. Now, I shall describe another alternative tradition for the fourth method of silent questions. ॥ 46 ॥

**Commentary —** Now, the third method for the "Silent Question" <note>the art of determining what a person is thinking without them speaking</note> is explained: Count all the dots in the entire geomantic chart and divide the total by 16. Whatever remains ॥ 45 ॥ <note>this refers back to the logic of the previous verse</note> should be looked for in the 13th, 14th, 16th, or the 1st through 4th houses. Locate where that number matches the sequence of the omen’s figures. By combining that figure with its "witness" <term>Sakshi</term> <note>a secondary figure used to verify or "testify" to the meaning of the primary figure</note>, you will find a resulting figure. Whatever house that figure belongs to in the omen sequence, you should declare the question to be related to the matters of that house. Now, the fourth method of answering a silent question is described according to an alternative tradition. ॥ 46 ॥

From the Earth elements of the figures in the fifteenth, thirteenth, ninth, and first houses; from the Fire elements located in the first, second, third, and fourth figures; ॥ 47 ॥ from the Water elements of the figures in the fifth, sixth, seventh, and eighth houses; from the Air elements of the eighth and twelfth figures; and from the Water element of the fourteenth figure; ॥ 48 ॥ and from the Fire element of the figure in the sixteenth house—take these dots and lines <note>the binary components that make up a geomantic figure</note> in order and form them into four sections. ॥ 49 ॥ From these four, produce two, and from those two, derive one final figure. Based on that final section, the question should be explained as before. ॥ 50 ॥

**Commentary —** In the 15th, 13th, 9th, and 1st positions, take the Earth element <term>Bhumi-bhaga</term> <note>the bottom-most level of a geomantic figure</note> of the figures; and from the 1st, 2nd, 3rd, and 4th figures, take the Fire element <term>Agni-bhaga</term> <note>the top-most level of a geomantic figure</note>; ॥ 47 ॥ similarly, from the figures in the 5th, 6th, 7th, and 8th houses, take the Water element <term>Jala-bhaga</term> <note>the second level from the bottom</note>; from the 8th and 12th figures, take the Air element <term>Vayu-bhaga</term> <note>the second level from the top</note>; from the 14th figure, also take the Water element; ॥ 48 ॥ and from the figure in the 16th house, take the Fire element. Arrange these dots and lines <term>shunya rekha</term> <note>literally "zeroes and lines," referring to the dots and dashes of the geomantic script</note> in order...

<summary>This page describes the third and fourth mathematical methods for determining the subject of a "silent question" by calculating remainders from the chart and combining specific elemental components (Fire, Air, Water, Earth) from various houses.</summary>

### Another Method

> I shall now describe another method, the fifth one, for the silent question. From the Fire elements <note>the top line of a geomantic figure</note> of the ninth, tenth, eleventh, and twelfth sections (houses) ॥ 51 ॥ the diviner should first construct one figure according to the previous sequence. From that, the experts in geomancy should declare the question based on the house belonging to the *Adah* order <note>a specific traditional sequence of geomantic houses</note>. ॥ 52 ॥

**Commentary —** This is another technique for determining a silent question, specifically the fifth type. The astrologer <term>Daivajya</term> should prepare one figure from the Fire parts of the 9th, 10th, 11th, and 12th houses using the method mentioned before. The geomancy expert <term>Ramal-vid</term> should then state the question related to whichever house that resulting figure occupies within the *Adah* sequence. ॥ 51 ॥ 52 ॥

### Now, the pinnacle method for all silent questions via the movement of dots from the fifteenth house.

> If there is an odd number of dots <term>Shunya</term> <note>literally "zeros," referring to the single points in a figure</note> in the fifteenth house, it indicates confusion. If there is only a line <term>Rekha</term> <note>the double points in a figure</note> or half-points, use the technique to obtain a pair of dots. ॥ 53 ॥ Without the success of the silent question appearing between the houses of the five elements and the void, no result will emerge. Therefore, one intent on the method of "opening" knowledge should establish a pair of dots in the fifteenth house. ॥ 54 ॥

**Commentary —** Now, the "Crest-Jewel" or pinnacle method for all silent questions is described by moving the dots <term>Shunya-chalana</term> from the fifteenth house. If the fifteenth house does not contain an odd-numbered dot section, it shows confusion. That is to say, when the figure *Jamaat* <term>Laetitia</term> <note>a figure consisting of four lines or eight dots, representing a "gathering"</note> appears in the 15th house, it is called a "locked chart" <term>Baddha-Prasthara</term> (or *Jaychaband*). A question cannot be determined from this. When there is a line or a dot, the "movement of the dot" occurs; for this reason, employ a technique for its "opening" <note>the process of unlocking a chart that seems to yield no answer</note>. ॥ 53 ॥ If there are not two dots in the fifteenth house...

## Now, the Unlocking (Udaghatana)

> In the first and thirteenth, the fourth and fourteenth, the seventh and fifteenth, and the tenth and sixteenth houses—one should multiply these pairs respectively after creating the sub-sections. ॥ 55 ॥
> From these, a spread <term>Prasthara</term> should be produced, and from that, one should move the dots. If a "Meeting" <term>Jamat</term> <note>original: *Jamat*, from Arabic *al-Jama'ah*, a specific geomantic figure representing a gathering or union</note> appears there again, then this specific method should be performed: ॥ 56 ॥
> The wise practitioner should combine the four figures: "The Prolix" <term>Lahyan</term> <note>original: *Al-Lihyan*</note>, "The Red" <term>Humra</term> <note>original: *Al-Humra*</note>, "The White" <term>Bayaj</term> <note>original: *Al-Bayad*</note>, and "The Inverted" <term>Ankish</term> <note>original: *Al-Inkis*</note>, respectively, with the first four figures of the unlocked chart. ॥ 57 ॥
> After producing these four sections, one must create the spread from them. The gain of dots will surely occur; from that, one should move the dots. ॥ 58 ॥

**Commentary**—Now the method for "opening" a "locked chart" <note>a chart that is 'closed' or stagnant, preventing a clear reading</note> is explained. By combining the 1st figure with the 13th, the 4th with the 14th, the 7th with the 15th, and the 10th with the 16th, four new sections should be prepared according to the sequence of multiplication. ॥ 55 ॥

From these four, create a chart and then perform the "movement of dots." If even then a "Meeting" <term>Jamat</term> figure appears in the 15th house, then use this method: ॥ 56 ॥

Take the figures known as "The Prolix," "The Red," "The White," and "The Inverted" <note>these are the Sanskritized names for the standard Arabic geomantic figures Al-Lihyan, Al-Humra, Al-Bayad, and Al-Inkis</note> and add them respectively to the first four figures of the chart that was previously opened. ॥ 57 ॥

By making four figures in this way and creating a spread from them, you will certainly obtain dots in the 15th house. At that point, you should perform the "movement of dots." ॥ 58 ॥

> Through coming and going, there are movements of the sky (dots). One's own "Birth Section" <note>a specific house or figure assigned to the start of the inquiry</note> and its connection to the dots... in the houses of the arrow and the moon, from here to the left and right...

...proceeding through the different sections in order. ॥ 59 ॥ Having considered the movement from the first and second figures, the answer to the seeker's question should be obtained. At that time, one should understand the strength of the "Witness" <term>Sakshi</term> <note>original: *Sakshi*; in geomancy, this refers to the figures that testify to the final outcome</note> to be half-strength, and if two are involved, the four witnesses possess full power. ॥ 60 ॥ The question should be interpreted from the section where the first "Zero" <term>Shunya</term> <note>original: *Shunya*; refers to a single dot in a geomantic figure, representing the element of Air or movement</note> is located, following the sequence of the years, months, and days. The limit for the question's fulfillment is declared through the second figure. ॥ 61 ॥

### Thus ends the Fourth Gem: The Analysis of Silent Questions in the "Nine Gems of Ramala." ॥ 4 ॥

**Commentary** — The "Movement of Points" <term>Bindu-chalana</term> <note>a technique used to trace a specific dot from the final outcome back to its house of origin</note> is described as follows: look at the points located in the fifteenth house within the positions of the "Zero" <note>the single dots</note> or the "Primary Elements." One must examine their place of origin—that is, from which houses they were derived. Wherever its birth occurred, its true position should be known.

From the 15th house, moving left or right, its position should be found within the first eight houses. For example, if a "Zero" is in the position of the Air element (or any other element) in the 15th house, one must see where that same "Zero" moved among the houses from 1 to 14. If it moved to the thirteenth house, then the "Zero" of that same element will be found in the ninth or tenth house. If it goes to the fourteenth house, the elemental "Zero" will be found in the 11th house. If it is positioned in the 11th house, it will be found in the fifth or sixth house. There, in whichever house the "Zero" occupies the same elemental position, that house should be known as its true location. If the "Zero" goes to the 12th house, its location should be known by where the "Zero" of that same element appears in the 7th or 8th house. This is the method for the "Movement of Zeros." 

In this same way, one should move the second "Zero." Whichever house it stops in between 1 and 8, that house should be known as the "Witness" <term>Sakshi</term> of the question. ॥ 59 ॥ By moving the first and second points from the 15th house, the houses where they find their position are the ones from which the answer to the question should be judged.

<summary>This page concludes the Fourth Chapter (Gem) on Silent Questions, detailing the "Bindu-chalana" method which traces dots from the 15th house back to the first eight houses to identify the focus of a seeker's hidden question.</summary>

<meta>This page concludes the technical instructions for "Silent Questions" (Mookaprashna) from the fourth chapter and transitions into the fifth chapter, which focuses on questions related to the twelve astrological houses (Bhavas). It explains the final calculations for determining the timing of an event (Past, Present, Future, or Accidental).</meta>

<page-num>५३</page-num>
<header>The Fifth Gem 5. ]</header>

At that time, based on the relative strength <term>balabala</term> <note>the comparative power or influence of different geomantic figures</note> of the "Witness Section" <term>Sakshi-khanda</term> and the house identified through the first "Movement of Points" <term>Bindu-chalana</term>, four types of questions should be declared. ॥ ६० ॥

From the geomantic figure <term>shakal</term> where the first "Zero" <note>original: *shunya*; referring to a single dot in a geomantic figure, signifying movement or the element of Air</note> is located, the question should be answered following the sequence of the years. From the position of the house found through the second "Movement of Points," the time limit <term>miyada</term> or the duration of the task must be stated. In all questions, there are four types of "Time" <term>kala</term> as follows:

1. Past <term>bhuta</term>
2. Future <term>bhavishya</term>
3. Present <term>vartamana</term>
4. Accidental <term>akasmika</term>

The "Accidental" is that which is already established or self-evident, but suddenly something else is added to it. The method for determining this is as follows: if that Witness Section <note>Sakshi-khanda</note> is in a specific row and house within the *Unmahat* <note>a specialized arrangement or "great row" in a geomantic grid</note> series, and it also appears in the preceding row of that same house, then it is "Accidental." If it appears in its own row but in the row immediately behind it, the result is "Past." If it appears in the row ahead of it, it is "Present." And if it remains within its own house but is in the row below it, the result should be declared as "Future." 

One must also remember that the figure indicating the "Accidental" time for a row is the fourth row. Specifically, consider the lower part of the preceding section. The "Future" of the fourth row is told from the first row. The "Past" at the start of a row is told from the 8th figure. The "Present" of the 8th figure is told from the 1st figure. In other words, if there is no figure ahead in a particular section, one must look to the final row of the opposite side as being "ahead." ॥ ६१ ॥

### Thus ends the Fourth Gem, titled "The Analysis of Silent Questions," in the Mahidhari-language version of the *Nine Gems of Ramala*.

"Who is this?" she <note>the Goddess Parvati</note> asked with a smile; may that son of Shiva <note>the god Ganesha, the remover of obstacles</note> grant us the gift of speech [wisdom]. ॥ १ ॥ By his grace...

Everywhere, one must remember this triad.

**Commentary** — "Who is this who has the body of a man but the face of an elephant, with a luster like the morning sun and fire in the eye of his forehead?" Outgoing <term>kharija</term>, Exit <term>nirgama</term>, Entry <term>dakhila</term>, Arrival <term>agamana</term>, Possession <term>savitta</term>, and Fixed <term>sthira</term>—these are the distinctions in a question. Their explanations are found in the previously written charts. ॥ ३ ॥ Understanding the houses and their types such as "Exit" in the manner previously stated, the scholar, at a favorable...

<page-num>५५</page-num>
<header>The Five Notes 5. ]</header>

Having meditated upon the feet of Yama <note>the god of death/justice</note>, one's own chosen deity, and the Guru who is an expert in geomancy <term>Ramalajña</term> <note>one who knows the science of Ramala or sand-divination</note>, one should throw the pair of dice. ॥ ४ ॥ After creating the grid <term>prastara</term> according to the previously mentioned method, and having understood the total strength and weakness <term>balabala</term> as well as the various types like opposition <term>maratina</term> <note>original: *marātin*; derived from the Arabic *mu‘āradāt*, referring to opposing or inimical figures</note>, one should then deliberate on the question. ॥ ५ ॥ There is a method for a second question arising from the first via reversal <term>inkidaba</term> <note>original: *inkidāba*; likely from the Arabic *inqilāb*, meaning the inversion or turning of a figure</note>; however, here the method of questioning via the first dice-grid is being described. ॥ ६ ॥ The first house is the primary "House of the Question"; the 14th is the Witness <term>Shukra</term> <note>literally "Venus," but used here as a technical term for the 14th house in a geomantic chart</note>, and the 15th is the Minister <term>Mantri</term> <note>referring to the Judge or final arbiter of the chart</note>. Everywhere, one must keep these three primarily in mind. ॥ ७ ॥

# Now, the success or failure of questions regarding Departure and Arrival is stated:

If the question here is of the type called Departure <term>Nirgama</term> <note>questions about leaving, going abroad, or an object being moved out</note>, and if Outgoing <term>Kharija</term> <note>original: *khārija*; geomantic figures that signify "going out"</note> and similar figures appear in these three houses [the 1st, 14th, and 15th], then there will be success in the task. It is achieved easily with auspicious figures, but through great toil if the figures are inauspicious or of the opposition <term>marativa</term> type. ॥ ८ ॥ If Incoming <term>Dakhila</term> <note>original: *dākhila*; figures signifying "entering"</note> figures appear in those houses, there will be failure; there is no success even with effort, even if the figures are auspicious. Success might only come from immense struggle, and never with inauspicious figures. ॥ ९ ॥ With Fixed <term>Savita</term> <note>original: *sāvita*; from the Arabic *thābit*, meaning stable or stationary</note> figures, there is only a hope of success, but the task may never actually be completed. With Changing <term>Munkaliva</term> <note>original: *munkalīva*; from the Arabic *munqalib*, meaning mobile or reversing</note> figures, success comes after a long time if they are auspicious, but the result is very slight if they are inauspicious. ॥ १० ॥ Thus one should speak regarding a question about Departure, having understood and weighed the potential strengths. If the question here is regarding Arrival <term>Agama</term> <note>questions about someone coming, returning, or profit coming in</note>, and if Incoming figures appear in these houses, it always brings success without any effort. ॥ ११ ॥ With auspicious figures, success might come after a long time and great effort; but with Outgoing <term>Kharija</term> figures, there is no success in an Arrival question, regardless of effort. Even with auspicious or inauspicious figures, there is failure if they are moving; however, a good Fixed <term>Savita</term> figure brings success after a delay. ॥ १२ ॥ With an inauspicious Fixed figure, the ancients say the success is mediocre. Even with a good Changing <term>Munkaliva</term> figure, success is not predicted; however, with an inauspicious one, success may eventually be attained through great effort. ॥ १३ ॥

<summary>This page provides specific rules for predicting the outcome of Departure and Arrival questions by analyzing the figures in the 1st, 14th, and 15th houses of a geomantic chart. Success is largely determined by whether the figure's "direction" (Incoming, Outgoing, Fixed, or Changing) matches the nature of the question.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the specific rules for interpreting "Departure" (Nirgama) and "Arrival" (Agama) questions based on the four fundamental states of geomantic figures (Outgoing, Incoming, Fixed, and Mobile). It builds on the previous page's identification of the 1st, 14th, and 15th houses as the primary keys for judgment.</meta>

<page-num>५६</page-num>
<header>[ In the Ramala Navaratna— </header>

**Commentary**—Now, the success of **Departure and Arrival** questions is explained. If it is a Departure <term>Nirgama</term> <note>questions about leaving, traveling, or sending something out</note> question, and if in the three locations <note>the 1st, 14th, and 15th houses</note> there are Outgoing figures <term>Kharij</term> <note>original: *khārij*; geomantic figures that represent movement away from the center</note> and these are also Auspicious <term>Shubha</term>, then the success of the task will be very good. However, if there are Opposing figures <term>Marativ</term> <note>original: *marātib*; refers to specific placements or "ranks" of figures that can imply opposition</note> or Malefic figures <term>Papa</term>, then the task will be accomplished only with great effort. ॥ ८ ॥

If Incoming figures <term>Dakhil</term> <note>original: *dākhil*; figures representing movement toward the center</note> appear in those three locations, the task will not be successful. Even if those Incoming figures are Auspicious, one should say that success is uncertain and would only occur through immense struggle. If those figures are Inauspicious <term>Ashubha</term>, then the task will not succeed even with the greatest effort. ॥ ९ ॥

If those figures are Fixed <term>Sabit</term> <note>original: *thābit*; figures representing stability and lack of movement</note>, there will only be the hope of success, but complete fulfillment will never occur. If they are Mobile <term>Munkalib</term> <note>original: *munqalib*; figures representing sudden change or reversal</note>, it will take many days. If some figures are Auspicious and some are Malefic, there will be only a small amount of success. ॥ १० ॥

In this way, having understood these three figures as Departure and so on, and having considered their strength, the scholar should answer the question. 

If it is an Arrival <term>Agama</term> <note>questions about someone coming, returning, or a gain being received</note> question, and if Auspicious Incoming <term>Dakhil</term> figures are in those three locations, the task is accomplished without any effort at all. ॥ ११ ॥

If Inauspicious figures appear among these, success occurs only after great effort and a long delay. If Outgoing <term>Kharij</term> figures appear, the task is not successful even if one puts in the effort. If there is a mixture of Auspicious and Malefic figures, failure results despite one's efforts. If they are Fixed <term>Sabit</term> figures, success comes after a delay. ॥ १२ ॥

If the figures are Fixed <term>Sabit</term>, success occurs even if they are Malefic. For "Medium" figures, the ancient Masters of Ramala <term>Ramalacharya</term> have prescribed medium results. If they are Mobile <term>Munkalib</term>, success is not predicted. If the figures are Auspicious but the Malefic influence is weak <note>literally "powerless"</note>, success occurs—this is an alternative opinion <term>matantara</term>. 

The wise practitioner should observe the relative comparison of strength and weakness <term>balabala</term> between the Auspicious and Malefic influences and give the result with logic. The consideration of the strength of Auspicious and Malefic influences is primary. Whichever side is seen to have more strength, that result should be declared as primary. In this, remember this much: Outgoing <term>Kharij</term> figures represent Departure, Incoming <term>Dakhil</term> represent Arrival, and Fixed <term>Sabit</term> represent a stable state. ॥ १३

<summary>This page details the specific outcomes for "Departure" and "Arrival" inquiries based on whether the geomantic figures are Outgoing (Kharij), Incoming (Dakhil), Fixed (Sabit), or Mobile (Munkalib). It emphasizes that success depends on the alignment between the nature of the question and the movement type of the figures in the key houses.</summary>
<keywords>Ramala, geomancy, Nirgama (Departure), Agama (Arrival), Kharij (Outgoing), Dakhil (Incoming), Sabit (Fixed), Munkalib (Mobile), Balabala (strength/weakness), divination rules</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the specific rules for geomantic interpretation within the "Ramala Navaratna." It transitions from questions of movement (Departure and Arrival) to "Fixed" questions—those concerning things intended to stay or remain stable (like building a house or staying in a job). It then provides a technical "tie-breaker" rule for when the figures in a reading contradict one another.</meta>

<page-num>५७</page-num>
<header>The Fifth Gem [5.]</header>

### Now, the Fixed Question.

> -> In a fixed question, when both figures are Auspicious and **Incoming** or **Fixed**, then the task will be firm and successful due to happiness. If they are Inauspicious, success comes only through great effort. ॥ १४ ॥ **Outgoing** and **Mobile** figures act as the destroyers of a fixed task. In this manner, the greatest knower of geomancy should explain all three types of questions. ॥ १५ ॥ <-

**Commentary**—Now the rule for the **Fixed Question** <term>Sthira-prashna</term> <note>questions regarding permanent or long-term situations</note> is explained: In a fixed question, if the geomantic figures are **Fixed** <term>Sabit</term> <note>original: *thābit*; figures representing stability</note> and **Incoming** <term>Dakhil</term> <note>original: *dākhil*; figures representing entry</note>, and they are Auspicious, then the fixed task will be successfully completed. If those same Fixed and Incoming figures are Inauspicious, the task will be achieved only after much hardship. ॥ १४ ॥ **Outgoing** <term>Kharij</term> <note>original: *khārij*; figures representing departure</note> and **Mobile** <term>Munkalib</term> <note>original: *munqalib*; figures representing change or reversal</note> figures are the destroyers of any task intended to be stable or fixed. In this way, a practitioner who is the best among those who know geomancy <term>Rammal</term> should deliver the judgment for the group of questions. ॥ १५ ॥

### Now, the Rule for Resolving Doubts.

> -> When two or three figures do not agree, and their results conflict regarding the task's outcome, then multiply the house of the question by its half. From that resulting figure, speak as before. ॥ १६ ॥ In a question, the wise practitioner should also observe the strength of the generated figure, its elemental nature, and whether it is Outgoing or otherwise. He should then describe the various differences such as the querent's departure, based on whether the indicators are Auspicious or Inauspicious, or lead to happiness or struggle. ॥ १७ ॥ In this way, the practitioner should state everything from the conclusion of the question to the person asking, without any doubt. If one's heart is pure and has faith in the Master of Geomancy, then success is certain. ॥ १८ ॥ <-

**Commentary**—If, while interpreting a question and its result, two different types of figures appear which contradict one another—for example, if one figure indicates stability <term>Sthira</term> and the other indicates movement <term>Chara</term>, or if one is **Outgoing** and another is **Incoming**, or if one is Auspicious and the other is Inauspicious—then one must use a "tie-breaker." Multiply the value of the **Fourteenth House** <note>represented here as the 'Question Segment'</note> and from the resulting figure produced by that multiplication, the practitioner should explain the answer using the previously described methods. ॥ १६ ॥ Within the question, one must also evaluate the inherent strength of the figure that has been produced. Consider its natural disposition <term>Svabhava</term> based on its position, and whether it is **Outgoing** <term>Kharij</term> or of another type. One should then tell the querent whether there will be a departure or other outcomes, and whether it will result in good or bad fortune, or happiness versus hard labor. ॥ १७ ॥ In this manner, the practitioner should confidently explain the entire conclusion of the reading to the querent. If the practitioner’s own mind is pure and they possess a heart that trusts in the **Master of Geomancy** <note>or "Guru of Ramal"</note>, then the prediction will certainly come to fruition. ॥ १८ ॥

<vocab>रमल (Ramal: Geomancy), रम्माल (Rammal: Geomancer), स्थिरप्रश्न (Sthira-prashna: Fixed question), साबित (Sabit: Fixed figure), दाखिल (Dakhil: Incoming figure), खारिज (Kharij: Outgoing figure), मुन्कलीव (Munkaliv: Mobile figure), शकल (Shakal: Geomantic figure/shape), संशयेनियामकम् (Sanshayeniyamakam: Resolver of doubts), सिद्ध (Siddha: Proven/Successful)</vocab>

It emphasizes the importance of the practitioner's mental purity and faith for an accurate divination.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the transition from the discussion of "Fixed" questions to the "Departure" questions. It concludes the section on the movement of figures and introduces a significant technical process in geomancy called "Inkilav" (Reversal or Transformation), specifically explaining how to interpret the final figures of a geomantic reading.</meta>

<page-num>५८</page-num>
<header>[ The Supreme Secret of Ramala ]</header>

After determining the nature of the **Outgoing** <term>Kharij</term> <note>original: *khārij*; figures indicating movement away</note> figures and others, and having certain knowledge, the practitioner should speak to the inquirer about the various types of **Departure** <term>Nirgama</term> <note>questions regarding leaving, traveling, or loss</note>, explaining whether the results will be auspicious or inauspicious, and whether they will come through ease or through great effort. ॥ १७ ॥

In this way, after contemplating all questions regarding departures and similar matters before the inquirer, the inquiry achieves success <term>Prashna-siddhi</term> only when the practitioner’s <term>Rammal</term> <note>a practitioner of Ramala geomancy</note> state of mind is steady, and there is faith in the science of geomancy and the teacher, along with purity of heart. ॥ १८ ॥

-> Thus ends the method for Departures and related inquiries. ॥ <-

-> Now, the purpose of Reversal. <-

I shall now speak of the results arising from questions involving **Reversal** <term>Inkilav</term> <note>original: *inkilāv* (from Arabic *inqilab*); a technical process where figures are transformed or "flipped"</note>. If the figure **Congregation** <term>Jamaat</term> <note>original: *jamāt* (from Arabic *jama‘at*); the 16th figure, often indicating a group or conclusion</note> appears in the designated place, one should judge the results from its two roots <note>the figures in the 13th and 14th houses which "produce" the later figures</note>. ॥ १९ ॥

If the figures are **Outgoing** <term>Kharij</term> and are Auspicious, they grant success in questions of departure. If they are **Incoming** <term>Dakhil</term> <note>original: *dākhil*; figures indicating entry or return</note> and Auspicious, they grant success in questions of arrival. ॥ २० ॥

Similarly, if they are **Fixed** <term>Sabit</term> <note>original: *thābit*; figures indicating stability</note> and Auspicious, they grant success in stationary tasks. In **Middling** <term>Madhyama</term> figures, there is success if the figure is strong, and only slight success if the figure is weak. ॥ २१ ॥

Through **Reversal** <term>Viparyayana</term> <note>synonym for Inkilav; turning over or inversion</note>, success is not achieved; however, one should deliberate on all factors as before, regarding whether the signs are auspicious or otherwise. One should judge the entirety of the inquiry section as previously described. ॥ २२ ॥

Questions concerning **Items held in the fist** <term>Mushti</term>, **Silent questions** <term>Muka</term> <note>where the inquirer does not speak their question aloud</note>, or questions about letters and time-periods, are settled without the use of Reversal; therefore, Reversal should not be applied to them. ॥ २३ ॥

In this manner, the judgment for all houses has been stated in a general sense. Now, I shall describe the specific characteristics pertaining to each individual house. ॥ २४ ॥

**Commentary—** Now the purpose of **Reversal** <term>Inkilav</term> is explained: At the time of a question arising from Reversal, if the figure **Congregation** <term>Jamaat</term> appears in the 15th house, one should speak the result based on its "roots," which are the 13th and 14th houses. ॥ १९ ॥ It is like this: if the figures in the 13th and 14th houses are **Outgoing** <term>Kharij</term> and are Auspicious, they grant success to a question about departure. If they are **Incoming** <term>Dakhil</term> and Auspicious, they grant success to a question about arrival.

<summary>This page concludes the rules for interpreting "Departure" questions and introduces the "Inkilav" (Reversal) method, which uses the 13th and 14th houses as "roots" to provide a final answer when the figure "Jamaat" appears.</summary>
<keywords>Nirgama (Departure), Inkilav (Reversal), Rammal (Geomancer), Jamaat (Congregation), Kharij (Outgoing), Dakhil (Incoming), Sabit (Fixed), geomantic houses</keywords>

Success is achieved when they are strong or average; if they are weak, the undertaking will fail. ॥ 21 ॥ If the figures are reversed, success will not be achieved, even if they are otherwise auspicious. ### Now, the Construction of the Table for that Purpose.

> One should draw a grid with sixteen vertical and sixteen horizontal lines; this creates the external houses. One should write the sequence of houses from one to sixteen, and then from the other side, write the numbers one to sixteen again. ॥ 29 ॥ The sum of the house number and the value situated within it should be calculated below; this process is repeated for all houses. The lifespan is declared based on the number of the cell that matches the total produced from the multiplication within the table. ॥ 30 ॥ That number represents the total years and months; if the figure is **Mobile** <term>Munkalib</term> <note>original: *munkalib*; a figure indicating change or movement</note>, those are the years, and if it is **Outgoing** <term>Kharij</term> <note>original: *khārij*; a figure indicating exit or loss</note>, it reveals the count of days. ॥ 31 ॥ When the table is expanded by the generated half, the wise should always determine the digits from the horizontal and vertical cells. ॥ 32 ॥

-> Thus ends the specialty of the first house. <-
