In the chart, under the figure of **Luhyan** <term>Luhyan</term> <note>original: *luhyāna*; a geomantic figure associated with the planet Jupiter, often signifying wisdom or gain</note>, across from the number 6, the number 17 is found. The same method should be applied to all houses. The lifespan stated here is equal to the number found in the cell of the segment derived from the previously mentioned expansion <term>Prastara</term> <note>original: *prastāra*; the systematic layout or mathematical expansion of geomantic figures</note>. ॥ 30 ॥ If that figure is **Entering** <term>Dakhil</term> <note>original: *dākhila*; a figure indicating stability or something coming in</note>, then that many years are confirmed; if it is **Mobile** <term>Munkalib</term> <note>original: *munkalība*; a figure indicating change or transition</note>, it indicates weeks; and if it is **Outgoing** <term>Kharij</term> <note>original: *khārija*; a figure indicating exit or movement away</note>, it should be understood as days. ॥ 31 ॥

If the figure generated by the combination of the 1st and 4th houses is not present in the expansion, the learned should take the number from the upper and straight cells using the **Vijadah** <term>Vijadah</term> <note>original: *vijadaha*; likely derived from the Arabic *wajada* 'to find'; a specific lookup sequence</note> order. Here, the author’s technique is as follows: when that specific figure is not in the expansion, one must ask who its ruler is and what its secondary figure is, and then work with that. For example, the ruler of **Luhyan** is **Jupiter** <note>original: *Bṛhaspati*</note>, and the ruler of **Inward Victory** <term>Nusrat-Dakhil</term> <note>original: *nusrata-dākhila*</note> is also Jupiter; therefore, one should use this figure of **Inward Victory**. There is further reasoning involved in this method as well. ॥ 32 ॥

<meta>This page continues from the previous discussion on life-expectancy and timing. <page-num>62</page-num>
<header>[ The Nine Gems of Ramal ]</header>

### Now, the specifics regarding the Second House are explained.

Between two men, which one will be the most wealthy? ॥ In such
an inquiry, the primary subject is the main focus, while the other is 
determined by their relationship <note>In this system, the 'primary' is usually the person asking the question (1st house), and the 'other' is often the person they are asking about (associated with the 7th house).</note>. ॥ 33 ॥ The house
of the primary person is the first house, and the other's house follows.
Their respective second houses <note>The 2nd house from the 1st, and the 2nd house from the 7th (which is the 8th house).</note> indicate their wealth; if both contain **Entering** <term>Dakhil</term> <note>original: *dākhila*; a geomantic figure indicating stability or things coming in</note> and auspicious figures, it is good. ॥ 34 ॥
Or if the figure is **Stable** <term>Sabit</term> <note>original: *sābita*; a figure indicating permanence and lack of movement</note>,
their wealth will be abundant; if the figures in both houses are the same, they are equal. If the figure is auspicious and **Outgoing** <term>Kharij</term> <note>original: *khārija*; a figure indicating exit or spending</note>, there is a little wealth; if it is **Outgoing** and inauspicious, there is none. ॥ 35 ॥
Based on whether the figures are auspicious, inauspicious, or **Reversing** <term>Munkalib</term> <note>original: *munkalība*; a figure indicating change, movement, or reversal</note>, one should understand the sequence: wealth is much, little, or non-existent. In questions regarding the specific quantity, the **Vijadah** <term>Vijadah</term> <note>original: *bijadaha*; a calculation method used to find numerical values</note> of the house should be determined as before. ॥ 36 ॥
The wise practitioner should determine the number through the **Diagonal Path** <term>Karnamarga</term> <note>original: *karṇamārgeṇa*; a technical term for calculating values by moving diagonally across the geomantic grid</note>. This value could range from a cowrie shell <term>Varatika</term> <note>A small shell used as the lowest denomination of currency in ancient India</note> to a **Gold Coin** <term>Svarnamudra</term>, according to the practitioner's own judgment. ॥ 37 ॥
The number can range from one to ten million, according to the capacity of the person <note>original: *pātrānusārataḥ*; literally "according to the vessel," meaning one must judge based on the person's social and financial status</note>. Through various combinations and additions, if there is a lack of a clear figure, that quantity shall be determined. ॥ 38 ॥

---

**Commentary:** — Now, in the second house, specific details are given regarding the question: "Of two men, who is more wealthy?" In such a question, one person is the primary subject and the other is determined by their relationship. ॥ 33 ॥ Then, in this context, one must see that the first house belongs to the primary querent and the house of the related person is the second <note>referring to the house assigned to the 'other' in the chart</note>. Their respective second houses are to be known as their houses of wealth. If both those wealth-houses contain **Entering** <term>Dakhil</term> figures and are auspicious, ॥ 34 ॥ or if they are **Stable** <term>Sabit</term>, then both have much wealth. If they are equal, their wealth is the same. If the figure is auspicious but **Outgoing** <term>Kharij</term>, then there is a little wealth; and if the figure is inauspicious and **Outgoing** <term>Kharij</term>, then there is no wealth. ॥ 35 ॥ In auspicious figures there is much, in inauspicious there is little, and in **Reversing** <term>Munkalib</term> there is no wealth—this is to be understood in order. Whichever person's wealth-figure is of a certain kind, one should describe their wealth accordingly. And if a specific quantity is asked—

<header>The Fifth Gem 5. ]</header>

Then, according to the **Vimdah** <term>Vimadaha</term> <note>original: *vimadaha*; a specific sequence or cycle used in Ramal geomancy to assign values to the sixteen figures</note> sequence, take the number from the figure's own section as previously described. ॥ 36 ॥ For example, if the figure representing wealth <note>the figure appearing in the 2nd house of the chart</note> occupies the same numbered house in the layout <term>Prastara</term> as it does in the *Vimdah* sequence, then the number should be determined using the diagonal path in the number chart. At that point, the quantity should be stated; a wise practitioner should use their own intellectual power to judge whether the amount refers to a **cowrie** <note>a shell used as the lowest denomination of currency</note> or a **gold coin** <term>Mohar</term>. ॥ 37 ॥ 

One may calculate any number from one to ten million <term>Crore</term>.

If two or three numbers are found, they should be added together and then announced. If there is an absence of numbers, one should say there is no wealth. ॥ 38 ॥

->**Now, the specifics regarding the Third House are explained.**<-

> I shall now specifically state the results born of dreams <term>Svapnajatam</term> pertaining to the third house. ॥ Among omens <term>Shakuna</term> and inquiries, the result arises from their mutual combination. ॥ 39 ॥ Auspiciousness comes from the auspicious, while the "sinful" <note>malefic or inauspicious figures</note> bring inauspicious results, and a mix of both brings a neutral result. The wise, through the science of dreams, understand the distinctions according to the divisions of that section. ॥ 40 ॥

**Commentary:** Now, there is a specific detail regarding the **Third House** <term>Tartiya</term>; in this, we specifically describe the results of dreams by combining the numerical data of the third houses from the omen sequence and the layout. ॥ 39 ॥ If the resulting figure is auspicious, the dream is good; if it is malefic <note>original: *pāpa*, meaning "sinful" or "dark"</note>, the dream is bad. If it is a mix of auspicious and inauspicious, the result is neutral. Based on the strength of that section, the wise man should explain the dream, making a specific judgment after considering the essential qualities <term>Sattva</term> <note>original: *sattvadeko*; referring to the three Gunas or inherent qualities of the figures: purity, passion, or ignorance</note> of that section. ॥ 40 ॥

### Now, the specifics of the Fourth House are explained.

If a woman comes and asks a question regarding her husband, or about two different men: ॥ To answer the beautiful woman, one must examine the house of the self <note>the 1st House, representing the querent</note>, the house of the husband, and the house of happiness. ॥ 41 ॥ From the Fourth House and the First House, or from the Tenth House and the First House combined ॥ a judgment should be made. If auspicious figures appear in both places, it indicates strength; the wise practitioner should then speak of the happiness related to the husbands through the Fourth and Tenth houses. ॥ 42 -

**Commentary—** In the specifics of the Fourth House, it is said that if a woman comes and asks: "Will my well-being come from my first husband or from my second husband?"—then the practitioner should identify the first house of the layout <term>Prastara</term> as the house of the woman asking the question. The first husband is known through the **Fourth House**, and the second husband is known through the **Tenth House**. ॥ 41 ॥ Then, one should combine the figure from the First House with the Fourth House; based on the resulting figure's <term>Shakal</term> <note>the geomantic sign formed by the combination</note> and its auspicious or inauspicious strength, one should predict the well-being associated with the first husband. Similarly, by combining the figure of the Tenth House with the First House, the resulting figure will indicate the well-being of the second husband. If the figures are equal in strength, then one should predict equal happiness or distress from both husbands. ॥ 42 ॥

Now, I shall explain another specific question regarding the Fourth House. One may contemplate whether there is **wealth** <note>hidden treasure</note> present in the ground at this location or not. ॥ 43 ॥
By combining the figures of the Fourth House and the Sixth House <term>Ripu-Khanda</term> <note>literally "the enemy's section," referring to the 6th house of the chart</note>, if the resulting figure is **Dakhil** <term>Dakhil</term> <note>original: *dākhila*; an "entering" figure signifying arrival, gain, or presence</note>, then the wealth is present. If the figure is **Unkali** <term>Unkali</term> <note>likely a variation of *Kharij*, an "exiting" or "unstable" figure</note>, then there is either no wealth or only a very small amount. ॥ 44 ॥
Otherwise, one should first conclude there is an absence of wealth. If wealth is indicated, then one must determine the location within a square plot divided by two lines. ॥ 45 ॥
By calculating the quadrants between the South and the West, or between the East and the North, and dividing the area into four parts, the direction can be found where the treasure remains. ॥ 46 ॥

### Now, Special Details Regarding the Fifth House.

Now I shall specifically explain the inquiries regarding the Fifth House. For instance: "Will I have offspring <note>original: संतान; progeny</note> in the future, or will I not?" ॥ 51 ॥ For this question, take the figures from the first, fifth, sixth, and seventh houses. Multiply these two pairs together to create a single resultant figure. ॥ 52 ॥ That figure is known as the "Offspring" figure. If it is auspicious, then everything will be favorable and children will be born. However, if it is inauspicious, it signifies the death of the offspring. ॥ 53 ॥ If the figure is a "fixed" or neutral type and is auspicious, children will be born after a long period. If it is an inauspicious "reversing" figure <note>original: मुन्कलीब; from the Arabic *Munkalib*, signifying change or instability</note> or neutral, there will be a miscarriage <term>Garbhapata</term> <note>the falling of the womb/pregnancy</note> without a doubt. ॥ 54 ॥ Otherwise, there will be no offspring at all. Thus, by calculating from the ascendant <term>Lagna</term> <note>the first house or the starting point of the query</note> and the sixth figure, one should pronounce the answer after careful reflection; the prediction will never be false. ॥ 55 ॥

**Commentary**—Now I will discuss the specific questions for the Fifth House. When someone asks, "Will I have children or not?" ॥ 51 ॥ In this inquiry, the first and the fifth...

# Section Five: The Fifth House

<page-num>67</page-num>

One should combine the figures of the sixth and seventh sections by multiplying them <note>in Ramal geomancy, "multiplying" refers to combining the points of two figures to create a new one</note> to form a single resulting figure. ॥ 52 ॥ If that figure, which is designated as the "Offspring" figure, is auspicious and of the **Entering** <term>Dakhila; a figure where the points flow inward, suggesting gain or arrival</term> type, then the birth of a child is indicated. If it is inauspicious, the child may be born but will not survive. ॥ 53 ॥ If the figure is **Stable** <term>Saabit; a fixed and steady figure</term> and **Moving/Changing** <term>Munkaliv; a figure that suggests transformation</term> yet auspicious, the child will be born after a long period. However, if it is an inauspicious **Moving** figure, a miscarriage will undoubtedly occur. ॥ 54 ॥ If any other type of figure appears, there will be no children. One should carefully analyze the query using the first, fifth, sixth, and seventh houses; a judgment made this way never proves false. ॥ 55 ॥

> When there is doubt regarding pregnancy or the number of children, one should examine the figure produced from the first <note>original: तनु; Tanu, the house of the self/body</note> and seventh <note>original: शैल; Shaila, literally 'mountain,' a numerical code for the number seven</note> houses. If that figure is **Entering** or **Stable**, then a pregnancy is certain. ॥ 56 ॥ If it is **Exiting** <term>Khaarij; a figure suggesting loss or departure</term> or **Moving**, then there is no pregnancy. Alternatively, one may judge this outcome by combining the figures of the sixth and first houses. ॥ 57 ॥

**Commentary:** In cases of doubt regarding whether a woman is pregnant or concerning the number of children, one should first combine the figures from the first and seventh houses to create a new figure. If it is **Entering** or **Stable**, a pregnancy is definitely present. ॥ 56 ॥ If it is **Exiting** or **Moving**, there is no pregnancy. Alternatively, this result can be determined by the combination of the sixth and first houses. ॥ 57 ॥

-> Now, describing the number of offspring. <-

> In that same query, if the figure corresponds to the Sun, the number of children is four <note>original: वेद; Veda; traditionally there are four Vedas, so the word is used as a cipher for the number 4</note>. If it is Venus, the number is six; if Mercury, two; if the Moon, five; and if Saturn, one. ॥ 58 ॥ If it is Jupiter, the number is three; and if Mars, the number is four <note>original: वेदा; Veda</note>. If the figures of Rahu or Ketu <note>the lunar nodes</note> appear, there will be a miscarriage or loss. A wise practitioner, having considered these factors, should announce the number of children. ॥ 59 ॥

**Commentary:** Now the number of children is explained. In a query regarding offspring, look at the figure previously derived from houses 1 and 7. If that figure belongs to the Sun, there will be 4 children; if Venus, 6; if Mercury, 2; if the Moon, 5; and if Saturn...

### Now, on the Question of What Will Happen Regarding the Possibility of Pregnancy

In the matter of pregnancy and offspring, one should take the figure resulting from the combination of the seventh and first houses. Multiply this figure by the figure situated in the **Fifth House** <note>original: पंचमस्थ; Panchamastha</note>. From the resulting figure, the outcome should be stated in sequence. ॥ 60 ॥ If the resulting figure is in a male segment, a son will be born. If it is in a female segment, a daughter will be born. Even if the figure is of a **Neuter** <term>Kliba; a "third-gender" or neuter figure in geomancy</term> nature, a girl's birth is usually predicted. Now, the specific conditions for the birth of a neuter child will be explained. ॥ 61 ॥ If the figures from the first and seventh houses, the figure generated from them, and the figure in the fifth house are all of a neuter nature, and if there is a **Repetition** <term>Punarukta; when the same geomantic figure appears in multiple houses of the chart</term> in an Earth-element house, then the child born will certainly be a eunuch/neuter. ॥ 62 ॥

**Commentary** — Regarding the question of whether a child in the womb will be a girl or a boy: when a pregnancy has been confirmed according to the previously mentioned methods, one must take the figure <note>original: शकळ; Shakal, the four-line geomantic sign</note> generated from the 1st and 7th houses and multiply it by the figure in the 5th house. The nature of the resulting figure determines the outcome. ॥ 60 ॥ If that segment is masculine, say a son will be born; if feminine, say a daughter. If it is a neuter figure, it generally indicates a girl, but a neuter birth is specifically predicted as follows: ॥ 61 ॥ If the figures of the 1st and 7th houses, their combined result, and the figure in the 5th house are all three neuter, and there is a "Repetition" in a house associated with the Earth element, then a neuter child will surely be born. "Repetition" refers to when the same figure active in the calculation appears again in another house of the layout. ॥ 62 ॥

Now I shall state another method for determining the number of children, as taught by the **Yavanas** <note>original: यवनादित; refers to the "Yavanas" or Greeks/Arabs, acknowledging the Perso-Arabic roots of Ramal astrology</note> and others. Count how many times the figure occupying the 5th house repeats in the chart. ॥ 63 ॥ That count indicates the number of offspring. Within that, figures associated with the Fire and Air elements indicate the birth of sons...

In the water element, a daughter is born or a miscarriage occurs; in the earth element, if the figure is strong, it is a good sign for a daughter's birth. A wise person should speak only after understanding this. ॥ 64 ॥ 

**Another Method:** If that figure is not repeated, then the number of offspring is determined by its position in the **Cycle of the Year** <term>Abdahālaya; a specific geomantic arrangement used for yearly predictions</term>. ॥ 65 ॥ 

As far as the fifth figure in that sequence, the number of offspring will be that many. The distinction between male and female should be understood as previously stated; this is what the masters of **Geomancy** <term>Ramal; a system of divination using figures formed by dots</term> have said. ॥ 66 ॥

**Commentary—** Now, regarding the number of children, I shall state the rules and reasons according to the Greeks/Persians <note>original: यवनोक्त; Yavanokta, referring to "Ionian" or foreign/Western systems, often used for Perso-Arabic geomancy</note>. If the **Figure** <note>original: शकल; Shakal</note> situated in the fifth house is repeated in another house—meaning the same shape appears elsewhere in the chart—then the number of children corresponds to the number of that house. For example, if it repeats in the 1st house, there is 1 child; if it repeats in the 10th house, there are 10 children. 

There is a specific detail here: if that figure belongs to the fire or air element, it will be a son. If it belongs to the water element, it will be a daughter. If it belongs to the earth element, a miscarriage may occur, but if the earth element is strong and auspicious, a daughter will be born. A scholar must consider the earth element in this way. 

There is also another method: if that **Figure** <term>Bālak; literally "child," used here to refer to the geomantic figure</term> is not repeated in the chart, then one should look at the **Cycle of the Year** <term>Abdahālaya</term>. The number of children will match the position of the fifth figure of that sequence relative to the original house. The distinction between male and female remains as previously described. This is the teaching of the experts in **Geomancy** <term>Ramal</term>. ॥ 63 ॥ 64 ॥ 65 ॥ 66 ॥

-> Now, the timing [of the birth] is stated. <-

If there is "Day-Strength," the birth is by day; if there is "Night-Strength," it is by night. If the strength lies in the twilights, the birth occurs during the dawn or dusk. When the figure in the fifth house is strong, the birth is certain. ॥ 67 ॥ Furthermore, one must look to the half of the ninth house or the **Trine** <note>original: त्रिकोण; Trikona, a 120-degree aspect or a specific group of houses</note> of the first house. Regarding the knowledge of the lifespan—when there is a question about a short or long life, one looks to the fifth and seventh houses for the offspring. ॥ 68 ॥

<page-num>71</page-num>
<header>Pancharatna 5. ] ( 71 )</header>

Wealth will come with ease; if the signs are gentle, life will also be lived in happiness, but if they are inauspicious, life will be full of hardship—this is what the ancient masters of the sages have said. ॥ 71 ॥

> If the first house represents the patient, the sixth is said to be the disease, and the fourth is the medicine. The tenth house represents the physician, the eleventh signifies the state of being free from disease, and the twelfth, known as the "Itvana" house, represents long-term illness. ॥ 72 ॥ In some traditions, the second and tenth houses are also designated for medicine. If gentle **figures** are found in these houses starting from the ascendant, those specific matters will be auspicious. If they are otherwise, the results will be contrary, especially when considering the specific strength of the figures. ॥ 73 ॥

**Commentary**—Now, specific considerations regarding the sixth house are described: In the **divination chart** <term>Prastara; the specific layout or "spread" of the sixteen geomantic figures</term>, the first house is designated for the patient. Similarly, the sixth house is for the disease, the fourth for medicine, the tenth for the physician, the eleventh for recovery, and the twelfth for chronic or long-term illness. ॥ 72 ॥ According to some opinions, the second and tenth houses are also used to judge the medicine. If auspicious **figures** <term>Shakal; the specific patterns or "signatures" used in geomancy</term> appear in these houses (the ascendant, etc.), then the matters related to those positions will be favorable. If they are the opposite, the results will also be unfavorable. Even in this, there is a distinction based on the consideration of the figures' strength or weakness. ॥ 73 ॥

> Having considered the general rules in this way, I shall now speak further of the specifics regarding the fourth house. All figures known as **Sabit** <term>Sabit; a "stable" or "fixed" geomantic figure, likely referring to Al-Qabd al-Dakhil</term> are suitable here, as are **Samunkali** <note>original: समुन्कली; likely referring to the figure *Al-Anquid* or "The Cluster"</note> and **Bhakharij** <note>original: भखारिज; likely referring to *Al-Kharij* or "Outgoing"</note>. ॥ 74 ॥ In the sixth house, [an auspicious figure] removes disease quickly and easily. If a malefic figure is present, the disease brings pain and delay. The figure **Dakhil** <note>original: वाखिल; referring to an "entering" figure</note> leads to the increase of the disease, and the figure known as **Half-Sabit** <note>original: साबितार्द्धम्; a figure with partial stability</note> acts similarly. ॥ 75 ॥ Based on the elements—Fire and the rest—placed in the sixth house, diseases arise respectively from **bile, wind, and phlegm** <term>Pitta, Vata, and Kapha; the three "doshas" or biological humors in Indian medicine</term>, as well as ailments born of indigestion. ॥ 76 ॥ If the earth element is present in the **house of enemies** <term>Riphu; the sixth house, which also governs illness</term>, or if the fire element is there, one should identify disorders of the blood and bones, as well as wounds deep within the skin and bone. ॥ 77 ॥

<vocab>Prastara, Shakal, Sabit, Samunkali, Bhakharij, Pitta-Vata-Kapha, Ripu, Balabala</vocab>

**Commentary**—This analysis has been stated in general terms; one should apply their own intelligence to the specific interpretation. Now, more specific details are given: in the fourth house <note>The house representing medicine and the end of the matter</note>, "Entering" <term>Dakhl; a geomantic figure indicating movement toward the subject</term> and "Exiting" <term>Kharij; a figure indicating movement away</term> types of **figures** are considered auspicious. In the sixth house, an auspicious "Reversing" <term>Munkalib; a mobile or changing figure</term> or an auspicious "Exiting" **figure** ॥ 74 ॥ will result in the disease being removed quickly and with ease. If "Sinful" <note>original: "pap"; refers to malefic or inauspicious signs</note> **figures** that are "Reversing" or "Exiting" are present, then recovery will come with difficulty and delay. If "Entering" **figures** appear in the sixth house, they are the cause of the disease increasing; similarly, "Fixed" <term>Sabit; a stable or unmoving figure</term> **figures** here should also be understood as increasing the illness ॥ 75 ॥ In the sixth house, the elements such as the Fire element, etc., should be used to diagnose the origin of the disease in the order of Bile, Wind, Phlegm, and Indigestion <note>This correlates the four geomantic elements (Fire, Air, Water, Earth) with the Ayurvedic humors: Pitta, Vata, and Kapha</note> ॥ 76 ॥ Regarding the Earth element, there is this special detail: disorders of the blood, bone disorders, and skin or bone injuries should be understood through the sixth house. Similarly, the consideration of disease via the Fire and other elements can also be done from the tenth house; this is an alternative scholarly opinion ॥ 77 ॥

### On the Knowledge of the Life or Death of the Patient.

> For the question of whether a patient will live or die, one must look to the figures of the first and sixth houses. If the figure of Saturn <note>original: "Sourasya"</note> or Mercury <note>original: "Budhasya"</note> appears, it causes the death of the patient ॥ 78 ॥ Other divisions may also indicate death to the wise; the learned have said this occurs if such figures are found in the first and third houses as well ॥ 79 ॥

**Commentary**—Now, regarding the question of whether a patient will live or die: if the **figure** in the first and sixth houses is of a harmful quality, the patient will surely die. Similarly, the **figure** of Mercury is also a bringer of death ॥ 78 ॥ If **figures** of other planets are present, the clever practitioner of Ramala <term>Rammal; a person who practices this form of geomancy</term> should say that the patient will not die. Scholars have also stated results based on the combination of the first and third **figures** ॥ 79 ॥

->On knowing whether the thief is a family member or a stranger.<-

Add the total sum of points to the number of lines multiplied by two, then divide the total by three. From the remainder: if one, it is an insider; if two, a neighbor; if three, a person from a distant land. || 82 ||
Another method—By combining the first, fourth, seventh, and sixteenth houses, create two figures and then combine those two into one. If that resulting figure is "Fixed" or "Entering," the thief is within the village. || 83 || If it is "Exiting," he has gone outside; if "Reversing," he is intending to leave. || 84 ||

**Commentary**—Is the thief one of our own or another (a stranger)? In such an inquiry, one must sum all the points in the chart and add to that the total count of lines multiplied by two. <note>The author explains the logic: "Because one line is produced from two points, two units are counted for each line here."</note> Whatever the total sum is, divide it by three and look at the remainder. If one remains, the thief is a member of the household. If two remain, he is a neighbor (living nearby) or a relative. If three remain, know the thief to be a foreigner <note>In this context, someone from outside the immediate community or village</note>. || 82 || Now, another consideration regarding the location of the thief: combine the first, fourth, seventh, and sixteenth figures <note>In geomancy, figures are combined by adding their points to create a new "daughter" figure</note> to make two figures, then combine those two into one final figure. If that resulting figure is **Stable** <term>Sabit; a "fixed" figure indicating no movement</term> or **Entering** <term>Dakhil; a figure indicating movement toward the center</term>, then the thief is still within the village and has not left the city. If it is **Exiting** <term>Kharij; a figure indicating outward movement</term>, he has gone outside the village. If it is **Reversing** <term>Munkalib; a mobile or changing figure</term>, he is currently preparing to flee. <meta>text continues to next page</meta>

### On Knowing the Location of the Thief and Related Matters.

> "Who is the thief? Where is he? Near whom is the taker?" In such an inquiry, from the **point-lines** <term>Bindu-rekha; the dots and dashes that form a geomantic figure</term> of the eighth, twelfth, fourteenth, and fifteenth figures <note>Specifically, the text suggests taking the second level/line of these four figures</note>, one should form a single new figure. || 85 || One should then observe where this figure, formed from the previous point-lines, resides within the **chart** <term>Prastara; the 16-house geomantic layout</term>. If it is in the first house <note>The House of Self</note>, the thief is one's own person and is staying in his own house; so one should say. || 86 || If in the second house, it is a relative or a neighbor. In the third, it is a brother, a friend, or a kinsman in their residence. In the fourth, it is the father, someone near him, or an uncle or grandfather. || 87 || In the fifth, it is among messengers, prostitutes, dancers, or those with artistic talents. In the sixth, it is among male or female servants or enemies. In the seventh, it is among partners or women. || 88 || In the eighth, he is traveling on a road, or is among those who perform illusions or magic. If in the ninth, he is among ascetics, guests, or travelers on a path, staying by their side but not in a house. || 89 || In the tenth, he is among kings, noble men, or those who hold authority. In the eleventh or twelfth, he would be among friends or trusted associates. || 90 ||

In such an inquiry, one should take the **point-lines** from the 2nd level of the 8th, 12th, 14th, and 15th figures <term>Shakala; the 16 possible four-line patterns in geomancy</term> in sequence to create a new figure. || 85 || One must then see where this figure, formed from the previous point-lines, appears in the chart. If it stays in the first house, the thief is one’s own person and is staying in his own house. || 86 || If it is in the second house, the thief is one’s own relative or a neighbor, or the thief is residing in their house. Similarly, from the third house, it is a brother, friend, kinsman, or sister. From the fourth house, it is the father or someone staying near him, or a paternal uncle, great-uncle, grandfather, etc. || 87 || From the fifth, it is a messenger (footman), a prostitute, dancers, or among people with skills in games and sports. ### Knowledge of the Direction of the Thief's Path and the Door of the House

If [looking at] the tenth house, the thief’s movement is in that direction. One should describe the door of the thief's house based on the figure in the ninth house. || 95 ||

And whichever direction is assigned to the ninth house, it should be said that the door of the thief’s house is in that direction. || 95 ||

### Now, Measuring the Distance of the Path if the Thief is Nearby or in the Same Village

When the question is asked, "How far has the thief gone?", one should indicate the distance of the path according to the value of the figure **Vijadaha** <term>Sanskritized from Arabic 'Al-Ijtima' (The Conjunction); a figure representing union or gathering</term> in the preceding house. || 96 || One should estimate the distance—ranging from a single field to a *yojana* <term>a traditional measure of distance, approximately 8 to 9 miles</term>—using one's own intelligence. If the owner and the thief are in the same place, then in that interval... || 97 || Regarding the question of "How many houses away?", if [the figure falls in] the fifth, sixth, seventh, or ninth house... multiply the lines by two, subtract the year’s count, and divide by five and a half. || 98 || The number of houses between the owner and the thief corresponds to the remainder. One should deliberate upon everything: the specific house, the village, and the surrounding lands. || 99 ||

**Commentary**—If the thief is nearby or in one's own village, the method for measuring the path is explained. When a question is asked regarding how far the thief has traveled, one must look at the previously mentioned ninth house and the figure **Vijadaha**. The extent of the path should be declared based on the number of houses separating that ninth house from the house where **Vijadaha** resides. || 96 || One must use their own judgment to estimate the scale, whether it be a small distance like a field or a great distance like a *yojana*. If the owner of the property and the thief are in the same village, then for their houses...

### On Knowing the Form of the Thief

> One should declare the caste, color, physical appearance, nature, and all other details of the thief by observing the figure of the seventh house through the **Abdaha** <term>Abdaha: An Arabic-derived sequence used to re-order the sixteen geomantic houses</term> sequence. || 4 ||

**Commentary**—To know the appearance and other traits of the thief, look at the figure <term>Shakala: One of the 16 binary figures in Ramal</term> found in the seventh house of the grid <term>Prastara</term>. Determine which house number that same figure occupies in the **Abdaha** sequence. Then, look at the figure in that corresponding house number of the original grid. Based on that figure’s previously mentioned attributes—such as caste, complexion, shape, gender, and so on—one should describe the thief. || 4 ||

### On Questions of Gain and Loss

> Combine the figures of wealth <note>2nd house</note> and gain <note>11th house</note>, and the figures of the self <note>1st house</note> and the witness <note>14th house</note>. By reducing these pairs to a single figure, one should declare everything regarding the stolen property. || 5 || Recovery is quick; otherwise, it is not. In the case of the figures **Atithi** <note>original: 'Atithi' (Guest), likely referring to the figure *Al-Jama'a*</note> or **Ulka** <note>original: 'Ulka' (Meteor), likely referring to the figure *Al-Lahiya*</note>, the result is the opposite. If the figure is **Dakhil** <note>original: 'daakhil' (Entering)</note> or **Sabit** <note>original: 'saabit' (Stable/Fixed)</note>, and it appears in a wealth-related house, it brings the recovery of everything. || 6 || If the figure is **Munqalib** <note>original: 'munkubi' (Inverted/Changing)</note> and malefic, only half the wealth is gained; if it is **Kharij** <note>original: 'kharije' (Outgoing/Exit)</note>, nothing is obtained. If a **Kharij** figure is in the eighth house, the wealth moves from the thief to another place. || 7 || Thus, the various methods for questioning about a thief have been declared through the seventh house. One should also identify the figures for gold-work and death <note>original: 'swarnakarya' and 'mara'</note> by this same method. || 8 || After considering their possibility through the two fragments of Mars <note>referring to specific geomantic figures associated with the planet Mars</note>, an expert who understands time and place should speak the remainder as before. || 9 ||

If the figure is an **Entering** <term>Dakhil: A category of geomantic figures indicating arrival or gain</term> type or **The White** <note>original: *Bayaj*, referring to the auspicious figure *Al-Bayad*</note>, the lost item will be recovered quickly; otherwise, it will not. If it is an **Exiting** <term>Kharij: A category of figures indicating loss or departure</term> type, nothing will be recovered at all. If an **Exiting** figure appears in the eighth house, the stolen goods are no longer with the thief; they have already been moved elsewhere. || 7 || 

These distinctions regarding the investigation of thieves are described via the seventh house. One should also observe the location of alchemists <note>original: *Rasayani*, those who attempt to create gold</note> and deities in the same manner—determining in which country, village, or house they reside. || 8 || 

This is determined by whether the figures of **Mars** <note>original: *Mangal*</note> are present or not. After considering these possibilities, the practitioner, knowledgeable in the nuances of time and place, should speak based on their own refined intellect. All other remaining matters regarding the thief should be judged as previously described. || 9 ||

->### Now, the Particulars of the Eighth House<-

> The wise practitioner should contemplate whether there will be 
> freedom from debt, an increase in debt, 
> or if the situation will remain the same. 
> If the figure **Inner Gain** <term>Kanjul Dakhil: From the Arabic *Kabd al-Dakhil*</term> is in the first house, 
> and **The Inner Threshold** <term>Atavedakhil: From the Arabic *Ataba al-Dakhil*</term> is in the second and eighth houses... || 10 || 
> ...then freedom from that debt will come quickly. 
> If there is an auspicious **Exiting** figure in the second and eighth houses, 
> it will take a very long time. 
> If the figures **The Red** <term>Humra: From the Arabic *Al-Humra*</term> or **The Reverted** <term>Ankish: From the Arabic *Ankis*</term> 
> are in the first, second, or third houses, then the debt will increase; 
> otherwise, the result is different. || 11 ||

**Commentary**—Now, the specific details regarding the eighth house are explained. In response to questions such as "Will my debt be cleared?", "Will it increase?", or "Will it stay the same?", the scholar should consider the following: If the figure **Inner Gain** <term>Kanjul Dakhil</term> is in the first house and the figure **The Inner Threshold** <term>Atavedakhil</term> is in both the second and eighth houses... || 10 || 

...then the debt will be repaid quickly. If an auspicious **Exiting** <term>Kharij</term> figure appears in the second or eighth houses, the debt will be cleared only after many days.

### Now, Specific Details Regarding the Ninth House

Regarding the knowledge of life or death for one who has gone abroad: if the elements of the figures are Air or Fire, they are alive; if they are Earth or Water, they are dead. One should determine the outcome from the remainder of the calculation; any remaining difficulty is equal to that calculation. || 12 || Then, by totaling the dots <note>original: *bindu*</note> of the houses, if the sum is greater than thirty-two <note>original: *rada*, a poetic term for 32 based on the number of teeth</note>, he lives. If less than thirty-two, he is dead. If it is exactly thirty-two, he is in extreme distress. || 13 || If the figures in the first and ninth houses are auspicious, he will live a long life. If the figures are the opposite, the result for the man abroad should be declared as the opposite. || 14 ||

**Commentary**—Regarding the specific question of whether a person who has gone to a foreign land is alive or dead: if there are points of the Air and Fire elements in the first and ninth houses, know them to be alive. If there are points of the Earth and Water elements, know them to be dead. Furthermore, add up all the dots from the first to the fifteenth house; if the total is more than 32, he is alive. If it is less than 32, he has died. If it is exactly 32, know that he is in extreme suffering. || This should also be kept in mind: if the figures <note>original: *shakal*</note> in the first and ninth houses are auspicious, that man will live for many days. In the opposite case, the result for the man abroad must be said to be the opposite as well. || 12-14 ||

### Regarding Victory or Defeat for Litigants in the Tenth House

Now, regarding a querent involved in a dispute: [the first house] represents the querent, and the seventh house represents the opponent. The second house represents the king <note>or judge</note>, and the tenth house also serves as the house of the king. || 15 || Through the combination of the king’s house and one's own house, if an auspicious figure appears, it indicates victory. If the figures are equal for both parties, the stronger figure wins. || 16 || Defeat...

<header>[ The Fifth Gem: Verse 5 ]</header>

> If inauspicious figures <note>original: *pāpakhaṇḍe*, literally "sinful parts" or "malefic geomantic figures"</note> are found in the houses of wealth for two disputants, they indicate loss or gain respectively, based on whether the figures are "Outgoing" or "Incoming." || 17 || 
> 
> Whichever combination of houses results in an auspicious figure, victory belongs to that side. If an inauspicious figure results, defeat will follow. The wise practitioner should declare this after careful reflection. || 18 ||

**Commentary**—We now consider the victory or defeat of those engaged in conflict via the tenth house <term>Dasham Bhava: The house of the King, authority, or the judge</term>. To judge a dispute, one must know that the first house represents the person asking the question <note>the querent</note>, the seventh house represents the opponent <note>the adversary</note>, and the tenth house represents the King or judge. || 15 || 

If you combine the geomantic figure of the King's house (10th) with the figure of another house and an auspicious figure <note>original: *shubh shakal*</note> is produced, that side wins. For example, if the combination of the 1st and 10th houses produces an auspicious figure, the querent wins. If the combination of the 10th and 7th houses produces an auspicious figure, the opponent wins. If both combinations result in figures that are either both auspicious or both inauspicious, then a compromise or treaty <note>original: *sandhi* or *rājināmā*</note> will occur. Alternatively, victory should be declared for whoever has the greater planetary strength. || 16 ||

Whoever has an inauspicious figure will face defeat. If an "Outgoing" figure <term>Kharij: A geomantic figure indicating exit, loss, or depletion</term> appears in someone's house of wealth, they will suffer financial loss. If an "Incoming" figure <term>Dakhil: A geomantic figure indicating entry, gain, or arrival</term> appears, they will receive financial gain. || 17 || 

Success comes through the assistance of whichever house (such as the seventh) produces an auspicious figure when combined. Conversely, defeat <note>original: *hār*</note> will occur through the influence of the house that produces an inauspicious figure. The wise man should answer the question only after such consideration. || 18 ||

If the opposite figures appear, the wise man should declare that the person has indeed eaten. || 20 ||

<header>### [The Fifth Gem 5.] ( 83 ) ###</header>

If there is an <term>Assembly</term> <note>original: *Ijtama*; a geomantic term for the conjunction or repetition of figures</note>, then one should describe the meal as consisting of various wonderful foods containing all flavors. Now, another method for determining the type of food is described: || 24 ||

If the figure in the tenth house belongs to the Sun, the food is prepared with oil and is pungent; if it belongs to the Moon, it is salty. For Mars, it is rice and bitter preparations made from chickpeas. || 25 ||

For Mercury, it is mung beans and a variety of flavors; for Jupiter, it is sweet products made from sugarcane and wheat. For Venus, it is barley-based foods with sour flavors; for Saturn, it is black gram <note>urad dal</note> with astringent tastes. || 26 || Regarding Rahu and Ketu <note>the lunar nodes</note>, the food is seen to be the same as Saturn, based on the figure's position in the division of the sky. || 27 ||

**Commentary**—If the <term>figure</term> <note>original: *shakala*</note> in the tenth house belongs to the Sun, know the food involves sesame, oil, and pungent items. If it is the Moon’s figure, it is salty. If it is Mars’s figure, it is rice and chickpeas (bitter or spicy items). || 25 || For Mercury, it is mung beans and the like with "all flavors." For Jupiter, it is sweets and wheat-based items. For Venus, it is barley and sour juices. For Saturn, know it to be black gram and curry <note>kadhi</note>. Similarly, for Rahu and Ketu, the description is the same as Saturn. In this way, one should describe the food based on whichever planet's figure occupies the tenth house. || 26 || 27 ||

->### Regarding Specific Gains and Success ###<-

When the question arises: "Will my command <note>or wish</note> be fulfilled or not?", one should take the first and eleventh figures of the <term>chart</term> <note>original: *prastara*</note>, and the first and fourteenth figures, and combine them to create two new figures. Then, combine those two into one. || 28 || The wise state that the outcome or direction belongs to the house where that resulting figure resides. If that figure is present in the query chart and possesses strength, then the command or wish shall attain success. || 29 || Benefic and malefic outcomes are determined as described previously, and the direction can be known from the "House of Omens." || 30 ||

**Commentary**—Now, there is a specialty regarding the eleventh house. In a query asking "Will my hope be fulfilled or not?", take the first and eleventh figures of the <term>chart</term> <note>original: *prastara*</note> and combine them, then take the first and fourteenth figures and combine them. By combining these, you create two figures. Then combine those two into one and see: if that resulting figure is present elsewhere in the chart, then the hope will be fulfilled. If it is not in the chart...

### Now, specific details regarding the Twelfth House.

**Regarding the twelfth house, release from imprisonment is now specifically stated. In the twelfth house,**
**if there is a <term>Leaving</term> <note>original: *Kharij*; a geomantic figure indicating outward movement or exit</note> figure, there will be release from bondage; not so if it is an <term>Entering</term> <note>original: *Dakhil*; indicating inward movement or staying</note> figure. || 31 || If it**
**is <term>Stable</term> <note>original: *Sthira*</note>, release should be predicted after fear; if it is <term>Reversing</term> <note>original: *Munqlib*; a figure that changes or returns</note>, there will be bondage again. If there is a <term>Leaving**
**figure</term> in the fourth or the twelfth, or if the <term>Meteor</term> <note>original: *Ulka*; a specific geomantic figure, often referring to *Ijtama* or *Al-Jama'ah*</note> figure is in the fifteenth house, there is no release. || 32 ||**
**In that case, there will be movement to another place and, without a doubt, death itself. Thus,**
**I have described the considerations for the houses starting from the <term>First House</term> <note>original: *Tanvadi*; literally "the body and the others"</note>. || 33 ||**

-> Thus ends the Fifth Gem, named the Description of Questions Regarding the Houses, in the Nine Gems of Ramal. || 5 || <-

**Commentary**—Now, the consideration for release from <term>bondage</term> <note>original: *bandhan*; referring to jail, chains, or captivity</note> in the twelfth house is specifically stated here. In the twelfth house, if there is a **Leaving** <note>original: *Kharij*</note> figure, then one will be released from bondage. If it is an **Entering** <note>original: *Dakhil*</note> figure, one will not be released. || 31 || If that division is stable, then there will be release after experiencing fear. If it is **Reversing** <note>original: *Munqlib*</note>, then after being released, one will fall into bondage again. And if there is a **Leaving** figure in the fourth and the twelfth houses, and the **Meteor** <note>original: *Ulka*</note> figure is in the fifteenth house, then one will not be released from bondage. || 32 || One will go from one place to another and will undoubtedly even die. In this manner, I have stated the considerations for the houses beginning with the First House. || 33 ||

-> Thus ends the Fifth Gem, named the Determination of Questions Regarding <-
-> the Houses, in the Mahidhari vernacular version of the Nine Gems of Ramal. || 5 || <-

### Now, the knowledge of the time limit for all questions.

**The house of the question multiplied by its own number, increased by its own digit, and then halved.**
**As long as it remains in the <unclear>dvijadaha</unclear> house, the time limit will be within its own segment. || 1 ||**

--- Page 93 ---
<meta>This page marks the beginning of the "Sixth Gem" of the *Ramal Navaratna*. Having concluded the analysis of the twelve houses in the previous chapter, the text now shifts to the technical methods for determining *when* a predicted event will occur (timing/duration).</meta>

<page-num>85</page-num>
<header>Sixth Gem 6. ]</header>

In the <term>spread</term> <note>original: *prastara*; the 16-figure geomantic chart</note>, if [the figure] is not in its own house, [look] ahead or behind in the <term>Vijjaha</term> <note>a mathematical table or sequence used specifically for determining timing in Ramal astrology</note>. It should be subtracted or added. This same method applies even if a figure is repeated twice, thrice, or more. || 2 || Whatever number is thus established shall be the time duration for the question. In this system, that number should always match the number produced by the <term>Longevity Cycle</term> <note>original: *Ayushya-chakra*; a specific reference table for calculating lifespans or durations</note>. || 3 ||

**Commentary**—Multiply the house of the question by its own number and add the number specified in the <term>Vijjaha</term>. If the <term>geomantic figure</term> <note>original: *shakal*</note> that appears in the question’s house is also in that same house within the *Vijjaha* sequence—meaning the house positions in the question and the *Vijjaha* are identical—then the task will be completed in a time equal to the number in that *Vijjaha* row. 

The core idea is this: if the <term>figure</term> in the question's house is in its "own house" in the *Vijjaha* row, use the corresponding *Vijjaha* number. These numbers should be taken from the previously mentioned mathematical table (1, 3, 6, 10, etc.), which range from 1 to 136. 

When a <term>figure</term> <note>original: *khanda*</note> is not in its own house in either the <term>spread</term> or the <term>Vijjaha</term>, the rule is as follows: if the figure appears in the *Vijjaha* in a house *prior* to the question’s house, subtract the house number of the question (as indicated in the spread) from the *Vijjaha* number. If it appears in a house *ahead* of the question's house in the *Vijjaha*, then add the house number of the question from the spread to the *Vijjaha* number.

If that <term>figure</term> is repeated in the spread, the same method applies. If it is repeated in the 2nd, 3rd, or other houses, one should subtract the numbers of all those houses from the "past" <note>original: *gat*; elapsed time</note> or add them to the "future" <note>original: *gamya*; time yet to come</note> accordingly. 

If the figure of the question is not found in the *Vijjaha* at all, then look at the previously mentioned <term>Longevity Cycle</term>. Locate where the question’s figure is, and the number in the <term>table cell</term> <note>original: *kostaka*</note> below it that matches the question's house should be understood as the <term>duration</term> <note>original: *avadhi*</note>. For the sake of easy understanding, the meaning is restated: the number that is thus proven/calculated should be known as the time limit of the question. Here, the duration is always equal to the number found in the aforementioned Longevity Cycle. || 1 || 2 || 3 ||

<summary>This page introduces the "Sixth Gem," focusing on the "Vijjaha" and "Longevity Cycle" (Ayushya-chakra) methods to calculate the specific timing and duration for when a geomantic prediction will come to pass.</summary>
<keywords>timing of events, Vijjaha table, Ayushya-chakra, geomantic figures, prastara spread, calculation methods, Ramal astrology</keywords>

<meta>Continuing from page 85, which discussed using the Vijjaha table to calculate a specific numerical value, this page explains how to interpret that number into actual units of time—such as days, weeks, months, or years—depending on where the geomantic figure is positioned within the sixteen houses of the chart.</meta>

<page-num>( 86 )</page-num>
<header>[ Gems of Ramal Astrology-</header>

# He now explains what that number represents.

## Regarding the four groups of four beginning with the <term>Ummahat</term>, and the
## repetition of the <term>figures</term>—days, weeks, months, and years 
## respectively, or these may be known from the omen-house. || 4 ||
## The rule for days and so on is standard, but <term>Vipalas</term> and such 
## are generally for [urgent matters]. In cases of imminent
## childbirth, <term>Vipalas</term> and <term>Palas</term> are used. || 5 ||
## <term>Ghatikas</term> and <term>Praharas</term> should also be spoken of
## in sequence by using one's own intelligence. In a question regarding rain
## during the rainy season, use <term>Ghatikas</term> and <term>Muhurtas</term>. || 6 ||
## <term>Praharas</term> and days should also be predicted based
## on the strength of probability. This "General Method"
## is proclaimed for all types of questions. || 7 ||

**Commentary**—If the <term>geomantic figure</term> <note>original: *shakal*</note> appears in the first four houses of the 16-house <term>spread</term> <note>original: *prastara*</note>, known as the **Ummahant** <note>the "Mothers"; houses 1-4</note> group, the number indicates the count of **days**. If it appears in the four houses starting from the fifth, known as the **Banat** <note>the "Daughters"; houses 5-8</note> group, it indicates the count of **weeks**. If it appears in the four houses starting from the ninth, known as the **Muttallav** <note>the "Procreators"; houses 9-12</note> group, it indicates **months**. And if it appears in the four houses starting from the thirteenth, known as the **Javadaat** <note>the "Descendants"; houses 13-16</note> group, it indicates **years**. Alternatively, this can be determined by the sequence of the <term>omens</term>. || 4 ||

The rule for days and such is the standard, but specifically for events that happen very quickly, such as an imminent **childbirth**, the time is given in <term>Palas</term> and <term>Vipalas</term> <note><term>Pala</term> is approximately 24 seconds; <term>Vipala</term> is 0.4 seconds</note>. || 5 || Units like <term>Ghatikas</term> <note>a period of 24 minutes</note> and <term>Praharas</term> <note>a "watch" of 3 hours</note> should also be mentioned after assessing the probability of the task and using one's own intellect to consider the location and time. Similarly, for a question about rain during the monsoon season, one should predict the timing in <term>Ghatikas</term> and <term>Muhurtas</term> <note>a period of 48 minutes</note>; in other seasons, one should speak of days. || 6 ||

<term>Praharas</term>, days, and so on should be predicted based on the strength of the probability; one should apply their own intelligence to judge what is likely at that specific moment. This is called the **General Method** <note>original: *sthula vidhi*; literally "gross" or "approximate" method</note> for all questions. || 7 ||

## Furthermore, I shall explain the "Precise Duration" in detail.
## For that purpose, one should write the four rows
## known as the <term>Ummahat</term>. || 8 ||

<summary>This page details the "General Method" for timing events in Ramal astrology, where the sixteen houses are divided into four groups to determine if a result will occur in days, weeks, months, or years. It also provides guidance on scaling these units down to minutes and seconds for urgent matters like childbirth or weather-related questions.</summary>
<keywords>Ramal astrology, geomancy timing, Ummahat, Banat, Muttallav, Javadaat, Ghatika, units of time, prediction methods</keywords>

--- Page 95 ---
<meta>Continuing from page 86, which explained how the position of a geomantic figure in the sixteen houses (Ummahant, Banat, Muttallav, etc.) determines the basic unit of time—days, weeks, months, or years—this page introduces a more refined method called the "Ummahant Chakra" to pinpoint exactly when within those periods an event will occur.</meta>

[Sixth Gem 6.] (87)
<page-num>87</page-num>

One should write four rows of eight figures each, separately categorizing those that possess "voids" <note>open points in the geomantic symbols</note> in the positions of Fire and the other elements, following the <term>Abdah</term> <note>a standard sequence in Ramal astrology: Alif (Fire), Ba (Air), Dal (Water), Ha (Earth)</note> order. || 9 ||
The four figures known as **Lahyana**, **Humra**, **Vayaza**, and **Ankisha** <note>These correspond to the geomantic figures: The Path, The Red, The White, and The Prison</note> are considered the "Pure Mothers" <note>original: *shuddha ummahant*</note> and should be placed at the beginning of the sequence. || 10 ||

**Commentary**—In the determination of a time limit for a task, to further investigate whether that task will be accomplished at the beginning, the middle, or the end of that period, a more "subtle time-frame" <note>original: *sukshma avadhi*</note> is explained. For this purpose, one must write the four rows of the table named **Ummahant**. || 8 || In the first row, write the figures that have a "void" <note>represented by a single dot rather than a double line</note> in the position of Fire, following the <term>Abdah</term> sequence. In the second and subsequent rows, write the figures for the elements of Air, Water, and Earth separately, eight figures in each, to complete the four rows. || 9 || **Lahyana**, **Humra**, **Vayaz**, and **Ankish** are the pure "Mothers"; in the first row—

->The Table of the Mothers (Ummahant Chakra).<-

Following this same order, one must write 8 figures <note>original: *shakal*</note> in each of the 4 rows. || 10 ||

->The Purpose of This Table.<-

Regarding the second moving element... from the Moon and the first eight segments... where the witness figure <note>original: *sakshi-khanda*</note> appears in the question. From this, the progression of high, low, and middle states is determined in sequence. || 11 || The past, present, and future should be understood by the wise by observing the witness figure. Where a "gentle" <note>benefic or auspicious</note> portion is seen, the work will reach its completion <note>original: *siddhida*</note> within that specific elemental division of the time limit. || 12 || The wise should determine the time as being equal to the Fire portion... by observing the disposition <note>original: *mizaj*</note> from its own house, or it should be spoken of based on the King's <note>ruling authority or the querent's status</note> actions. || 13 || From the start of the row, the time of manifestation...

<summary>This page describes how to construct and use the "Ummahant Chakra" (Mothers' Table), a specialized grid used in Ramal astrology to divide predicted timeframes into precise sub-periods (past, present, and future) based on the four elements.</summary>

<meta>Continuing from page 87, which detailed the construction of the Ummahant Chakra (a table of the "Mothers" or primary geomantic figures) categorized by their elemental voids, this page concludes the rules for using that table to predict exactly when an event occurred or will occur.</meta>

<page-num>88</page-num>
<header>In the Ramal Navaratna (The Nine Gems of Ramal)—</header>

...and one should speak [of the past] from the eighth **figure** <note>original: *shakal*; the geomantic symbols used in Ramal</note>. One should speak of the present from the first figure of the eighth row. || 14 ||
The future should be spoken of from the first row of the fourth set of rows. Sudden or accidental occurrences should be spoken of from the first strong figure. || 15 ||
This method has been described by me as the supreme way for determining the time limit of all questions. A wise man should first consider the possibility of the task's success and then speak his prediction. || 16 ||

->Thus ends the Sixth Gem, named the "Determination of the Time Limit," in the *Ramala Navaratna*. || 6 ||<-

**Commentary**—The purpose of the **Ummahant** <note>literally "The Mothers"; here referring to a specific diagnostic table</note> is this: one or two dots—however many are obtained—should be moved from the fifteenth house <note>the house of the "Judge" in a Ramal chart</note>. Whichever of the first eight segments this movement reaches should be known as the "witness" <note>original: *sakshi*</note> for the question. The method for moving these dots was explained previously. Following that witness figure, one should take the numbers for the time limit in order—behind, ahead, below, and above. || 11 ||

These are of three types: first, the present; second, the future; and third, the "accidental" <note>original: *akasmika*; referring to sudden or unexpected events</note>. These are somewhat self-evident and sometimes mixed; the scholar should observe them in the result. Where a "gentle" figure <note>original: *saumya*; a benefic or auspicious symbol</note> appears, one should know there will be success in the task. If a figure is in its own "house" in the fire element portion, the task will be successfully accomplished. 

The clear meaning is this: if the "witness" figure in the **Ummahant** row is in the same house as it was in the previous row, the result is accidental/sudden. If it is in a previous house within its own row, it indicates the past, and if it is in a house ahead of itself, it indicates the future. || 12 || 

In that regard, just as with the fire element portion, scholars should observe and speak when it is in a previous house. If it is in its own house within the "disposition" <note>original: *mizaj*; a term from Persian/Arabic medicine and astrology referring to temperament or nature</note> row, then one should speak from that own-house. If the task is of great importance, one should also speak from the tenth house <note>traditionally the house of career, status, and major actions</note>. || 13 || At the beginning of the row, the past should be spoken of from the eighth figure. The present of the eighth figure should be spoken of from the first figure—that is, whichever figure is further ahead in their respective sections...

<summary>This page concludes the sixth chapter on time-determination in Ramal astrology, explaining how to move dots from the "Judge" (15th house) to identify a "witness" figure that reveals if an event is past, present, or future.</summary>
<keywords>Ramal, geomancy, Ummahant, time limits, past present future, astrology, witness figure, tenth house</keywords>

<meta>Continuing from page 88, which discussed the "Ummahant" table and the three types of time (past, present, and future) for predicting event outcomes, this page concludes the rules for timing and transitions into the Seventh Gem, focusing on "Mushti" (hidden object) questions.</meta>

<page-num>89</page-num>
<header>The Seventh Gem 7] ( 89 )</header>

Just as described, the last row should be understood as the end of the matter coming forward, and the fourth row should be known as the figure <note>original: *shakal*; the geomantic symbols used in Ramal</note> indicating "accidental" or sudden time. That is to say, the row below it in the previous spread <note>original: *aag*; likely referring to the sequence of the chart</note> should be known, and the future time of the fourth row is told by the first row. In other texts, the method for determining the day of the week <note>original: *vāra*</note> is as follows: in the spread <note>original: *prastāra*; the arrangement of the sixteen geomantic houses</note>, if the question figure is in the same house as it appears in the temperament <note>original: *mijāja*; the underlying nature or row of the figure</note> row, then the task will be accomplished on that figure’s corresponding day. If it is repeated in two or three houses, the day should be determined by whichever is the strongest. If it is in its own house but not in its own temperament, then the day should be predicted based on the day associated with the question figure itself. || 14 || 15 ||

This alternative method I have stated is for determining the time limit of all questions. In this as well, a wise man should first observe the possibility of the task's success and then speak his prediction based on the judgment of his own intellect. || 16 ||

->Thus ends the Sixth Gem, named the "Statement of the Method of Questioning," in the Mahidhari vernacular commentary on the *Ramala Navaratna*. || 6 ||<-

## Now, in the seventh [Gem], the types of "Fist" questions <note>original: *muṣṭi-prakāra*; a divination method where the practitioner identifies an object hidden in the querent's closed fist</note> are told. First, the forms of the figures useful for hidden object questions are described:

> The figures of Jupiter and Venus, *Bayaj* and *Ijjatama*, are gentle.
> Those of Mars, Ketu, and the Sun—such as *Jamata*—are harsh. || 1 ||
> Those of Saturn and Rahu, like *Tarik*, are both gentle and harsh.
> They are powerless or powerful respectively, according to the elements beginning with Fire. || 2 ||

**Commentary**—Now, in the Seventh Gem, the "Fist Questions" are described. First, the natures of the figures <note>original: *khanda*; literally "parts" or "segments," referring here to the geomantic figures</note> useful for these questions are stated: the figures of Jupiter <note>original: *guru*</note> and Venus <note>original: *bhārgava*</note>, namely *Bayaj* <note>Al-Bayad, "The White"</note> and *Ijjatama* <note>Al-Ijtima, "The Conjunction"</note>, are **gentle** <note>original: *mṛdu*</note>. The figures belonging to Mars <note>original: *kuja*</note>, Ketu <note>original: *shikhi*</note>, and the Sun <note>original: *nānā*; a rare synonym here for the Sun/Surya</note>, such as *Jamata* <note>Al-Jama'a, "The Gathering"</note>, are called **harsh** <note>original: *niṣṭhura*</note>. || 1 || The figures of Saturn and Rahu, and...

<page-num>90</page-num>
<header>[ In the Ramala Navaratna --</header>

The *Tariq* <note>original: *tariq*; meaning "The Path," one of the 16 geomantic figures</note> (even, soft, or harsh) are both soft and hard. Among these, the weak and powerful qualities should be known according to the sequence of the fire and other elements. || 2 ||

### Now, the description of colors in the figures.

> The colors are: yellow, red, white, red-black, white-black, wheat-colored, white-yellow, black, extremely black, || 3 ||
> black-red, white, yellow, dusky <note>original: *shyama*; a dark, greyish-blue or blackish color</note>, white-black, green-dusky-yellow, green-white-black, || 4 ||
> green, and green-white-dusky. According to the order of the figures <note>original: *Lahyana* etc., refers to the sequence of the 16 signs</note>, these colors should always be understood by the wise. || 5 ||

### Form and Shape.

> Long, circular, long again, square, somewhat long, not quite circular, circular, triangular <note>original: *vyasra*; three-sided</note>, circular, and long-wide; || 6 ||
> long-wide-long, long-wide-long again, circular, circular, long, and wide—these are the shapes in the sequence of the geomantic figures. || 7 ||

### The Locations are stated.

> *Nusratul-Kharij* <note>original: *Nusrutwari*; meaning "Outward Victory"</note>, *Lahiyan* <note>meaning "The Beard"</note>, and *Awtul-Kharij* <note>original: *Avatvekharijas*; meaning "Outward Threshold"</note> are found in the desert. The cities and forest dwellings are indicated by *Tariq* <note>meaning "The Path"</note> on agricultural paths. || 8 ||
> Gold and silver shops are the place of *Qabd al-Dakhil* <note>original: *Kajjud-dakhilla*; meaning "Inward Possession"</note> in assembly halls. *Ankish* <note>meaning "The Constriction"</note> and *Humrah* <note>original: *Hatheshya*; meaning "The Red"</note> etc., are associated with houses. || 9 ||
> The place of *Naqi* <note>meaning "The Pure"</note> is a new village, and *Mata al-Dakhil* <note>original: *Matavedakhilam*; meaning "Inward Opinion"</note> is in the forest. In trees and in schools, the *Nusratul-Dakhil* <note>original: *Nusrud-dakhillam*; meaning "Inward Victory"</note> is found. || 10 ||
> In fields, entryways, and gardens, the house of *Bayaz* <note>meaning "The White"</note> is traditionally placed. *Qabd al-Kharij* <note>original: *Kabbajutul-kha*; meaning "Outward Possession"</note>...

<page-num>91</page-num>
<header>The Seventh Gem 7. ]</header>

In the mountains and forests is the place of the figure named *Farah* <note>original: *farah*; the Arabic *Al-Farah*, meaning "Joy," often associated with the figure Laetitia</note>. || 11 || In dancing halls, gardens, and places filled with pearls, *Ijtima* <note>original: *mutassim-ikhadhyam*; referring to *Ijtima*, meaning "Conjunction"</note> is celebrated as the location. The houses of doctors and those who wield violent weapons are said to be the place of *Humra* <note>original: *humra*; the Arabic *Al-Hamra*, meaning "The Red," often associated with the figure Rubeus</note>. || 12 ||

### Now, the description of Value and Worthlessness.

The figures *Lahyan* <note>meaning "The Beard"</note>, *Nusrat al-Kharij* <note>meaning "Outward Victory"</note>, and *Nusrat al-Dakhil* <note>meaning "Inward Victory"</note> are considered of high value <note>meaning expensive or precious in trade or loss</note>. *Bayad* <note>meaning "White"</note>, *Tariq* <note>meaning "The Path"</note>, *Utbat al-Kharij* <note>meaning "Outward Threshold"</note>, and *Qabd al-Dakhil* <note>meaning "Inward Capture"</note> are of moderate or equal value. || 13 ||

*Naqi* <note>meaning "Pure"</note>, *Ankis* <note>meaning "Downward/Inverted"</note>, *Ijtima* <note>meaning "Conjunction"</note>, *Utbat al-Dakhil* <note>meaning "Inward Threshold"</note>, and *Qabd al-Kharij* <note>meaning "Outward Capture"</note> are known as figures of low value. *Humra* <note>meaning "Red"</note>, *Farah* <note>meaning "Joy"</note>, and *Ijtima* <note>variation of the term</note> are also placed within these valuations. || 14 ||

### Now, the description of Lightness and Heaviness.

The segments belonging to the Fire and Air elements are indeed possessed of great weight <note>original: *bahubhārayutāni*; implying they carry a significant "burden" or influence</note>. The segments belonging to Water and Earth are dense and heavy. || 15 ||

### Now, another designation.

The four segments of Fire are the causes for nurturing others. The other segments are said by the great sages to be nurtured by others <note>This refers to the "feeding" or supportive relationship between different geomantic houses</note>. || 16 ||

### Now, another designation.

*Lahyan*, *Farah*, *Humra*, *Nusrat al-Kharij*, *Utbat al-Kharij*, *Naqi*, and *Ijtima* are remembered as being joined or united <note>referring to their traditional groupings in the Ramala charts</note>. || 17 ||

### Now, the enemies of the segments and so on.

The enemy of the *Kharij* <note>Outward-moving</note> figures is Fire. For the Earthy figures, the enemy is Stone <note>original: *drishat*; meaning rock or hardness</note>. For those of the Water element, Water is the enemy <note>perhaps implying a self-canceling effect</note>, and for the rest, the support or base itself is the enemy. || 18 ||

### Now, the designation of "Full" and other states.

*Ijtima*, *Nusrat*, *Utbat*, and *Qabd al-Dakhil*—these are considered the "Full" segments. The others are "Broken" or incomplete segments. || 19 ||

<meta>Continuing from page 91, which categorized the geomantic figures by their economic value (high, moderate, and low) and their general locations in the world (mountains, gardens, hospitals, etc.), this page defines their sensory attributes—specifically tastes—and their classification into the three kingdoms of nature: mineral, vegetable, and animal.</meta>

<page-num>92</page-num>
<header>[ Description of Ramal (Geomancy) --</header>

### Now, the Tastes of the Figures.

The figures associated with the element of Fire possess tastes that are bitter and pungent; the figures of the Air element are known for tastes that are sweet and sour. || 20 ||
In the figures known as *Nusrat al-Dakhil* <note>meaning "Inward Victory"</note>, *Nusrat al-Kharij* <note>meaning "Outward Victory"</note>, *Bayad* <note>meaning "White"</note>, *Naqi* <note>meaning "Pure"</note>, and *Tariq* <note>meaning "The Path"</note>, the tastes are described as sweet, salty, and bitter, as well as an impure salty taste. || 21 ||
In the figures *Qabd al-Dakhil* <note>meaning "Inward Capture"</note>, *Ijtima* <note>meaning "Conjunction"</note>, and *Uqlid* <note>meaning "The Key"</note>, as well as in *Ankis* <note>meaning "Inverted/Downward"</note>, *Utbat al-Dakhil* <note>meaning "Inward Threshold"</note>, and *Humra* <note>meaning "The Red"</note>, the tastes are sweet-sour and an intensely dark sourness. These are the tastes of the figures as proclaimed by the ancient sages. || 22 ||

#### On Foundations and Construction.

All figures belonging to the element of Fire are the causes or drivers of construction <note>original: *nirmana-hetavo*; referring to the creative or destructive energy needed for building</note>. Apart from these, the Earth figures relate to the ground, foundations, and the essence of craftsmanship. || 23 ||
The remaining figures are also secondary causes for various crafts. Having reflected upon these associations, the wise practitioner should provide answers to the seeker's questions with a composed mind. || 24 ||

### Now, the Designations of Mineral, Plant, and Animal.

Mineral, plant, then mineral again; plant-animal <note>a combination</note>, and then plant; plant-animal, then plant; mineral-plant, and finally mineral. || 25 ||
Plant-animal and animal-plant follow according to the sequence of the figures <note>term: *shakuna*; here referring to the sixteen geomantic figures</note>. This classification is highly useful in "fist-guessing" <note>term: *mushti-gyana*; a divination technique where the practitioner guesses what object is hidden in a seeker's closed hand</note> and other such inquiries, which is why they are explained here. || 26 ||

---

I have already provided these in a circular chart <note>original: *chakra*</note> at the end of the first "Gem" <note>chapter</note> of this book. However, for the easy understanding of the readers, a commentary on these twenty-four verses is also written here in the form of a chart. Readers may understand the classifications by referring to this diagram:

The meaning of the 18th verse, as shown in the chart, is as follows: 
For the "Outward" <note>original: *kharij*</note> figures, Fire is the enemy. For an object held in the hand <note>original: *mushtaka*</note>, Stone is the enemy. For the figures *Ankis*, *Ulka*, and *Kabd al-Kharij*, Stone is also the enemy. For the figures *Farhan*, *Humra*, *Ijtima*, *Utbat al-Kharij*, and *Naqi*—as well as for all other figures of the Earth element—the enemy is Water.
