<meta>This page continues the technical classification of geomantic figures, moving from their general properties into a specific application of the "Ramal" (geomancy) system: the "Fist Query." This is a popular divinatory practice used to identify hidden objects or to determine which person in a crowd is holding something.</meta>

<page-num>94</page-num>
<header>[ In the Nine Gems of Ramal —-</header>

### Now, the Explanation of Queries Regarding Hidden Objects in the Fist

By the figures <term>Humra</term> <note>The "Red" figure, associated with the element of Fire</note> and <term>Naqi</term> <note>The "Pure" figure, associated with the element of Water</note>, or by even one of them appearing, an object should certainly be considered to be in the fist at the start of the inquiry. If these figures appear in many places in the spread, then a collection of various objects should be inferred. || 27 || If both are absent, the wise practitioner should consider the fist to be empty and speak accordingly. According to another school of thought, if the <term>Sabit</term> <note>Arabic: *Thabit* — "Fixed" or stable figures</note> and <term>Dakhil</term> <note>Arabic: *Dakhil* — "Entering" or incoming figures</note> figures are in the fourth and sixth houses, the presence of an object in the fist should be declared. || 28 ||

**Commentary**—This is the consideration of the "fist query," used when someone asks, "What is in my hand?" In this, first consider whether there is an object in the hand or not. Having created the <term>Prastara</term> <note>The formal grid or spread of the sixteen geomantic figures</note>, if the figures **Humra** and **Naqi** are present, or if even one of them is there, then certainly there is some object in the hand. If those figures appear in many "houses" (positions), one should say there are many objects in the hand. || 27 || If neither of those two figures is in the spread, the intelligent person should conclude that the hand is empty. And if the **Sabit** and **Dakhil** figures are in the fourth and sixth houses, there is an object in the hand—this is an alternative opinion. || 28 ||

If a crowd of people asks to identify who holds the object in their fist, a row of ten people should be formed immediately. Then, create another row of the remaining people. Wherever the <term>Kuja-khanda</term> <note>Literally "Segment of Mars"; likely referring to a specific geomantic figure or numerical calculation associated with the planet Mars</note> appears, that is where the fist [holding the object] is located. || 29 || Having made the query, if the count arising from the "mountain-petal" <note>A technical numerical calculation in Ramal</note> reaches the ninth house, and if the <term>Sabit</term> portion or the <term>Dakhil</term> is on the right side, or if from the left side in the ninth house there is <term>Kharij</term> <note>Arabic: *Kharij* — "Exiting" or outgoing figures</note> or <term>Munkib</term> <note>Arabic: *Munqalib* — "Changeable" or "Inverted" figures</note>, then the person at that position is the one holding the object in their hand. || 30 || In this way, when the man with the object is identified, his actions are determined by the <term>Dakhil</term> and <term>Sabit</term> figures. The object will be in the right hand; otherwise, it is in the other [left] hand.

<vocab>मुष्टिप्रश्न (Mushtiprashna) - A query regarding a hidden object in the hand; रमल (Ramal) - The art of geomancy, originally from Arabic traditions; हुम्रा (Humra) - The "Red" geomantic figure; नकी (Naki) - The "Pure" or "Clean" geomantic figure; शकल (Shakal) - The geomantic "figure" or "sign"; प्रस्तार (Prastara) - The mathematical spread or grid of figures; साबित (Sabit) - Fixed figures indicating stability; दाखिल (Dakhil) - Entering figures indicating arrival; मुन्क्कीब (Munkib) - Changeable or inverted figures.</vocab>

<header>Chapter 7 ] ( 95 )</header>

> **In case of conflict among these, judge by the stronger force. || 31 || Or, judge as before from the figure arising from the fifth and sixth houses. Speak of whether it is in the left or the right hand, or even if it originates from water. || 32 ||**

**Commentary** — When a "Fist Query" <note>An inquiry to find a hidden object held in someone's hand</note> is posed within a large group of people, one should have the people sit in rows. Arrange the first ten people in one row in front of you, the next ten in a second row, and so on, creating separate rows of ten for as many people as are present. Now, to determine which of these groups of ten holds the object: || 29 || 

Look at the <term>Shakal</term> <note>The geomantic figure or symbol</note> located in the seventh "house" or position of the <term>Prastara</term> <note>The rectangular grid or spread of sixteen figures</note>. Take the numerical value of that figure according to the <term>Abjad</term> <note>The Arabic system of assigning numerical values to letters/figures</note> order; the person corresponding to that number is the one holding the object. There is also a specific order for counting these people: if the figure in the ninth house of the spread is <term>Sabit</term> <note>Arabic: *Thabit* — a "Fixed" or stable figure</note> or <term>Dakhil</term> <note>Arabic: *Dakhil* — an "Entering" or incoming figure</note>, you must count the people starting from your right-hand side. If the ninth house contains a figure that is <term>Kharij</term> <note>Arabic: *Kharij* — an "Exiting" or outgoing figure</note> or <term>Munqaviv-badal</term> <note>A technical term likely referring to a "Converted" or "Strengthened" figure; usually *Munkalib* (reversing)</note>, count from your left-hand side. The person on whom the count ends is the one holding the object. || 30 ||

Once the person holding the object is identified, to determine which hand it is in, observe the following: if the fifth, sixth, and ninth houses contain **Dakhil** or **Sabit** figures, the object is in the person’s right hand. If those houses contain **Kharij** or <term>Munkalib</term> <note>Arabic: *Munkalib* — a "Reversing" or unstable figure</note> figures, the object is in the left hand. If these three houses contain conflicting figures—meaning some are **Sabit/Dakhil** and others are **Kharij/Munkalib**—then you must declare the direction (left or right) based on whichever figure is the "strongest" among them. || 31 || 

Alternatively, one may combine the fifth and sixth figures to create a new figure and judge based on the previous rules, or one may determine the left/right hand query solely from the ninth house. || 32 ||

> **I shall now speak of the method for describing the object itself. Its hardness or softness is known from the first house, and its color from the second house. || 33 || Its form from the third, and from the fourth, whether it is long, round, or otherwise. Its price and value from the fifth...**

*   The **hardness or softness** of the object is known from the **first house**.
*   The **color** (complexion) from the **second house**. || 33 ||
*   The **appearance** from the **third**.
*   The **shape** (whether it is large, small, round, etc.) and its **location** from the **fourth**.
*   The **price** or value from the **fifth**.
*   The **lightness or heaviness** from the **sixth**. || 34 ||
*   Whether the object is **solitary** or part of a set, and the cause of its **sturdiness** from the **seventh**. Furthermore, the **quantity** or number of items is determined by observing which house the figure found in the seventh repeats itself.
*   The **enmity** <note>In this context, "enmity" likely refers to hidden defects or things that "oppose" the object's quality</note> from the **eighth**. || 35 ||
*   Whether the object is **fragmented or whole** from the **ninth**.
*   The **taste** or flavor from the **tenth house**.
*   Its **construction** or how it was made from the **eleventh**.
*   Whether it is <term>Dhatu-mula</term> <note>Literally "Mineral-Root"; a classification system for materials: Dhatu (minerals/metals), Mula (roots/vegetable matter), or Jiva (living beings/animal products)</note> from the **twelfth**. || 36 ||
Whatever house you are interpreting, you must also consider the possibility of that outcome by looking at the **seventh house** with your own intelligence. Do not predict anything that is impossible. || 37 || If a contradiction is found between the results of the first and seventh <term>Shakals</term> <note>The geomantic figures or symbols</note>, then create a single new figure by combining those two, and describe the qualities of the hidden object according to that new figure. || 38 ||

<header>The Gem of Ramal 7 ] ( 17 )</header>

If the figure in the third house <note>The third house traditionally represents the "appearance" or environment of the object.</note> is such that the object's interior is empty, it should be declared as hollow. If the figure **Tarika** <term>Tarika: Literally "The Path," represented by a single vertical line of dots; it signifies thinness or transit.</term> is placed in the third or ninth house, the object has a hole or is perforated. || 40 ||

Alternatively, it may be richly decorated with various engravings or marked with subtle signs. If either **Naki** <term>Naki: "The Pure," a figure associated with clarity.</term> or **Faraha** <term>Faraha: "Joy," a figure often associated with beauty.</term> is in the third house, then that object is triangular in shape and possesses a hole. || 41 ||

If the figure **Ukla** <term>Ukla: "The Knot" or "The Bond," representing restriction or wrapping.</term> is stated to be in the third or first house, the object is wrapped in cloth or the bark <note>original: "valkala"</note> of a tree. If the **Air element** <note>In geomancy, figures are composed of four elements: Fire, Air, Water, and Earth.</note> is in the ninth house, one should say the object is covered in hair or wool. || 42 ||

If the **Fire element** is present when **Ukla** is mentioned, one should then say the object has been burnt by fire. When the **Water element** is present alongside a fire-nature house, it might be something like a ball or pill filled with cotton. || 43 ||

Thus has the "Distinction of the Fist" been correctly described in the *Ramala* by the ancients, following the traditions of the past. For the sake of the fame and honor of the astrologers <note>original: "daivajña," literally "knowers of fate."</note>, and for the entertainment of the people, one should perform this divination. || 44 ||

->This concludes the seventh Gem, named the Description of the Fist Query, in the Jewel-Mine of Ramal. || 7 ||<-

If the figure **Bayaja** <term>Bayaja: "The White," a figure indicating brightness or emptiness.</term> is in the third house, the object in the fist is hollow inside or empty. If **Tarika** is in the third or ninth house, there is a hole in that object. || 40 || 

Or, it is endowed with various types of pictures or marked with tiny signs. If in either the 3rd or 9th house there is **Naki** or **Faraha**, then that object is triangular and has a hole. || 41 ||

### Now begins the Eighth Gem: The Chapter on the Binding of Names.

> Without revealing the name, there is no total peace of mind,
> and the success of the query is not easily obtained. ||
> Therefore, I shall explain the complete "Binding of Names,"
> from which popular belief and great fame are won. || 1 ||
> First, a wise person must determine exactly
> how many letters are in the name ||
> of a thief or other person, and only then
> should they strive to bring forth the letters. || 2 ||
> In a query regarding thieves or the illiterate, the number
> of letters is determined by the figure **Faraha** <term>Faraha: "Joy," a geomantic figure composed of two dots, one dot, and one dot (from top to bottom). It is generally auspicious.</term>. ||
> Therefore, the practitioner of Ramal <note>original: "Rammal"</note> should first observe the position of **Faraha**. || 3 ||

**Commentary—** Complete mental satisfaction only comes from revealing the specific name, because the success of the divination is not considered "easy" or "proven" otherwise. For this reason, I am describing the complete process of "Name-Binding," which grants the practitioner both the trust of the people and a good reputation. || 1 || First, a wise practitioner should decide how many letters are in the name. One should exert effort to deduce the letters of a thief's name or the names of others in this way. || 2 || In a query to find the letters of names for thieves and others, the number of letters is indicated by the figure **Faraha**. Therefore, the expert in Ramal should first look for the **Faraha** figure in the chart. || 3 ||

<page-num>99</page-num>
<header>The Eighth Gem [8]</header>

### Determining the Number of Letters in a Name

If the figure **Faraha** <note>original: "farihā" (Joy). A geomantic figure represented by dots and lines.</note> is positioned in the first house, the name contains seven letters. For the houses from the second to the sixth, and the eighth to the eleventh, the count follows a specific sequence. If it resides in the seventh or the twelfth, the count is also seven. || 4 || If it is in the thirteenth house, the count matches the **Abjad** <term>Abjad: A system where each letter of the alphabet is assigned a numerical value.</term> value of the figure in the second house. If it is in the fourteenth or sixteenth house, there are four letters. If the figure appears in multiple houses, determine the number from the house that possesses the most strength, as previously described. || 5 || If **Faraha** does not appear anywhere in the query spread, then the number of letters in the name is equal to the **Abjad** value derived from the figure in the first house. || 6 ||

**Commentary:** To determine the number of letters in a name: if **Faraha** is in the first house, the name will have 7 letters. From the second house to the seventh, increase the count by one for each house; from the eighth to the eleventh, decrease it by one. For example:
- Second house: 2 letters
- Third house: 3 letters
- Fourth house: 4 letters
- Fifth house: 5 letters
- Sixth house: 6 letters
- Seventh house: 7 letters
Then, counting down:
- Eighth house: 6 letters
- Ninth house: 5 letters
- Tenth house: 4 letters
- Eleventh house: 3 letters
And in the twelfth house, there are 7 letters. || 4 ||

If **Faraha** is in the thirteenth house, look at the <term>Shakal</term> <note>Shakal: The specific "shape" or "figure" formed by the geomantic dots.</note> in the second house; the number of letters is equal to that figure's value in the **Abjad** system. If **Faraha** is in the fourteenth or sixteenth houses, there are 4 letters. If **Faraha** appears in many places, the number should be declared based on whichever instance is the most "powerful" (astrologically dominant). || 5 || 

If **Faraha** is not present in the <term>Prastara</term> <note>Prastara: The "spread" or horizontal arrangement of the sixteen geomantic houses.</note>, then the number of letters is determined by the **Abjad** value of the figure sitting in the first house. || 6 ||

### Now, Concerning the Letters.
->A chart for this purpose:<-

Now I shall describe the method for identifying all the individual letters. One should derive the first section <note>original: "khanda"</note> from the first and thirteenth houses. || 7 || In the houses marked nine, eight, and two, one should write the **Abjad** letters (A, B, J, D, H, W, Z...) in their proper order.

<page-num>101</page-num>
<header>The Eighth Gem 8.</header>

# The Eighth Gem 8.

The wise practitioner should form a single figure <note>original: "shakala," the basic 4-line symbolic unit in geomancy.</note> by combining the first and thirteenth figures of the layout <note>original: "prastāra," the grid of 16 houses.</note>. In the chart, whatever house that figure occupies, find the corresponding value in the upper row. || 9 ||

Take the number generated from that figure according to the *Vijadaha* <note>A Sanskritization of "Abjad," the system where letters are assigned numerical values.</note> method and find the coordinate in the grid. || 10 ||

By following the horizontal order in the two-axis letter chart, the letter found at the intersection of those coordinates is the first letter of the name. || 11 ||

The second letter is derived in the same way from the fourth figure. The third letter is produced from the seventh and fifteenth figures <note>original: "naga-tithi-udbhavāc-cāpi"; Naga represents 7 and Tithi represents 15.</note>. || 12 ||

The fourth letter is extracted from the sixteenth house. The fifth letter is found through the figure produced from the "odd-half-zero" count <note>A technical calculation involving the dots and lines of the geomantic figures.</note>. || 13 ||

From the count of the central zeros combined with the double-line marks, whatever figure is produced, use the previously mentioned method to find the sixth letter <note>original: "arṇa-rasonmitam"; Rasa represents 6.</note>. || 14 ||

From the group of the thirteen <note>original: "vishvā," meaning 13.</note> and the zeros, the seventh letter <note>original: "naga-sankhyakaṃ"; Naga represents 7.</note> is identified. Thus, the collection of letters is declared; however, the vowel marks <note>original: "mātrā"</note> must be inferred by the practitioner's own intelligence. || 15 ||

The wise person should slowly deduce the names of any person, or names related to the origin of an event, according to the specific country and caste of the subject. || 16 ||

**Commentary:** <note>original: "ṭīkā"</note> Now, the method for extracting letters is described. Combine <note>original: "jarba," likely from the Arabic 'darb' meaning to strike or multiply</note> the first and thirteenth figures of the layout to create a new figure. Locate which house that figure occupies in the layout and find that number in the top row of the letter-grid. Then, see which house that same figure occupies in the *Vijadaha* sequence and find that number in the horizontal row. At the point where the vertical column of the first number and the horizontal row of the second number meet, you will find the first letter of the name.

<vocab>अष्टरत्नम्: Eighth Gem (chapter title), शकल: Geomantic figure, प्रस्तार: Layout/Grid, विजदह: Abjad system, टीका: Commentary, जर्ब: Combination/Multiplication, कोष्ठक: Cell/Column, रामल: Ramala (Geomancy)</vocab>

<summary>This page provides a step-by-step algorithm for identifying the seven letters of a person's name by combining specific houses from a geomantic reading and plotting them on the Abjad chart.</summary>

Knowing the letters. || 9 || 10 || 11 || Now, here is the method for obtaining the second letter:

Combine the fourth and fourteenth figures <note>original: "shakal," the four-line symbolic units of geomancy.</note>, and from the resulting figure, determine the second letter of the name according to the previously mentioned sequence. In the same way, the third letter is to be known from the figure produced by the seventh and fifteenth houses. || 12 || 

Similarly, take the fourth letter from the section produced by the sixteenth and seventeenth figures <note>The 17th figure in Ramal is often a "derived" figure used for confirmation, sometimes called the 'Sub-Judge'.</note>. To know the fifth letter, it is said: add the dots <note>original: "bindu"</note> of all the odd positions in the layout <note>original: "prastāra," the grid of 16 houses</note>—specifically houses 1, 3, 5, 7, 9, 11, 13, and 15. Divide this sum by 16. Whatever remainder is left, find that house in the layout. From the figure found in that house, determine the fifth letter according to the sequence mentioned earlier. || 13 || 

Count the number of lines <note>original: "rekha"</note> in the figures of the central houses—1, 4, 7, and 10—and double that number. Add to this the number of dots found in those same four houses. Divide the resulting sum by 16. The remainder indicates the house number; find the figure in that house to determine the sixth letter. And by adding the zeros <note>dots/bindu</note> of houses 13, 14, 15, and 16, determine the seventh letter using the aforementioned sequence. 

In this, it should be remembered that if a specific figure is not found in the layout, one should proceed using the figure from the layout house that corresponds to that figure’s position in the *Vijadaha* <note>A reference to the "Abjad" system where each figure has a fixed numerical rank.</note>. Such is the method described for extracting letters. The vowels <note>original: "matra"</note> for these letters should be determined through one's own intellect. || 14 || 15 || 

The scholar should announce the name of a person or a hidden object <note>original: "mushtigat," literally "held in the fist," referring to objects concealed by a querent to test the diviner.</note> after considering the naming customs prevalent in that specific region and community, using their own wisdom. || 16 ||

-> Combine the first and the thirteenth <note>original: "vishva," a code word for 13</note>; then, [locate] the figure in the tenth house of the *Vijadaha*. <-
-> Following that, find the letter associated with the sixth house of the layout; the letter produced by their intersection is the indicator. || 17 || <-
-> In a query, [combine] the fourth and the fourteenth <note>original: "shakra," a code word for 14</note> to find the figure in the ninth house. <-
-> By looking horizontally across the eighth house, one obtains the letter that acts as the indicator. || 18 || <-

**Commentary**—Now, an example of the letters of the name is given. In the layout <note>term: "prastāra" (layout/grid) — the 16-house arrangement used in geomancy.</note>, by combining the first and thirteenth figures <note>term: "shakal" (figure) — the four-line binary symbols used in Ramal.</note>, the *Ukla* figure is produced. Its position in the *Vijadaha* <note>term: "vijadaha" — a specific house configuration or counting system in Ramal, here referring to the 10th house.</note> is the tenth (10), and this same *Ukla* figure is in the sixth (6) house of the layout. Now, looking at the chart, find the intersection where the vertical column of the tenth (of the *Vijadaha*) and the horizontal row of the sixth house meet. Where these two cells align, the letter **S** (स) is found. This should be known as the first letter of the name. || 17 || 

Again, by combining the 4th and 14th figures of that same layout, the *Ankisha* figure is produced. This is in the ninth house of the query and the eighth house of the *Vijadaha*. Where the vertical from the 8th and the horizontal from the 9th houses meet, the letter **A** (अ) is found; this should be known as the second letter of the name. In this manner, examples for all letters should be understood. || 18 ||

> Now, the method for bringing forth the letters of the query is spoken by me. <note>decorative floral motif</note> Through the body
> and the segments located in the second, sixteenth, and final houses, one should extract the letters. || 19 ||
> In the wheel of nine primary houses, one should write the alphabet as before. Within that,
> the letter should be known from its own segment, as stated here. || 20 ||
> If there is an absence of its own house in the query, then however many houses
> forward or backward it may be, one should derive the forward or backward letter in that sequence. || 21 || Thus,
> through the segments of the 2nd, 4th, and 16th houses, one should compose the sixteen letters.
> The wise one who knows the science of Ramal <note>original: "ramlaviddhīro"</note> should also [determine] the vowels through their own intellect. || 22 ||

**Commentary**—Now, another method for obtaining the name letters is described: one should take the letters from the 1st to the 16th figures. || 19 || This is done as follows: count the segments <note>original: "khanda"</note> that appear in the 9th, 7th, and 5th houses of the layout chart in the alphabetical order <note>original: "harfākrama"</note>. Among these, whichever figure belongs to its own house, use the previously mentioned sequence—

### Now, the Clarification of the Thief.

> ->When there is a desire to bring a thief to sight within a crowd of people, then cast the lot.<-
> ->Likewise, having performed the inversion <note>term: "inkilāva" — a method of rearranging the geomantic layout to find hidden meanings</note>, consider which house the *Jama'at* figure <note>term: "jamāta" — a specific geomantic figure representing an assembly or collection</note> occupies. || 23 ||<-
> ->If that house contains the *Sabit* <note>term: "sābita" — a stable or fixed figure</note> or *Dakhil* <note>term: "dākhila" — an 'entering' figure indicating something coming toward the seeker</note>, then the thief is among the people in that direction.<-
> ->If it is otherwise <note>original: "naccānyathaivaṃ" — meaning if the figure is 'exiting' or 'moving'</note>, the thief is not there. In the same way, divide the men into two groups and examine the figures from the beginning. || 24 ||<-
> ->For the remainder also, proceed in two parts as before. One should perform this method repeatedly until only a single person remains. || 25 ||<-
> ->Now, if the *Jama'at* figure enters the inversion and is seen in the second or eighth parts... then the questioner himself is the thief; this should be spoken after careful thought through these five methods. || 26 ||<-

**Commentary**—The clarification of the thief is explained: when a thief is clearly among a large group of people, to reveal them, create a layout and perform the *Inkilav*. In this, whatever house the *Jama'at* figure occupies, if that same house in the first layout contains a *Sabit* or *Dakhil* figure, then the thief is within that group of people. If it is *Kharij* <note>term: "khārija" — an 'exiting' figure</note> or *Munqib* <note>term: "munqiba" — a 'transforming' or 'inverted' figure</note>, then the thief is not among them. When the thief is known to be within a certain group, divide those men into two rows and repeat the aforementioned method...

If there are few people and the total number is high, count through the row again from the start. ### Now, the Ninth Jewel: The Calculation of the Year’s Results.

On the day the tropical Sun enters its house <note>original: "sāyanārka" — referring to the Sun's entry into Aries using the tropical zodiac system</note>, the wise practitioner, filled with joy, maintaining a good appearance, and remembering their personal deity and mantra, should proceed. With a steady soul and being pure of body, they should cast the dice <note>term: "pāśaka" — the four-sided or oblong markers used in geomancy to generate figures</note> seven times upon the board for the success of the world’s annual predictions. || 1 || For all people, this should be performed on the day of the year’s entry <note>term: "varṣapraveśa" — the exact moment the year begins for a person or the world</note>. For the sake of attaining the results of the coming years, it may also be done on that day or another auspicious day. || 2 || Thus, there are two types of annual results here: those for the world and those for individual humans. Among these, the layout for the annual results of humans is described first. || 3 ||

**Commentary**—Now, in the Ninth Jewel, the consideration of the year is discussed. On the day of the Sun’s entry into tropical Aries <note>original: "sāyana meṣa saṃkrānti"</note>, in the forenoon, the astrologer should be purified by bathing and other rites. Being of a pleasant disposition and well-dressed, they should remember their personal deity and mantra. After consecrating the dice seven times with the mantra and steadying their mind, they should throw them upon the board. || 1 || Then, having created the layout <note>term: "prastāra" — the 16-house geomantic grid</note>, they should contemplate the auspicious and inauspicious events, the joy and sorrow, and the state of food and disease in the country for all people on that day of the year's entry. For the consideration of a specific individual, one should calculate this on their birthday (the moment of their annual return) or another auspicious day to determine the results for the entire year. || 2 || In this way, two types of annual results must be considered here: one for the world and one for each individual. First, the construction of the annual results for individuals is being set forth. || 3 ||

One should judge the results separately for each house, from the first through to the house of loss <note>term: "vyaya" — the twelfth house of the zodiac or geomantic chart</note>. Through the auspicious or inauspicious figures, one determines the nourishment or the loss of matters such as the body, wealth, and so on. || 4 || Even if an inauspicious omen <note>term: "śakuna" — here referring to a geomantic figure</note> appears, it should be known by the wise to grant nourishment if it is situated in its own house and is particularly strong. || 5 ||

> The results of "exit" and other movements are determined here through the "Outgoing" <note>original: "khārījādi" — referring to the *Khārij* or outgoing geomantic figures</note> figures.

**Commentary**—In this process, first consider the twelve houses starting from the Body (1st house) and ending with the house of Expenditure (12th house). One must judge whether there is growth or fulfillment from auspicious figures, or loss and harm from inauspicious ones, for each house individually. || 4 || Even if a figure <note>term: "śakala" — one of the sixteen tetragrams or symbols used in Ramala geomancy</note> is naturally inauspicious, if it is placed in its "own house" according to the sequence of the omens, it provides strength to that house. Scholars should particularly emphasize the results when a figure is strong. || 5 || Through the "Outgoing" <note>original: "khārīja"</note> figure, an "exit" (leaving or departing) is proven; through the "Stable" <note>original: "sābit"</note> figure, an "arrival" (entry or coming) is the result. Here, the word "and others" implies that just as the "Outgoing" is similar to the "Mutable" <note>original: "munqalīb"</note> figure, the "Stable" is similar to the "Entering" <note>original: "dākhil"</note> figure. If a figure is repeated <note>term: "punarukta" — when the same geomantic figure appears in more than one house in the chart</note> in a certain house, its results must be stated with even greater emphasis. That is, if an auspicious figure is repeated in an auspicious house, the good results are intensified; if an inauspicious figure is repeated in an inauspicious house, the bad results are intensified. In a moderate house, the result is middling—for instance, if an inauspicious figure is in an auspicious house or an auspicious figure is in an inauspicious house, a moderate result occurs. || 6 ||

->Now, the Combinations.<-

> If the figures of the Sun and Venus <note>original: "ravikavī" — Ravi (Sun) and Kavi (Venus)</note> are in the first, fifth, ninth, or tenth houses, it is considered a cause of happiness.
> Happiness is also found when the houses of the Body, Wealth, Siblings, Family, Friends, or the King <note>note: these refer to the 1st, 2nd, 3rd, 4th, 11th, and 10th houses respectively</note> are occupied by figures of the Sun, but not by those of Mars, Rahu, or Saturn. || 7 ||
> If the center houses <note>term: "kendra" — the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, which are the pillars of a chart</note> are joined with auspicious figures, one should predict immediate happiness and unthought-of wealth.
> If the house of Death (8th) is joined with the figures of Jupiter or Venus <note>original: "guru-bhārgava"</note>, it is auspicious; however, death is indicated if it contains the figures of Mars or Saturn. || 8 ||
> If an "Entering" <note>original: "dākhil"</note> figure is placed in the houses of Wealth (2nd) or Gains (11th), it is auspicious, bringing festivals, wealth, and great happiness.
> If the house of Enemies (6th) contains the figures of Saturn, Mars, or Rahu...

<page-num>१०९</page-num>
<header>The Ninth Jewel 9. ]</header>

To know the nature of the months: if the segment <note>original: "khaṇḍa" — a portion or figure within the geomantic chart</note> of that month is auspicious, one should predict favorable results; if the segment is inauspicious, one should consider the results unfavorable. || 11 ||
If the segment of a particular month is repeated in a house, the wise should assign the results pertaining to that house to that specific month. || 12 ||
If a favorable part <note>original: "dalam"</note> is produced from the combination of the half-month and the house-segment, then the result is auspicious. The results for repeated positions should be understood in their proper sequence. || 13 ||
If the month-segment is produced from two auspicious figures, then the result will be auspicious. If produced from two middling figures, the result is average; if from two malefic <note>original: "pāpābhyām" — literally "sinful" or "dark" figures</note> figures, the result is declared undesirable. || 14 ||

**Commentary**—If the figure <note>term: "śakala" — one of the sixteen geometric symbols used in Ramala divination</note> generated from the month-segment and the house-segment is auspicious, then the result will be favorable. In the same way, the results for repeated houses should be known in their respective order. || 13 || If the month-segment is born from auspicious figures, there will be a favorable result; from neutral figures, a middling result; and if born from malefic segments, the result is said to be undesirable (bad). || 14 ||

If there is friendship <note>note: "Friendship" refers to the elemental compatibility (Earth, Air, Fire, Water) between the figures and the houses</note> between the elements of the half-month and those of the house, it is auspicious. This applies to the month and the lord of that segment as well; however, if these are inimical or reversed, the result is the opposite. || 15 ||
The experts of Ramala <note>term: "Ramala" — a system of geomancy of Persian-Arabic origin that became highly integrated into Indian astrology</note> should also infer the same for repeated houses. The auspiciousness arising from the union of a repeated half-segment and a month-half segment brings about the desired outcome. || 16 ||
If there is friendship between the month and the witness-segments <note>term: "sākṣī" — the 'Witness' figures, usually the 15th and 16th figures in a chart, which summarize the reading</note>, everything will be auspicious. If they are inimical, it will be inauspicious, and if neutral, the result is recorded as average. || 17 ||
In the same manner as the month-segment, one should also contemplate the witness-segment. This logic should be applied to the second ruler of the segment as well. || 18 ||
One should follow the previously mentioned method and declare the results accordingly. If the segment is the opposite of the resident figure, that segment is born from its limbs...

<page-num>११०</page-num>
<header>[ On Ramala — ]</header>

...as before. || 19 || One should also declare the results for this according to the method of the **Universal Witness** <note>original: "sarva-sākṣi" — a specific summary figure in a Ramala chart that "witnesses" or oversees the entire reading</note>. In this way, one should contemplate the results for the month using these four segments. || 20 ||

**Commentary**—If there is friendship between the month-segments <note>term: "khaṇḍa" — a section or figure within the divination grid</note> and the elements <note>term: "tattva" — refers to the four elements: Earth, Air, Fire, and Water</note>, then it should be known that the results for that month will be auspicious. Similarly, friendship between the month-segment and the figures <note>term: "śakala" — one of the 16 geomantic symbols</note> belonging to its owner also results in auspiciousness. If there is no friendship between them but rather enmity, one should expect undesirable results. || 15 || In the same manner, those who know <term>Ramala</term> <note>a method of divination using dice or marks on sand, originally of Arabic origin (Raml) and adapted into Sanskrit traditions</note> should state the results based on the friendship or enmity of the repeated houses. From the mutual meeting of the repeated segment and the month-segment, if an auspicious figure is formed, the result is auspicious; if inauspicious, it produces inauspicious results. || 16 || From the friendship of the month-segment and the witness-segment <note>original: "sākṣi-khaṇḍa"</note>, all results are auspicious; if there is opposition (enmity), they are inauspicious; and in the case of neutrality, the result is middling. || 17 || One should understand the witness-segment in the same way as the month-segment. One should perform the previously mentioned method for the owner of the segment and the other segments as well; then, one should state the mentioned results accordingly. || 18 || 19 || One should state the results of this according to the previous method of the Witness. In this manner, one should know the results within a month through these four mentioned segments. || 20 ||

Now, I shall duly explain the major time-periods <note>term: "daśā" — a period of time during which a specific planetary or geomantic influence dominates</note> and the subtle sub-periods <note>term: "sūkṣma-daśā"</note> as well. These time-periods are dependent on the **Sabita** <note>original: "sābitam" — derived from the Arabic "Thābit," meaning a fixed, confirmed, or valid chart configuration</note>; therefore, the *Sabita* must be established first. || 21 || I shall describe that method according to the ancient scriptures. From the chart <note>term: "prastāra" — the 16-house geomantic spread</note> generated by the dice, using the parts named *Vishvakarman* and *Shakra*... || 22 || the wise practitioner should construct the chart with four parts as before. If the tenth and first figures are found in the thirteenth and eleventh houses... || 23 || then that should be known by the wise as a **Sabita** chart. If it is not so, then again from those four, beginning with the *Vishva*... || 24 || [construct] the chart as before...

<page-num>१११</page-num>
<header>The Ninth Question 9. ]</header>

...having done so, one should observe the result there. This process should be repeated until the **Fixed** <note>original: "sābita" — from the Arabic *thābit*, meaning a stable or verified configuration of the geomantic chart</note> state is achieved. || 25 ||

**Commentary**—Now, I shall explain the method for determining **Major Time-Periods** <term>Dasha</term> and **Sub-periods** <term>Sukshma-dasha</term> in accordance with the rules. These periods are dependent upon the "Fixed" state; therefore, one must first establish the "Fixed" grid. Following the methods of the ancient scriptures: after casting the dice, the resulting **grid** <term>prastāra</term> is formed. One should take the 13th, 10th, 11th, and 14th **segments** <note>term: "khaṇḍa" — the individual houses or positions in the geomantic square</note> of that grid and place them into the first four positions (1, 2, 3, and 4) to create a new grid. 

In this new arrangement, if the new 13th segment is identical to the original 1st, the new 10th is identical to the 2nd, the new 11th to the 3rd, and the new 14th to the 4th, then the learned scholars recognize this grid as **Fixed**. This is the defining characteristic of a "Fixed" configuration. If the "Fixed" state is not achieved in the first grid, one should then take segments 13, 14, 15, and 16 and place them in the first four positions to create yet another grid. One should continue this process <insert>using the 13th, 14th, 15th, and 16th segments</insert> until the "Fixed" state is finally attained. || 21-25 ||

The "Fixed" state never goes beyond the sixth iteration; it occurs within the first six. Prosperity and wealth are found in the first; gain in the second; and happiness is the medium result in the third. || 26 ||
Suffering and even death are to be known if the "Fixed" state occurs at the end of the six iterations, respectively. This sequence of the "Fixed" state should always be understood by the wise. || 27 ||

**Commentary**—By following this method, the "Fixed" state will certainly appear within six iterations; one does not need to create more than six grids to achieve it. Therefore, the results for these six possibilities are described: 

1. If the "Fixed" state appears in the **first grid**, there will be happiness and wealth. 
2. If it appears in the **second**, there will be profit or gain. 
3. If it appears in the **third**, there will be happiness. 
4. If it appears in the **fourth**, the results will be average or mediocre. 
5. If it appears in the **fifth**, one will encounter suffering. 
6. If it appears in the **sixth**, it signifies death. 

The learned should always understand the sequence of results for the "Fixed" state in this manner. || 26 || 27 ||

<summary>This page explains the technical process of "Sabita" (fixing the chart), an iterative method used to verify the divination grid and determine the timing of Major and Sub-periods. The number of iterations required to reach this state dictates whether the outcome will be prosperous, painful, or even fatal.</summary>
<keywords>Sabita (Fixed state), Dasha (Time-period), Prastara (Divination grid), Khanda (Segment), Ramala calculation, divination outcomes</keywords>

<page-num>११२</page-num>
<header>[ Ramala Navaratna -</header>

One should divide the measure of a year in days by the number [of iterations taken to reach] the **Fixed Grid**. The results obtained for the grids are declared as the **Major Time-Periods** in sequential order. || 28 ||

That Major Period, when divided by twelve, results in what is called the **Subtle Period**. The fruit [prediction] for each **geomantic figure** should then be given according to the duration of these subtle periods. || 29 ||

This should be understood through the previously mentioned method of monthly segments. If the **Fixed** state <note>original: "sābita" — the stable configuration</note> occurs in the first grid, then the first period provides the result. || 30 ||

Thus, the wise should understand the first, second, and subsequent periods. In this way, the results of the periods are to be spoken. Now, I shall declare another method. || 31 ||

**Commentary**—Divide the yearly measure of 360 days by the number of the **Fixed Grid** <note>original: "sthira prastāra" — the specific grid where the stable "Sabita" configuration was found</note>. The resulting number is considered the **Major Time-Period** <term>Dashā</term> of that grid. || 28 || 

By dividing that Major Period by 12, the **Subtle Period** <term>Sūkshma Dashā</term> is determined. Then, one should state the results for each **geomantic figure** <term>shakala</term> according to the sequence of these subtle periods, as previously described. || 29 || 

One must predict the results for a specific month according to the segment belonging to that month. If the "Fixed" state <note>original: "sābita"</note> is achieved in the first grid, the result pertains to the first period; if in the second grid, it pertains to the second, and so on in that order. 

As an example: if the Fixed state appears in the fourth grid, then divide the yearly measure of 360 days by 4. The quotient is 90. Thus, each grid represents 90 days. The results for the entire year should be explained across these four 90-day divisions. 

For the **Sub-period** <term>Antardashā</term>, the method is as follows: divide the aforementioned 90-day period by 12. This results in sub-periods of 7 days and 30 **ghatis** <note>original: "ghaṭī" — a traditional unit of time; 60 ghatis make one day, so 30 ghatis is half a day</note> each. One should state the result for each individual geomantic figure lasting 7 days and 30 ghatis. 

In this manner, use the first grid for the first 90 days, the second grid for the second 90 days, the third for the third 90, and the fourth for the final 90 days. One should declare the results for exactly as many periods as the number used to divide 360. In this Ramala system, this number never exceeds six. This describes the **Solar Return** <term>Varshapravesha</term>...

The year is reckoned from the **Aries Ingress** <note>original: "Mesha Sankranti" — the moment the Sun enters the first sign of the zodiac, marking the solar new year</note>; one should also understand the entire year from that same ingress. **Commentary**—The **geomantic figure** <term>shakala</term> produced from the monthly segment and the period segment is called the "Result-Namer." According to that, clever astrologers should understand the results of the **Subtle Period** <term>Sūkshma Dashā</term>. For example, as previously mentioned in the grid arising from the casting of the first month, if one wishes to see the sub-period, they should first examine the figure of the **Fixed** <note>original: "sābita" — the stable configuration</note> state. There, one should combine the figures that are present; depending on whether that resulting figure is auspicious or inauspicious, the result should be declared. || 32 ||

If that figure is auspicious, repeated in an auspicious house, and strong, then an auspicious result should be stated. If it is inauspicious, in an inauspicious house, and weak, then an inauspicious result should be stated. || 33 ||

One should declare the results in the sequence of the houses—Body (1st), Wealth (2nd), and so on—as per the previously mentioned house meanings. Based on the types such as **Outgoing** <note>original: "khārij" — a figure indicating movement away or loss</note>, one should speak of departures. For instance, if the figure is **Outgoing**, it indicates a decrease; if it is **Incoming** <note>original: "dākhil" — a figure indicating gain or arrival</note>, it indicates an increase. In this way, the scholar should state every result after full consideration. || 32–34 ||

By combining the figures from the fifteenth and seventh houses, and the tenth and eighth, and creating four segments || 36 || from those four, two are made, and from those two, one is produced; this becomes the Lord of the Year for a period of five years. <note>The Sanskrit "tithi-shailo" refers to 15 and 7, while "dasha-ashtau" refers to 10 and 8. These are traditional numerological codes where 'Tithi' means 15 days of a lunar fortnight and 'Shaila' means the 7 mountains.</note> The expert should announce the results according to the methods previously explained. || 37 || Through the variations of "Outgoing" and other movements, the arrival and other events should be determined. **Commentary**—Now, I shall describe the method that creates supreme wonder for all living beings, through which the results for the year become clear. In the grid named **Sabita** <note>original: "sābita" — the fixed or foundational geomantic arrangement</note>, one should combine the **geomantic figures** <term>shakals</term> of houses 1 and 13, 4 and 14, 15 and 7, and 10 and 8 to create 4 new figures. From these 4, one should derive 2, and from those 2, make 1. The figure that emerges is the **Lord of the Year** <term>Varshesha</term>. The clever astrologer should announce the results according to the paths previously described. Using its variations such as **Khariz** <note>original: "khārija" — an 'outgoing' figure indicating movement away or expenditures</note> or movements like "arrival and departure," and through repeated analysis, the scholar should explain those various outcomes. || 36-38 ||

->Now, the Method for the Months.<-

If there is a desire to know the monthly results here, then from the four segments arising from the previously mentioned **Sabita** grid || 39 || the wise one should create a new grid using the previously mentioned halves. By making one figure from these two, that becomes the lord of the first month. || 40 ||

One should declare the results for the first month using this figure as before. Then, using the four segments produced from the first month || 41 || a grid for the second month is made, and from that, the third. One should proceed in this manner repeatedly until twelve months are reached. || 42 || The wise person should then declare the results of those months as previously explained. || 43 ||

**Commentary**—Now the method for the **Lord of the Month** <term>Māsesha</term> is described. If you wish to know the results for the months, then take the four segments from houses 1, 13, 4, and 14 of the **Sabita** grid...

Create a **grid** <note>original: "prastāra" — an arrangement or spread of geomantic figures</note> from the segments of houses 15, 7, 10, and 8. From the four segments of this grid, take one **geomantic figure** <term>shakal</term> in the order previously described. Using the method mentioned before, announce the result for the first month. In the same way, from the four segments of the first month’s grid, create a grid for the second month. From that, use the same method for the third month; from the third, the fourth. By repeating this process, create the 1, 2 [etc.] grids for all twelve months. From these, the scholar should announce the monthly results according to the sequence of Outgoing <note>original: "khārija" — a figure indicating movement away or external results</note> and other variations. || 39-43 ||

> One desiring the result for a specific day should create a grid for the first day using the four segments mentioned previously from the first month. Thus, one should contemplate the result of the segments throughout the twelve months. || 46 ||

**Commentary**—Beyond the monthly results, one desiring the daily results should create a **grid** from the four segments mentioned earlier. This will be for the first day. From that, one extracts the **Lord of the Day Segment** <term>dinesha khanda</term> for the second, third, fourth, and so on. Just as one month leads to the next, as described before, one must follow the same method for each day until all 30 days are clarified. From the four segments of the resulting grid, one must derive two, and then one. || 44 || 45 || By doing this, the daily results for those months are derived from the daily segments by analyzing the previously mentioned variations such as Outgoing <note>original: "khārijādi" — referring to the four states: Outgoing, Incoming, Fixed, and Changing</note>. In this manner, the results for the 12 months of the year should be considered. || 46 ||

# Now, the Method for Predicting the Year for the Whole World.

When the expert in **Ramala** <note>original: "ramlavit" — one who knows the art of geomancy</note> makes up his mind to speak on the fruits of the entire world, || 47 ||
then, at the previously mentioned time, the wise one should cast the **dice** <term>pashaka</term> and condense the results.
He should perform the prior ritual there until he determines the **Lord of the Year** <term>varshapa</term>. || 48 ||
Having established the Lord of the Year, he should announce the results as they are described hereafter.

**Commentary**—Now, the method for determining the year for the entire world is explained. When the geomancer desires to predict the auspicious and inauspicious results for the whole world for the duration of a year, the scholar should cast the dice on the day of the **Aries Equinox** <note>original: "Mesha Sankranti" — the moment the sun enters the sign of Aries, marking the traditional solar new year</note>. || 47 || From that, using the previously described method, one specific **geomantic figure** <term>shakal</term> is extracted from the four primary figures to serve as the Lord of the Year. The results for the year, which are spoken of next, should then be declared. || 48 ||

# Now, the Results of the Segments for the Lord of the Year.

This is the result according to the **Tortoise Chart** <note>original: "Kurmatah" — referring to the Kurma-chakra, a traditional Hindu astrological map that overlays a tortoise shape onto the geography of the world/India</note>.

If the segment is **Kanjuladakhila** <insert>geomantic symbol</insert> <note>original: "Kanjuladākhila" — corresponds to the figure Acquisitio (Gain)</note>, then men will see profits from trade in many ways. Through merit, they shall easily enjoy eating fruits that are like nectar and free from disease. || 50 || Kings will be devoted to justice and the protection of their subjects; the world will be healthy and fearless, and there will be an increase in excellent goods. According to the **Tortoise Chart**, this result will manifest in the **East**.

**Commentary**—The specific results for each figure when they act as the "Year-Lord" <term>varshesha shakal</term> are now explained. If the figure **Lahyana** is the Year-Lord, all people will remain constantly happy, diligent in their respective traditions, and inclined toward good works. Day after day, there will be a continuous growth in farming and food supplies. Despite minor threats of pests or weather...

Market rates will shift, grains will be sold cheaply, and wealth will be gathered. Auspicious events will occur repeatedly in the world. Kings will perform many industrious and virtuous deeds. There will be great prosperity. There will be an increase in **jewel-examiners** <note>original: "parīkṣaka"—specialists who value gemstones</note> and **secret lovers** <note>original: "jāra"—paramours or those involved in illicit affairs</note>. One should specifically know these results for the countries of Kabul, Rome, and Egypt, and for the **Western direction** of the Tortoise Diagram. || 53 ||

# Prediction for Uqla <insert>÷</insert>
<note>original: "Ukkā"—corresponds to the geomantic figure Cauda Draconis (The Dragon's Tail)</note>

If the figure <term>Shakala</term> <note>geomantic sign</note> is **Uqla**, men will experience great distress. Rainfall will be scarce, and winds will blow excessively. The price of grain and other goods will become very high, and people will remain anxious. There will be diseases, or perhaps trouble caused by **sorcery and deception** <note>original: "kuhaka"</note>, and the King will act unrighteously. Furthermore, in the **Middle Country** <note>original: "Madhyadesha"—the central plains of India</note>, kings will be very powerful. When this figure appears, there will be **earthquakes** <note>original: "bhūmicālana"</note> beyond the central region of the Tortoise. || 54 ||

**Commentary—** If **Uqla** <insert>÷</insert> is the figure, men will face much suffering. There will be little rain and high winds. The price of grain will rise, or people will remain in a state of agitation. Disease will spread, and there will be disturbances from **slaves and servants**. The King will commit injustices. The kings of the Middle Country will remain powerful, and there will be earthquakes. These results should be understood for the **Central region** of the Tortoise Diagram. || 54 ||

# Prediction for Ankis <insert>≡</insert>
<note>original: "Ankīś"—corresponds to the geomantic figure Caput Draconis (The Dragon's Head)</note>

If **Ankis** is the Lord, there will be scant rain and the accumulation of wealth will be unsuccessful. Diseases will increase among men. There will be difficulty in completing any task. Kings will fall under the control of their enemies and their minds will be filled with anxiety. Grain will become very expensive. Wicked-minded people will find happiness, while there will be a decline of noble and excellent men. These results apply entirely to the **West** of the Tortoise; in other countries, the effect will be minimal. || 55 ||

<vocab>Kurma Chakra, Farah, Ukka, Ankis, Shakala, Varsha-isha, Kabul, Rome, Mishra</vocab>

<summary>This page details the astrological forecasts for three geomantic figures: Farah (predicting prosperity in the West), Uqla (predicting distress and earthquakes in Central regions), and Ankis (predicting hardship and political anxiety in the West).</summary>

<header>The Ninth Gem 9. ] ( 114 )</header>

If **Humra** <insert>☵</insert> is known, there is sparse rain, loss of accumulated wealth, and the determination of theft and raids. There is a lack of courage, the movement of fierce winds, and the rulers follow wrong paths, leading to the death of men. Deeds are difficult for men to accomplish, yet there is an abundance of fruit on the trees. Men attain wealth and joy, and the craftsmen are filled with happiness. This result is found in the **direction of Fire** <note>original: "dahana diśi"—the Southeast</note> and also in the **Middle** according to the Tortoise. || 56 ||

**Commentary—** If the figure **Ankis** <insert>☴</insert> appears, then rainfall will be low. The clouds may remain very thick but will be wasted <note>they will pass without raining</note>. Diseases will increase among men. There will be difficulty in the completion of tasks. Kings will remain agitated in heart, falling under the control of their enemies. The price of grain will become expensive. Men of wicked intellect will find happiness, while the best of men will perish. This entire result occurs especially in the **Eastern** lands of the <term>Kurma Chakra</term> <note>A traditional map resembling a tortoise used to assign astrological influences to specific regions of India</note> and will be minimal in other countries. || 55 ||

If the figure is **Humra** <insert>☵</insert>, rainfall will be scant. Investigations into thefts will continue in various places, and thieves will wander about fearlessly. Everyone’s patience will vanish. The winds will blow very harshly. Kings will walk the **path of vice** <note>original: "kumārga"—literally the "bad path" or immoral conduct</note>. Many men will die. It will become difficult for men to perform their work. Trees will bear much fruit, and men will also experience the gain of wealth and joy. The <term>Karu</term> <note>original: "kārū"—referring to craftsmen, artisans, and royal architects/builders</note> will remain happy. This result should be understood for the **Southeast** direction of the Tortoise Diagram and also for the **Middle** country. || 56 ||

If it is **Bayaz** <insert>☴</insert>, men who have traveled abroad will have their tasks completed and be filled with joy. Those blinded by wealth <note>The commentary interprets this as moneylenders/bankers</note>, students of the science of ethics, and those whose livelihood depends on water will see constant growth. There will be the eating of sweetmeats, kings will be delighted, and there will be excessive rain. These people will be constantly filled with happiness; such is the result declared for the **Northwest** <note>original: "vāyavīye"—the direction of the wind god, Vayu</note> according to the Tortoise. || 57 ||

**Commentary—** If **Bayaz** <insert>☴</insert> occurs, the tasks of men who have gone to foreign lands will be successful and they will remain happy. Also, those who live on the interest of wealth (**bankers**), those who study the science of ethics, and those who earn a lifelong living through water-based labor such as boats and ships...

This result should be considered complete in **Samarkand, Bukhara, Nishapur**, and the **Eastern** direction of the Tortoise Diagram. || 58 || 

If the <term>Shakal</term> <note>A geomantic figure or sign</note> is **Nusrat al-Dakhil**, there will be the joy of grain. The people will remain happy, wealth will increase, and there will be an increase in water. Trees will bear fruit and wealth as desired. Crops will be abundant, and men will remain fearless. There will be prosperity of wealth through vigorous deeds. Dharma <note>Righteousness or religious duty</note> will grow, the population will increase, and the policy among kings will be good. Everywhere there will be (auspicious) bliss. This result is to be known specifically in the countries of **Turkestan, Nishapur, and Samarkand**, and in the **North** of the Tortoise Diagram. || 59 ||

> If the figure is **Atabat al-Kharij** <note>original: "Atavakharijam" — The figure representing 'The Outer Threshold' (usually associated with the South Node/Cauda Draconis)</note>, there are very harsh winds, diseases born of heat, and an excess of both cold and heat. There is little wealth among the people and sparse rain...

<header>Chapter Nine] ( 121 )</header>

**Harsh speech. The fruits of service are lost; the people, sullied by the king's anger, are afflicted. People shall sink into shallow water, and there is fear of wells and lions <note>original: "hari" — can mean lion, monkey, or even a thief in this context</note> in the center of the Tortoise. || 60 || Rain is very sparse; long echoes of thunder and the falling of lightning occur. High prices for grain and the threat of killing, as well as fear of thieves, shall prevail. There is despair, the enemies of kings increase, the health of the populace is poor, and anxiety reigns. These results for Turkestan and the Jangaval region <note>A historical regional designation, likely referring to the forested or wild borderlands of the northwest</note> occur in the North of the Tortoise. || 61 ||**

Commentary—If the figure **Ataba al-Kharij** <note>original: "Atavekhārija" — The geomantic figure representing 'The Threshold Out' or 'Loss' (Ammissio)</note> <insert>⸫</insert> appears, then fierce winds will blow. Diseases will arise from the heat. There will be an excess of both cold and heat. The prosperity of the people will diminish. Rain will be scarce. Harsh speech will increase, and service to superiors will be fruitless. The subjects will be demoralized by the royal treasury and remain fearful. In shallow ponds and wells, objects will sink. There will be fear of snakes and similar creatures. These results should be understood to apply to the **Middle Country** <note>Madhya-desha, the central plains of India</note> of the <term>Kurma Chakra</term> <note>The 'Tortoise Diagram' used for regional astrological forecasting</note>. || 60 || If the figure **al-Naqi** <note>original: "Nakkī" — The geomantic figure 'The Pure' (Acquisitio), representing gain</note> <insert>⸫</insert> appears, then rain will be slight. There will be great sounds from the clouds. Lightning will fall frequently. The price of grain will be high <note>indicating inflation or scarcity</note>. There will be fear of dacoits and thieves. Despair will take hold. The enemies of kings will increase. The health of men will not be good, and anxiety will persist. This result will be particularly prominent in **Turkestan**, the **Jangaval** country, and in the **North** of the Tortoise diagram. || 61 ||

**Everything becomes like the beginning <insert>⸫</insert>; there is great joy among the people, and the kings and the earth are magnificent. The land is flooded with water, and there is an increase in the transport of wealth and grain. Vines and trees are filled with nectar-like fruit, and there is excessive rain. This result occurs in the West direction of the Tortoise, specifically in Rum <note>original: "Rumaka" — Historically referring to the Byzantine Empire, Rome, or the Ottoman territories</note> and Syria <note>original: "Shāma"</note>. || 62 || There is an increase in the joy of jewels, ministers, perfumes, and scribes. Sovereignty over the earth is established among kings, yet the servant classes suffer and struggle day and night. This is the result of the figure named Ijtima <note>original: "Ijtamākhya" — The geomantic figure 'The Union' (Conjunctio)</note> <insert>⸫</insert>, bringing profit through good deeds on earth...**

<header>[ The Nine Gems of Ramal—</header>

**...the kings will be endowed [with these traits]; the fruits are abundant in the North and West of the Tortoise. || 63 || If The Path <term>Tariq</term> <note>original: "Tarīka" — The geomantic figure consisting of four single dots, representing a journey or flow</note> appears: there will be peerless rain and wind. People in the cities will be workers of sin, slanderers, and messengers of lies. Merchants will possess great wealth and see much growth, but there will be wandering <note>or trembling/instability</note> of the earth. This fruit occurs in the Northwest <note>original: "Anila-dik" — the direction of the Wind god, Vayu</note> and the North of the Tortoise. || 64 ||**

**Commentary**—If the figure **The Inward Threshold** <note>original: "Atavedākhila" — derived from the Arabic *Ataba ad-Dakhil*, the figure of 'Entrance' or 'Acquisition'</note> <term>⁝</term> appears, then men will remain happy. The King’s prosperity and power will increase. The earth will be "enjoyed by water," which is to say there will be excellent rainfall. There will be a great abundance of wealth and grain. Trees and vines will be laden with fruit as sweet as nectar. These results should be understood specifically for the Eastern direction of the Tortoise Diagram, as well as the regions of Rum <note>Modern-day Turkey/Anatolia</note> and Syria <note>original: "Shama-desha"</note>. || 62 || 

If the figure **The Conjunction** <note>original: "Ijattama" — derived from the Arabic *Ijtima*, the figure of 'Union' or 'Assembly'</note> <term>⁝</term> appears, then ministers <note>original: "Rata/Amatya"</note> and viziers who earn their livelihood through the writing of fragrant matters <note>possibly meaning diplomatic or courtly literature</note> will achieve great status and growth upon the earth. Sovereignty among kings will increase, but the servant class will suffer constant hardships. Auspicious works will be undertaken on earth, the benefits of which will be reaped by the rulers. These results are to be known specifically for the North and West directions of the Tortoise Diagram. || 63 || 

If the figure **The Path** <note>original: "Tarīka" — *Tariq*</note> <term>⁝</term> appears, there will be excessive rain and strong winds. The inhabitants of the cities will become thieves and commit sinful deeds. Messengers will speak lies. The prosperity of the merchant class will grow significantly. The earth will tremble <note>referring to earthquakes or geographical instability</note>. These results are to be known specifically for the Southeast <note>original: "Agneya" — the direction of the Fire god, Agni</note> and North directions of the Tortoise Diagram. || 64 ||

**I have spoken these results for the Lord of the Year in the science of Ramal <note>Geomancy</note>; one should also apply their own intellect to them. Whatever is found to be impure or of a censurable meaning should be corrected by wise men, setting aside any prejudice. || 65 || I do not know the proper division of words, their meanings, the grammar of compounds, the sequence of ideas, etymology, or the ornaments of rhetoric. Nevertheless, O noble ones, you who are always intent on affection: please forgive my child-like efforts. || 66 || [Gathering] the essence of the ocean of Ramal by the best of the noble...**

It ends with a traditional poetic colophon where the author asks for scholarly indulgence for any errors.</summary>

<page-num>123</page-num>
<header>9. The Nine Gems</header>

...even from what has been gathered, I have taken the essence of the essence. || I have created this work named the **Nine Gems** <note>original: "Navaratna"</note>; the wise should reflect upon it constantly and with great effort. || 67 ||

**Commentary**—The author states: I have described the results pertaining to the Lord of the Year. In this science of **Geomancy** <term>Ramal</term> <note>A system of divination traditionally performed by making marks in the sand, which traveled from the Islamic world into India</note>, humans must also use their own intellect and logic when speaking. If there are any inaccuracies or low meanings in this book, the learned scholars should set aside anger and resentment to correct them. || 65 || I know nothing of the division of words, the meaning of terms, grammatical compounds, etymology, the true essence of things, or even figures of speech. || 66 || Nevertheless, may the great and noble people always forgive my childishness. This book is the essence of the ocean of Geomancy that I have distilled; taking the very essence of the essence from the great scriptures, I have titled it the **Nine Gems**. Let the intelligent reflect upon this repeatedly with diligence. || 67 ||

### Description of the Author's Lineage

In the land of the **Yadu-Vraja** <note>Modern-day Vrindavan/Mathura region, associated with the clan of Krishna</note>, where the wise remember the son of Nanda <note>Krishna</note> and where the thick groves of Vrindavan exist, there lived one who was devoted to the service of **The One who Wanders in the Groves** <note>original: "Kunja-Vihari," an epithet for Krishna</note>. Honored by the lords of the earth, he was a **religious leader** <term>Goswami</term> named **Lalitaprasad**, an ornament among the virtuous. || 68 ||

His son was **Madanmohana**, a devotee of the Lord, who lived in the city of **Narnaul** and was honored by the community of **scribes and administrators** <term>Kayastha</term>. His son was the wise **Chandralal**, whose feet were honored by the learned. He was a sun among astrologers, his mind was deeply attached to knowledge, and he dispelled internal darkness through the **Three Vedas** <term>Trayi</term>. || 69 ||

Among his sons, the youngest of six, peerless in age, wealth, and virtue, was one whose mind was adorned with and prepared for grammar, rhetoric, and poetry, and who was a servant of the virtuous. This very man, the successful **Shri Rangalal**, having been requested by the best of the twice-born <note>Brahmins</note>, composed this pure **Nine Gems** for the pleasure of the virtuous. || 70 ||

<vocab>Ramal, Navaratna, lineage description, Vrindavan, Narnaul, Shri Rangalal, Kayastha, Goswami, Lalitaprasad, Madanmohan, Shrichandralal</vocab>

<summary>The author, Rangalal, concludes his work on geomancy by asking for scholarly forgiveness for any errors and provides a detailed genealogy tracing his family from Vrindavan to Narnaul.</summary>
<keywords>Rangalal, genealogy, Narnaul, Vrindavan, Ramala Shastra, Nine Gems, book conclusion, astrological lineage</keywords>

<page-num>134</page-num>
<header>[ The Nine Gems of Geomancy --</header>

> -> In the year measured by the elephants (8), the fires (3), the nines (9), and the moon (1) <note>Vikram Samvat 1938, roughly 1882 CE</note>, in the month of Magha, during the bright fortnight, on the thirteenth lunar day, on a Tuesday, this **Nine Gems of Geomancy** reached its completion. May it forever shine for the learned. || 71 || <-

## Thus ends the Ninth Gem, named the Description of the Results of the Year, in the "Nine Gems of Geomancy." || 9 ||

**Commentary**—The land of **Vraja** <note>The sacred region around Mathura and Vrindavan</note> is like the very neck of the Earth, where the inhabitants remain free from sin by meditating on the lotus feet of Shri Krishna. In this land, those of noble intellect constantly remember Krishna, the son of Nanda. Within its forests lies the beautiful **Vrindavan**, where there lived a man famous by the name of **Lalita Prasad**. He was highly honored by the local King, known for his beauty among virtuous men, and was ever-intent on the service of Shri Krishna, the "Wanderer of the Groves." || 68 || His son, a devotee of the Lord named **Madan Mohan**, went to reside in the city of **Narnol**, where he was revered by the **Kayastha** <note>A community traditionally serving as administrators and scribes</note> community. His son was the wise **Shri Chandralal**, whose feet were honored by scholars. His mind was deeply immersed in the science of the Sun (astrology) and was purified by the knowledge of the three Vedas. || 69 || Among his six sons, the youngest in age did not equal the others in wealth or virtues; nevertheless, his heart was adorned with the study of words, poetic ornaments, and literature. Remaining a servant of the virtuous, and at the request of the best of the Brahmins, this **Ranglal Pandit** composed this **Nine Gems** <note>original: "Navaratna"</note> in beautiful, pure verses for the pleasure of noble people. || 70 || On Tuesday, the thirteenth day of the bright half of the month of Magha, in the **Vikram Samvat** <note>A traditional Hindu calendar era</note> year 1938, this **Geomancy of Nine Gems** was completed. May the wise ones grant it beauty and grace time and again. || 71 ||

## Thus ends the Ninth Gem, named the Description of the Year-Inquiry, in the Mahidhari dialect of the "Nine Gems of Geomancy." || 9 ||

In the year measured by the Vedas (4), the arrows (5), the nines (9), and the earth (1) <note>Vikram Samvat 1954, roughly 1897 CE, likely referring to the date of this specific edition or revision</note>, in Delhi, this work was revised. **Mahidhara** performed this revision under the command of the King of Kings, **Khemraj** <note>Likely the famous publisher Khemraj Shrikrishnadass</note>, creating this new, singular gem of **Geomancy** <term>Ramal</term>. By correcting the pure and impure parts, this book has attained a new life; may the author be happy. Because this science is famously **Persian** <note>original: "Paarasiyah"</note> in origin, success in the inquiry is achieved through this language as well.

<summary>This final page provides the author's genealogy and the completion date of the manuscript (1882 CE), while acknowledging the Persian roots of the geomantic system and its translation into the local dialect for wider accessibility.</summary>
<keywords>colophon, Ranglal Pandit, Vrindavan, Narnol, astrology, Persian geomancy, Vikram Samvat 1938, Khemraj Shrikrishnadass</keywords>

**The Method for Obtaining the Answer** || Make marks <note>usually dots in the sand or on paper</note> in four (4) rows, whether they be fewer or more than the circles shown above. When the marks are made by the querent...

<summary>This page features a divination grid used in Ramal (Persian geomancy) that maps sixteen standard life questions (e.g., health, travel, marriage) to the sixteen geomantic figures. It uses a Sanskrit vowel-shifting cipher as a lookup mechanism for determining outcomes.</summary>
<keywords>Ramal, geomancy, divination chart, Prashnavali, Arabic astrology in India, Sanskrit vowels, geomantic figures, oracle</keywords>

--- Page 134 ---
<meta>This page provides the practical instructions for using the Ramal Oracle Chart introduced on the previous page. It explains the method of generating a geomantic figure through randomized counting and matching that figure to the grid to find answers, while also listing specific auspicious dates for consultation.</meta>

<page-num>136</page-num>
<header>[ Ramal— </header>

If dots are marked, they should be counted from right to left (that is, from the right-hand side toward the left-hand side). If the count is <term>visham</term><note>original: "visham"; an odd number</note>, mark a single dot ( . ). If the count is <term>sam</term><note>original: "sam"; an even number</note>, mark a line ( — ). Collect these marks for all four rows to form a <term>shakal</term><note>original: "shakal"; a geomantic figure or sign</note>. This will result in a figure similar to a <term>pashak</term><note>original: "pashak"; a die used in divination</note>. It should also be remembered that if the count in any row exceeds 9, one must apply <term>tasht</term><note>original: "tasht"; the process of finding a remainder by casting out or dividing</note> by 9. Use the remaining number to determine if it is even or odd.

### ॥ Example of the Above Marks ॥

| Description | Count of Dots | Nature |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| First Row | ० ० ० ० ० ० ० ० ० ० ० | Odd |
| Second Row | ० ० ० ० ० ० ० ० ० ० ० ० ० ० | Even |
| Third Row | ० ० ० ० ० ० ० ० ० ० ० ० ० | Odd |
| Fourth Row | ० ० ० ० ० ० ० ० ० ० ० ० ० ० | Even |

Make the marks accordingly—then, to find the answer, look at the <term>jantri</term><note>original: "jantri"; a systematic table or almanac</note>. Find the figure at the head of the chart that matches the one obtained through the four-row method described above. Then, look at the letter-mark corresponding to the head of your specific question. Finally, look at the <term>kosthak</term><note>original: "kosthak"; the cell or bracket in a grid</note> where that specific letter and that figure intersect. There you will find the answer to your question.

### Days for Asking Questions

January: 1, 2, 4, 6, 11, 12, 20 | February: 1, 17, 18 | March: 14, 16 | April: 10, 17, 18 | May: 7, 8 | June: 17 | July: 17, 21 | August: 20, 21 | September: 10, 18 | October: 6 | November: 6, 10 | December: 6, 11, 15.

The same question should not be asked a second time on the same day. = Finis ॥

<summary>This page describes the technical process of generating geomantic figures by counting dots and reducing them to binary states (odd or even) and provides a calendar of specific dates considered suitable for divination.</summary>
<keywords>Ramal, geomancy, divination method, auspicious dates, dot counting, odd and even, oracle instructions</keywords>

### A.

1 ⋮ Whatever your desire is, it will be fulfilled in a few days.
2 = There will be suffering and <term>pashchattap</term><note>original: "पश्चात्ताप"; remorse or regret</note>.
3 ⁞ Whatever work you do today, do it with <term>khabardari</term><note>original: "खबरदारी"; extreme caution or vigilance</note>, lest some <term>aapatti</term><note>original: "आपत्ति"; calamity or misfortune</note> befall you.
4 - . The prisoner will die, and his friends will grieve.
5 ≡ For now, the body will be saved; though there were preparations for a meeting with <term>mrityu</term><note>original: "मृत्यु"; death</note>, the body will survive this suffering. In the next instance, there is no hope.
6 = . You will find a <term>rupvati</term><note>original: "रूपवती"; beautiful or comely</note> girl, but one who is full of suffering.
7 ⋮ You (or your spouse) will meet a <term>dharmatma</term><note>original: "धर्मात्मा"; a virtuous or righteous soul</note> and <term>dharmajnya</term><note>original: "धर्मज्ञ"; one who knows the religious laws</note> man or woman.
8 - .. If you enter into a <term>vivah</term><note>original: "विवाह"; marriage</note> with this person, then enemies will arise wherever you issue a command.
9 .. - It is better that you abandon this <term>priti</term><note>original: "प्रीति"; affection or love</note>, because it is neither permanent nor is it true.
10 . - Abandon your <term>yatra</term><note>original: "यात्रा"; journey</note>, because you will gain no benefit from it.
11 = There is true <term>maitri</term><note>original: "मैत्री"; friendship</note> between you.
12 = : You will not find the stolen wealth.
13 ≡ The foreigner will return soon and with joy.
14 ⁞ You will not depart from where you are at this moment; that is, you will remain stationary.
15 - . You will receive help from the <term>hakim</term><note>original: "हाकिम"; a judge or high official</note> in a favorable legal case.
16 ≡ You are not fortunate; you are devoid of favorable <term>prarabdha</term><note>original: "प्रारब्ध"; destiny or accumulated fate</note>. Seek help from <term>parameshwar</term><note>original: "परमेश्वर"; the Supreme Lord</note>.
