# A.

1 ⁝ : Whatever has been written in your <term>prarabdha</term><note>original: "प्रारब्ध"; the portion of one's destiny or karma to be experienced in the present life</note>, people will look upon it with <gloss>envy</gloss> and malice.

4 -.- : Friendship will be established between them.

->( Ḷ )<-
<note>This section corresponds to the Sanskrit vowel 'Ḷ' (ऌ).</note>

<meta>This page transitions from the list of specific divinatory answers seen on the previous page to the technical instructions for casting a geomantic (Ramal) chart. The previous page concluded a questionnaire; this page begins a manual titled "Ramal Daniyal," providing the mathematical and symbolic rules for determining timing, wealth, and travel.</meta>

## Obeisance to Lord Ganesha
# Now begins the Geomancy of Daniel
---
### Now, questions regarding the duration of a task.

If someone asks: “In how many days and on which day of the week will my work be completed?”
Then, one must draw sixteen <term>shakal</term> <note>Geomantic figures composed of four lines of dots or bars.</note>. Multiply <term>jarb</term> <note>A technical process in geomancy where two figures are combined to create a third.</note> the first and the fifth figures; multiply the second and the sixth; multiply the third and the seventh; and multiply the fourth and the eighth. Extract the resulting figures. Perform an inversion <term>inkilab</term> in this manner. Having extracted four figures in this way, generate a full set of sixteen figures from them. Then, perform the divination <term>amal</term> upon these. Look at the figure in the fourteenth house <term>ghar</term>; whatever number is written below it is the number of days in which the work will be completed, and the task will occur on the weekday ruled by the master of that specific figure. For example, if the fourteenth house contains the figure *Lahan* (𝌆), the work will be done in 9 days and on a Thursday <note>original: "बृहस्पातिवार" (Brihaspativar), the day of Jupiter.</note>. Thus should all such readings be given. || 1 ||

**Question regarding wealth (2):** If someone asks, “Will I have wealth or not?” then consider this question from the second house. If the figures in the first, second, and eleventh houses of the spread <term>prastara</term> are auspicious, there will be wealth; if they are inauspicious, there will not be. In this context, ⁝ 𝌆 𝌆 ⁝ ⚌ ⁝ are auspicious figures, and ⚌ ⚌ 𝌆 ⚌ ⚌ are inauspicious figures. || 2 ||

And if someone asks: “Is the place I am going to nearby <term>nikat</term> auspicious or inauspicious?” judge this question from the third house. If there is an auspicious figure in the third house, declare an auspicious result; if it is inauspicious, declare an inauspicious result. If there is a "fixed" <term>sabit</term> <note>From the Arabic 'thabit,' referring to stable or non-moving figures.</note> figure, say that the person will return back from that place. || 3 ||

If someone asks: “How will my life be from this point forward?” then judge from the first, second, and ninth houses. If "entering" <term>dakhil</term> <note>Figures that indicate inward movement or gain.</note> figures such as ⁝ ⁝ ⁝ appear there, then life will pass well; but if "exiting" <term>kharij</term> <note>Figures indicating outward movement or loss.</note> figures like ⁝ ⁝ ⚌ ⁝ are present...

<summary>This page introduces 'Ramal Daniyal,' a system of geomancy, providing technical rules for calculating the timing of events and answering questions about wealth, travel, and the general course of life using sixteen symbolic figures.</summary>
<keywords>Ramal, geomancy, Daniel, divination, astrology, weekday rulers, houses, auspicious, inauspicious, wealth, travel</keywords>

[ Ramal Daniyal -

then it will not pass well. If an auspicious fixed figure <term>savit</term> <note>original: "सावित" (from Arabic *thabit*), meaning stable or unchanging.</note> appears, then life will continue as it has in the past. If an inverting figure <term>munkaliv</term> <note>original: "मुन्कलीव" (from Arabic *munqalib*), meaning a figure that transforms or turns over.</note> appears, then it will pass with good fortune. Another method is to consider the 12th house; if it is auspicious, the present is passing well and the future will also be good. If it is inauspicious, the future will pass poorly. 

If someone asks: "How has my livelihood been? And how will it be in the future?" In that case, multiply <term>jarb</term> <note>A mathematical combination of two geomantic figures to produce a result.</note> the figures of the 1st and 4th houses, and multiply the 7th and 10th houses. If an auspicious figure is formed from these, then it is a good sign; the past has passed well. If they are inauspicious, the past has been poor and deficient <term>nakis</term>. If these two results are opposite—meaning one is auspicious and the other inauspicious—then say that the past has been average. Then, multiply the figure produced from the 1st and 4th houses with the figure produced from the 7th and 10th houses to create a single final figure. If this is inauspicious, the future will pass poorly; if auspicious, it will pass well; and if it is a neutral figure, the future will be average. || 4 ||

If someone asks, "Is my brother pleased with me or not?" then consider the third house <note>In astrology and geomancy, the third house traditionally represents siblings.</note>. If the "entering" figures <term>dakhil</term>—namely ⁚ . . ⁚ , . ⁚ ⁚ . , . ⁚ . ⁚ , or ⁚ ⁚ ⁚ ⁚—appear there, then the brother's love is very great. And if "exiting" figures <term>kharij</term>—namely ⁚ . ⁚ . , . ⁚ ⁚ ⁚ , or ⁚ ⁚ ⁚ .—appear, then there is no love, but rather enmity. If a fixed figure <term>sabit</term> appears, then he maintains some love superficially; he makes a outward show <term>ill bhall</term> <note>original: "इल्ल भल्ल", a colloquialism for a deceptive or superficial display.</note> for the sake of the public. If an inverting figure <term>munkaliv</term> appears, say that he wishes to show love only out of fear of public opinion, but there is no love in his heart. One should determine the specifics of the situation by also weighing the overall auspiciousness or inauspiciousness of the chart. || 5 ||

If someone says, "What is the nature of the woman I wish to marry?" then consider the seventh house <note>The house of partnerships and marriage.</note>. If the figures . ⁚ . ⁚ , ⁚ . . ⁚ , . ⁚ ⁚ . , or ⁚ . ⁚ . appear, then she is a devoted and virtuous wife <term>pativrata</term>. <header>### Questions on Procedures ###</header>

is sorrow-giving. If any other figure remains from these, the woman is an adulteress <term>jar</term> <note>original: "जार" (jar), meaning a paramour or unfaithful woman.</note> (unfaithful), though she is sweet in her speech. || 6 ||

If someone asks: "My item is lost; will it be found or not?" At that time, cast the geomancy <term>ramal</term>, prepare the chart <term>prastar</term> <note>The geomantic tableau or grid of figures.</note>, and extract the sixteen figures <term>shakal</term>. Then, examine the seventh figure. If it is one of the "entering" <term>dakhil</term> <note>Figures that indicate something coming in or staying.</note> figures, it will be found very quickly. If it is an "exiting" <term>kharij</term> <note>Figures indicating movement away or loss.</note> figure, it will not be found. If it is an "inverting" <term>munkalib</term> <note>Figures that represent change or instability.</note> figure, some clues or traces will be found, but the item itself will not be recovered. If it is an auspicious "fixed" <term>savit</term> <note>Stable or stationary figures.</note> figure, it will be found after a delay. If it is inauspicious, it will not be found. || 7 ||

If someone asks: "Will it rain or not?" At that time, create two charts and extract a single figure from the fifth and sixteenth figures. If that result is an "entering" <term>dakhil</term> figure, there will be much rain. If it is the figure *Jamaat* ☰ <note>The figure "The Congregation" (Arabic: *al-Jama'ah*).</note>, the rivers will rise significantly. If it is this figure ⁝, the clouds will remain for a long time. If it is this figure ⁚, there will be many dark storm clouds, but the rainfall will be slight. If it is this figure ⁛, only wind will blow and there will be no rain. If it is this figure ⁝⁝, a hot, red, dusty wind will arrive. If it is the figure *Bayaz* ☰ <note>The figure "Whiteness" (Arabic: *al-Bayad*).</note>, there will be little rain and much wind. If it is the figure *Tarikha* ⋮ <note>The figure "The Path" (Arabic: *al-Tariq*).</note>, the rain will repeatedly miss its mark or pass by, and if it is an "exiting" <term>kharij</term> figure, the rain will be greatly diverted. || 8 ||

If someone asks: "Will grain <term>ann</term> <note>Refers to food grains or cereals.</note> become expensive or cheap?" At that time, create the chart and extract the sixteen figures. Then, multiply <term>jarb</term> <note>A geomantic operation where two figures are combined to form a third.</note> the first and tenth figures; multiply the second and eleventh figures; multiply the fifth and ninth figures; and multiply the seventh and twelfth figures. In this way, produce four figures. From these four, construct sixteen figures. Then, perform an "inversion" * <term>inkilab</term> of this chart and extract four figures from it, and then combine those into a single figure. If that figure is of the "fiery" <term>atashi</term> nature...

---

* By multiplying 1 with 5, 2 with 6, 3 with 7, and 4 with 8, four figures are extracted—this is called an inversion <term>inkilab</term>.

### [ Ramal Daniyal- ###
<note>original: "रमलदानियाल" (Ramal Daniyal). This refers to a system of geomancy traditionally attributed to the Prophet Daniel, widely used across the Islamic world and India.</note>

If the element is Fire <term>agni-tattva</term>, grain will become expensive; if it is Earth <term>prithvi-tattva</term>, it will be slightly expensive; if it is Air <term>baadi</term> <note>original: "बादी" (baadi), from the Persian 'bād' for wind/air.</note>, the price will remain moderate; and if it is the Water element <term>jal-tattva</term>, it will be very cheap. || 9 ||

**Question: How does my father feel toward me?** In that case, having prepared the chart <term>prastaar</term>, one should examine the fourth house. If an auspicious "entering" figure <term>dakhil shakal</term> <note>Figures that suggest gain, arrival, or internal movement.</note> is in the fourth house, he holds great love for you; if it is a "fixed" <term>sabit</term> figure, the love is moderate; if it is an "exiting" <term>kharij</term> figure, there is no love; if it is a "changing" <term>munqalib</term> figure, he shows love outwardly but does not hold love in his heart. || 10 ||

**Question: Will I get employment or not?** Having prepared the chart, examine the second house. If an "entering" figure <term>dakhil</term> appears, employment will be obtained very quickly; if an "exiting" figure <term>kharij</term> appears, there will be no employment; if it is a "fixed" <term>sabit</term> figure, employment will be obtained after a delay; if it is a "changing" <term>munqalib</term> figure, a little employment will be obtained after a delay; if it is inauspicious, it will not happen at all. || 11 ||

**Question: If someone asks whether money <note>original: "खरचा" (kharcha), literally 'expenses' or 'funds'.</note> from a person abroad will arrive or not?** Having prepared the chart, look at the fifth house. If these figures <insert>⸪-⸪-</insert> <insert>-⸪-⸪</insert> <note>These represent specific geomantic figures composed of single and double dots.</note> are in the 5th house, the money will arrive and the person abroad is happy; if these "exiting" figures <insert>⸪--⸪</insert> <insert>-⸪⸪-</insert> <insert>⸪⸪--</insert> appear, then no money or news will come; if an auspicious "fixed" figure <term>sabit</term> appears, the money will arrive with delay; if a "changing" figure <term>munqalib</term> appears, the money will not come, only news will arrive. || 12 ||

**Question: I want to ask someone for an object; will they give it or not?** Having prepared the chart, perform a combination <term>jabr</term> <note>original: "जब्र" (jabr). A technical term in Ramal for a mathematical operation where two figures are combined to create a third resultant figure.</note> of the sixth and tenth figures to make one. If that result is an auspicious "entering" <term>dakhil</term> figure, they will give it; if it is an auspicious "changing" <term>munqalib</term> figure, they will give it very quickly; if it is an inauspicious "changing" figure, they will give it but then take it back; if it is an inauspicious "exiting" <term>kharij</term> figure, they will not give it; if it is an auspicious "fixed" <term>sabit</term> figure, they will give it after a delay; if it is an auspicious "exiting" figure, they will offer comfort/reassurance but will not actually give the object. || 13 ||

### [ Section on Practical Procedures ]

**Question—** If someone asks whether the beloved <note>original: "mashuq" (प्रियजन), a term of Persian origin for a sweetheart or object of affection.</note> will be attained or not, whether news of a traveler is true or false, and whether the traveler will return in person or not? To answer these questions, one must examine the fifth house of the chart. If any of these figures ≡ ⁚ ⁚ <note>These symbols represent geomantic "shakals" or figures composed of dots and lines.</note> appear there, the beloved will be attained. Furthermore, the traveler's news will arrive, and the news that arrived previously is true. However, if any of these figures ⁚ ⁚ ≡ appear, the traveler remains where they are and will not return, and the beloved will not be attained for some days. If any of these figures ⁝ ⁚ ⁝ appear, the traveler is no longer alive, the beloved will not be attained, and the news is false. || 14 ||

**Question—** Will the pregnant woman give birth to a son or a daughter? For this, cast the <term>ramal</term> <note>The practice of geomancy, literally "divining by sand."</note> and generate the sixteen figures. Combine <term>jarb</term> <note>original: "जर्ब" (jarb); the technical process of "multiplying" or merging two geomantic figures to produce a resulting third figure.</note> the first and the tenth figures; then combine that result with the sixth figure. If the resulting figure is among ≡ ⁝ ≡ ⁝ ⁝ ⁚ ⁝, she will give birth to a handsome son. If it is among ≡ ⁚ ≡ ⁝ ≡ ⁝, she will give birth to a daughter. A second method is this: combine the first and fifth figures, and combine the sixth and seventh figures. Then, create a single figure from those two results and examine it. If it belongs to the Fire element <term>agni-tattva</term> or the Air element <term>vayu-tattva</term>, she will bear a son. If it belongs to the Earth <term>prithvi</term> or Water element <term>jal-tattva</term>, she will bear a daughter. || 15 ||

**Question—** If someone asks whether a woman is pregnant or not? In that case, examine the sixth house. If the figure is "Entering and Fixed" <term>dakhil sabit</term> <note>A figure that indicates something coming in and staying.</note>, she is pregnant. If it is "Exiting" <term>kharij</term>, she is not pregnant. If it is "Changing" <term>munqalib</term> <note>original: "मुन्कळीव" (munqalib); a figure indicating flux or instability.</note>, she is pregnant, but it will be difficult for the pregnancy to reach term. To determine if the pregnancy will stay, examine the thirteenth and seventh figures; if they are "Entering and Fixed" <term>dakhil sabit</term>, the pregnancy will remain; if they are "Exiting or Changing" <term>kharij munqalib</term>, it will not remain. || 16 ||

**Question—** Will the woman give birth to the child <note>original: "balak" (वाळक)</note> with ease or with pain? For this, prepare the chart <term>prastaar</term> and look at the sixth house. If an auspicious "Entering" figure <term>dakhil</term> or an auspicious "Exiting" figure <term>kharij</term> appears, she will give birth with ease. If an auspicious "Fixed" figure <term>sabit</term> appears, then through some illness...

**Question—** If someone asks how many months pregnant a woman is? If they belong to Mars <term>二</term><term>⠅</term> <term>二</term> <term>二</term><term>⠅</term><term>二</term>, she is five months pregnant. If they belong to Jupiter, <term>⠅</term><term>二</term><term>⠅</term> <term>⠅</term><term>二</term><term>⠅</term>, she is six months pregnant. If a figure of Mercury appears, she is 3–4 months pregnant. || 18 ||

**Question—** On what day and at what time will this woman give birth? For this, examine the eleventh house of the chart. If the figure of the Sun appears, the birth will occur on a Sunday. In this same manner, whatever planet's figure appears in the eleventh house, name that corresponding day. To determine the time of birth, first examine the first house. If in the first house these figures <term>二</term><term>⠅</term><term>二</term> <term>二</term><term>⠅</term><term>二</term> <term>⠅</term><term>二</term><term>⠅</term> <term>⠅</term><term>二</term><term>⠅</term> appear, she will give birth in the evening or at midnight. If <term>⋮</term> <term>三</term> appear, then at sunrise. If <term>二</term><term>⠅</term> appears, then after midnight. One should also consider and speak from the day and night figures described in the <term>Shakun Pankti-chakra</term> <note>A "row-chart" of omens used to correlate geomantic figures with specific times of day or night.</note>. || 19 ||

**Question—** Which day, month, or year is difficult <note>original: "karada" (hard/harsh/difficult)</note> for this child? To answer, create the chart and examine the eighth house <note>The eighth house in both astrology and geomancy traditionally relates to death, crises, and hidden dangers.</note>. If <term>三</term> appears there, then 14 days or 14 years will be difficult. If <term>二</term><term>⠅</term> appears, then 30 days or the thirtieth year will be difficult. If <term>三</term> appears, then 40 days or the fortieth year will be heavy <note>original: "bhari" (heavy); used here to mean a period of significant karmic weight or danger.</note>.

**Question—** If someone asks: "My animal or bird has been lost; will it be found or not?" In that case, create the <term>Prastara</term> <note>original: "prastara" (a spread); the geomantic chart or grid used to layout the sixteen figures.</note> and contemplate the second house.

If **☱** appears, he is of a reddish complexion, tall, with black marks or spots on his face and hands; he has a mole or a wart on his hand. He carries a stick, is stylish in his dress, but has a crooked or bent posture.
If **☷** <term>Jama’at</term> <note>original: "jama'at" (The Congregation); representing the figure *Populus*.</note> appears, he is dusky with a short neck and black eyebrows.

**Question — What is the patient’s ailment?**

In this matter, cast the <term>Ramal</term> <note>original: "ramal"; a system of divination using sixteen figures formed by dots and lines.</note> and construct the chart <note>original: "prastara"; the grid or layout of the geomantic reading.</note>. Examine the sixth house <note>In astrology and geomancy, the sixth house typically represents illness, debt, and enemies.</note>. 

If the figure <term>Lahyan</term> ≡ <note>original: "Lahyan" (Arabic: *Al-Lihyan*, "The Bearded"). This figure is associated with Jupiter.</note> appears there, then a few days prior, there was a disorder caused by heat. In that state of confusion, the patient bathed or failed to follow a proper diet <note>original: "kupathya"; dietary mistakes that aggravate an illness.</note>, and from this, the illness arose. Consider this: one should perform acts of charity and merit in the name of the planet Jupiter <note>original: "Brihaspati".</note>.

If <term>Kanjul dakhil</term> ⠇ <note>original: "Kanjul-daakhil" (Arabic: *Atabat al-Dakhila*, "The Entering Threshold").</note> appears, they have eaten something white. Because that item was cold in nature, the affliction <note>original: "aarja"; a sickness or trouble.</note> occurred. For this, perform charity for the Sun. 

If it is <term>Kanjulkharij</term> ⠇ <note>original: "Kanjul-khaarij" (Arabic: *Atabat al-Kharija*, "The Exiting Threshold").</note>, the person went to bathe or perform some task in a filthy place, where a "shadow" fell upon them <note>original: "chhaya"; a common term for spirit possession or the evil eye.</note>. In this case, one should bind a protective amulet <note>original: "yantra"; a mystical diagram used for protection.</note> to destroy ghosts and spirits, perform a sacrifice <note>original: "balidan".</note>, and by performing charity, merit-making, chanting, and recitations for the planet <term>Rahu</term> <note>the North Node of the Moon, associated with sudden illnesses and spirits.</note>, the disease will be removed.

If <term>Jamaat</term> ≡ <note>original: "Jama’at" (Arabic: *Al-Jama’ah*, "The Congregation").</note> appears, then benefit will come from charity and merit-making for the planet Mercury <note>original: "Budh".</note>. 

If <term>Farha</term> ⠇ <note>original: "Farha" (Arabic: *Al-Farah*, "The Joy").</note> appears, the affliction was caused by cold. The patient drank water at night or ate something at the crack of dawn <note>original: "fajar"; the first light of day.</note>. The illness has been caused by indigestion <note>original: "ajirna".</note>. They should perform charity and merit-making according to their means.

If <term>Uqla</term> ⠇ <note>original: "Uqla" (Arabic: *Al-Uqla*, "The Shackle").</note> appears, there is a "shadow" (an obstruction caused by ghosts or spirits). The stomach feels heavy. There is constant pain and a desire to eat, but the food will not pass down the throat. They should give charity of grain, clothes, and the like, and have charity, merit-making, and chanting performed for the planet Saturn <note>original: "Shanaishvar".</note>.

If <term>Ankish</term> ≡ <note>original: "Ankish" (Arabic: *Al-Ankis*, "The Reversed").</note> appears, the stomach is heavy. There is very little appetite. This is the fault of the ancestors <note>original: "pitar".</note> or the family deity <note>original: "ishtadeva".</note>. When one performs charity and merit-making for their family deity, then peace will come.

If this <term>Humra</term> ≡ <note>original: "Humra" (Arabic: *Al-Hamra*, "The Red"). Associated with Mars.</note> figure appears, there is some disease of the blood. One should feed Brahmins in the name of the planet Mars <note>original: "Mangal".</note> and have chanting and recitations performed.

**Question—Will this man abandon this particular woman or not?** In this case, construct the <term>Prastar</term> <note>The geomantic chart or array of sixteen houses.</note> and examine the seventh house. ⁚ ⁚ = = If these <term>Shakal</term> <note>Figures or shapes used in geomancy.</note> appear, he will leave her. If ≡ = ⁚ ⁚ = these appear, he will not leave her. If a <term>Munkalib</term> <note>Arabic: *Munqalib*; a "reversible" or unstable figure. In geomancy, these figures indicate change, transition, or a state of flux.</note> figure appears, say that sometimes he will leave and sometimes he will not. Thus ends the section. || 26 ||

**Question—The traveler who has gone abroad: has he died? Is he happy or sad? Does he have employment or not?** In this case, construct the chart and examine the eighth house. If a <term>Shubha Dakhil</term> <note>A "Fortunate Inward" or "Entering" figure, such as *Al-Uql* (The Intellect) or *An-Nusratu ad-Dakhilah* (Inward Victory).</note> appears in the eighth house, he is happy and earning a good living. If a "Fortunate Reversible" (Shubha Munkalib) figure appears, he is living in a good state and desires to return. If an "Unfortunate Reversible" (Ashubha Munkalib) figure appears, he is alive, but there is some instability or upheaval in his business. If an "Unfortunate Outward" (Ashubha Kharij) figure appears ⁚ ⁚ he is no longer alive. If a "Fortunate Outward" (Shubha Kharij) ≡ = appears, he is alive, but his current state is not good. If an "Unfortunate Fixed" (Ashubha Sabit) = <term>Humra</term> <note>Arabic: *Al-Humra*, meaning "The Red." This figure is associated with Mars and often signifies strife, blood, or harshness.</note> appears, he has died a harsh or violent death. Thus ends the section. || 27 ||

**Question—Will the entrusted deposit be returned or not?** In this case, construct the chart and perform a <term>Jarv</term> <note>Arabic: *Darb*; the mathematical process of combining or "multiplying" two geomantic figures to produce a third resultant figure.</note> of the second and sixth houses. If a Fortunate Entering (Shubha Dakhil) and a Fortunate Fixed (Shubha Sabit) figure appear, the deposit will be returned—that is, the person will act as a guarantor. If the figure is a Fortunate or Unfortunate Reversible (Munkalib), the person will not return it; they may give it and then take it back or deny it. If an Unfortunate Entering, Unfortunate Fixed, or Unfortunate Outward figure appears, they will not return the deposit and will only offer empty reassurances. Thus ends the section. || 28 ||

**Question—I am going into a fight or conflict; will I be victorious or not?** In this case, cast the <term>Ramal</term> <note>The geomantic divination system.</note> and construct the chart. If the first figure is a Fortunate Entering (Shubha Dakhil), there will be victory. If it is an Unfortunate Entering (Ashubha Dakhil), there will be no victory. If an Unfortunate Reversible (Ashubha Munkalib) or an Unfortunate Outward (Ashubha Kharij) figure appears, there will be no victory. If an Unfortunate Fixed (Ashubha Sabit) figure appears, victory will come after a delay. Thus ends the section. || 29 ||

<meta>This page continues the divinatory instructions for the practice of Ramal (Islamic geomancy). It moves from inquiries regarding travelers and missing persons to determining the outcome of battles and interpreting "unspoken" or "mute" questions by locating specific figures within the sixteen houses of the chart.</meta>

<page-num>152</page-num>
<header>[ Ramal Daniyal— </header> <note>original: "Ramal-Daniyal"—referring to the Prophet Daniel, who is traditionally credited in Islamic tradition as the recipient of the divine revelation of geomancy.</note>

**Question—I am going to fight an enemy; will the victory (victory) be mine or will it be his?** In this matter, construct the <term>Prastar</term> <note>The geomantic chart or grid consisting of sixteen houses.</note> and examine the eighth house. If a "Fortunate Inward" <note>original: "Shubha Dakhil"; a figure like *Al-Uql* (The Intellect) indicating things entering or coming to the querent.</note> or a "Fortunate Reversible" <note>original: "Shubha Munkalib"; a figure indicating change or transition.</note> figure appears there, the victory is yours. If a "Fortunate Outward" <note>original: "Shubha Kharij"; indicating things leaving or moving away.</note> or an "Unfortunate Outward" <note>original: "Ashubha Kharij"</note> figure appears, the victory (victory) will belong to the enemy. If a "Fortunate Stable" <note>original: "Shubha Savit" (from the Arabic *Thabit*); a fixed or unchanging figure.</note> figure appears, the victory will be mine. If an "Unfortunate Reversible," "Unfortunate Stable," or "Unfortunate Inward" figure appears, the victory will go to the enemy. Furthermore, if "Fortunate Figures" <note>original: "Shubha Shakal"</note> appear in houses 1, 2, 3, 4, 9, 10, 11, 13, and 15, and if there are many lines <note>Refers to the count of points/dots within the geomantic figures.</note> present, then, O seeker, the victory will be yours alone. If such figures and many lines appear in houses 5, 6, 7, 8, 12, and 16, then, O <term>Querent</term> <note>original: "Prichak"; the person asking the question.</note>, the victory will belong to your enemy. Thus ends this section. || 30 ||

If someone asks a **mute question** <note>original: "mook prashna"; a silent or unspoken query where the seeker does not tell the diviner what they are thinking of.</note>, cast the <term>Ramal</term> <note>The practice of geomancy, literally "divination by sand."</note> and construct the chart. Then, look for "The Red" figure <note>original: "Humra Shakal"; the geomantic figure *Al-Hamra* (The Red), associated with the planet Mars, blood, and conflict.</note> to see where it sits in the chart. If it appears in the **first house**, then some work related to war has been done; the answer is regarding a fight that is your responsibility; the enemy’s strength is currently greater. 

If "The Red" appears in the **second house**, the question concerns employment; the employment will be good. If it concerns a traveler, they may return; there is an intention to move, yet they may not depart. If there is an intention for a meeting or a marriage, do not proceed with it. If there is an intention for a legal claim (a dispute), do not pursue it. 

If it appears in the **third house**, it indicates a fight with brothers or friends; one who lives in a poor state regarding you will bring you down. After some days, it will only show you hardship. 

If it appears in the **fourth house**, then nothing will be gained; it will not come into your hands. A fight may occur, and the enemy is currently furious with you. If you go to someone to lodge a formal complaint (a plea for justice), your complaint will not be heard. If the question is about a person abroad, they are coming from the heart <note>Meaning they are thinking of home or acting on emotion.</note>. If there is an intention to travel, do not go. Whatever work is being done, ensure it is done correctly. 

If it appears in the **fifth house**, there is sorrow (melancholy). If you wish to purchase animals or other items associated with grief, do not do so. However, that which you have lost shall be found, and the loved one who has departed shall also return.

You will meet a friend on the road from whom some gain will be obtained. If ☷ <note>This symbol represents "The Gathering" or "Populus."</note> appears, then do not travel. There is danger in going. If **The Gathering** <note>original: "Jamaat"</note> or **Conjunction** <note>original: "Ijtima"</note> appear, do not go, as there will be no benefit. Your work will be accomplished right here. If **Redness** <note>original: "Humra" (Arabic: Al-Humrah); associated with the planet Mars and often indicates conflict.</note> appears, there is danger mid-way; the body will suffer distress at the hands of thieves. End of section 37.

### Question—In which direction is it good for me to go?
In that case, create the **alignment** <term>prastara</term> <note>The geometric grid or chart used in Ramal divination.</note> and look at the **ninth house**. If these figures appear: ⚀ ⚁ ☷ ☵, then there is benefit from the **South**. If these appear: ⚀ ☷ ⚁ ⚂, then it is good to go in the **East**. If these appear: ☵ ⚃ ⚄ ☷, then there is benefit in the **West**. If these appear: ⚀ ☵ ⚌, then there is profit from the **North**. End of section 38.

### Question—What kind of people will I meet?
In that case, look at the **ninth house** of the alignment. If ⚂ appears, you will meet a **great man** <note>A person of high status or authority.</note>. If ⚁ appears, there will be benefit from **friends**; a meeting will occur and some profit will be gained. If ☷ appears, a meeting with **great people** will occur. If ⚀ appears, you will meet a **wealthy person**. If ☷ ☷ these appear, you will meet a **scholar** <term>ilmdar</term> <note>original: "Ilmdar"; a person possessing "ilm" or specialized knowledge, usually religious or scientific.</note>. If ☵... these figures appear, you will meet **cloth merchants** <term>bajajon</term>. If ⚁ ⚀ ⚄ these appear, you will meet and associate with **thieves**, yet some benefit will come of it. If ☷ **Redness** <note>original: "Humra"</note> appears, you will meet some **violent and wicked person**. End of section 39.

### Question—Does that man love me or not?
In that case, create the alignment and look at the **first house**. If an **Entering Auspicious** <term>shubh-dakhil</term> figure appears, he loves you very much and will also give you something. If a **Reversing Auspicious** <term>shubh-munkalib</term> figure appears, he loves you sometimes and sometimes he does not. And if **Stable** <term>sabit</term>, **The Gathering** <note>original: "Jamaat"</note>, **The White** <note>original: "Bayaz" (Arabic: Al-Bayad)</note>, **Conjunction** <note>original: "Ijtima"</note>, or **Redness** <note>original: "Humra"</note> appear, he will become kind after a long time <note>original: "muddat."</note>. If an **Outgoing Auspicious** <note>original: "Shubh Kharij"</note>, **The Tongue** <note>original: "Lahyan"</note>, or **External Victory** <note>original: "Nusrut-ul-Kharij"</note> appear, he will be a little kind. If an **Inauspicious Outgoing** <note>original: "Ashubh Kharij"</note> figure appears, he will not have true love. End of section 40.

<header>Questions on the Outcome of Tasks. ] ( 157 )</header>

### Questions on the Outcome of Tasks. ]

**Question—What object is in my hand? And what is its color?**
In this case, create the **alignment** <term>prastara</term> <note>The geometric grid used in Ramal divination to arrange the sixteen figures.</note> and look at the first house.
If **⋮** appears, say the color is **white**.
If **ल० ⋮** appears, it is a **yellow** <note>original: "jard"</note> color.
If **⁚** appears, it is a **plain, good** color.
If **≡** appears, it is a **white** color.
If **⋮ ⁚** are present, it is a **yellow** color.
If **⁛** appears, it is a **red** color.
If **≡ ≡** appears, the color of this object is **black** <note>original: "syah"</note>.
Look at the first house; if **The Gain** <note>original: "Lahyan" (Arabic: Al-Lihyan); representing a bearded man or a person of wisdom.</note> or **Redness** <note>original: "Humra" (Arabic: Al-Humrah)</note> appears there, it is a **yellow-white** color and is a sweet and valuable object.
If **⋮** appears, it is an **earthy** <note>original: "khaki"</note> color and the taste is sweet.
If **⁚** appears, it is **black-white** and the taste is sour.
If **≡** appears, it is a **mixed** <note>original: "mumukse"</note> color and is **unattractive** <note>original: "buruk"</note>.
If **—** appears, it is a **dark/azure** <note>original: "shyam"</note> color and has a bitter taste.
If **≡** appears, it is a **black** color and a valuable, **fragrant** <note>original: "khusboi"</note> item.
If **Outward Capture** <note>original: "Kabjul Kharij" (Arabic: Qabd al-Kharij); representing loss or something leaving.</note> appears, it is a **white and green** color.
If the figure **Whiteness** <note>original: "Bayaz" (Arabic: Al-Bayad)</note> appears, it is a **white and fragrant** object.
If **—** appears, it is a **red** color.
If **⋮** appears, it is a **red-white** edible object.
If the figure **⁚** appears, it is a **yellow-white**, sweet, and expensive item.
If **The Way** <note>original: "Tarikha" (Arabic: At-Tariq)</note> appears, it is a **blue** object. Thus ends section 41.

**Question—Where is the lost object?**
In that case, create the alignment and look at the **fourth house** <note>In traditional astrology and geomancy, the fourth house represents hidden treasures, the home, and things beneath the earth.</note>.
If an **Inward** <note>original: "dakhil"</note> or **Fixed** <note>original: "sabit"</note> figure appears, the object is **inside the earth**.
If an **Outward** <note>original: "kharij"</note> or **Mobile** <note>original: "munkalib"</note> figure appears, it is **not in the house**. Thus ends section 42.

**Question—Has the foreigner married a woman or not?**
In that case, create the alignment and look at the **seventh house** <note>The seventh house traditionally governs marriage and partnerships.</note>.
If an **auspicious inward** figure appears, he has married.
If an **auspicious mobile** figure appears, he has either married or intends to marry.
If an **auspicious fixed** figure appears, many days have passed since he married.
If an **inauspicious outward** or **auspicious outward** figure appears, he has **not married** and is in a poor state.
If an **inauspicious mobile** figure appears, he has not married; he wishes to, but she is not within his reach.
If an **inauspicious fixed** or **inauspicious inward** figure appears, then the foreigner is in a **bad state** there. Thus ends section 43.

<header>[ Ramal-Daniyal— </header>

**Question—Will the King or Emperor grant me a reward or a specific rank?**
In this matter, create the **alignment** <term>prastara</term> <note>The grid or map used to arrange the sixteen geomantic figures.</note> and examine the **tenth house** <note>In this system, the tenth house represents authority, career, and the sovereign.</note>. If a **favorable "Entering"** <term>dakhil</term> <note>original: "shubha dakhil"; figures representing energy moving inward or things being received.</note> figure appears, then he will grant you a reward or position. If a **favorable "Changeable"** <term>munqalib</term> <note>original: "shubha munqalib"; figures that suggest movement or transformation.</note> figure appears, he intends to give it to you but will first put you to a test. If an **unfavorable "Entering," "Changeable," or "Stable"** <term>sabit</term> <note>original: "ashubha sabit"; "stable" figures represent things staying as they are.</note> figure appears, then an informant has slandered you to him. If an **"Exiting"** <term>kharij</term> <note>original: "kharij"; figures representing energy moving away or things leaving.</note> figure appears, then your current rank or previously held rewards will be seized. Thus ends the section. || 44 ||

**Question—Is the thief still in the city, or has he fled outside?**
Create the alignment and examine the **seventh house** <note>The seventh house typically represents "the other," including thieves or opponents.</note>. If a **favorable "Entering"** figure appears there, the thief is still in the city. If **The White** <term>bayaz</term>, **The Conjunction** <term>ijtema</term>, or **The Red** <term>humra</term> <note>Arabic: Al-Bayad, Al-Ijtima, and Al-Humrah respectively.</note> figures appear, the thief is no longer in the city. If **The Gathering** <term>jamat</term> <note>Arabic: Al-Jama'ah.</note> appears, then the thief is within the city. If **≡** <note>The figure for "The White."</note> this figure appears, the thief is in a wretched state and is sitting by the road. If **= : . :** these appear, he is sitting in a place where stories or religious discourses are being told. Thus ends the section. || 45 ||

**Question—Will the lost object be recovered or not?**
Create the alignment and examine the **fourteenth house**. If **Inward Gain** <term>qabz-ud-dakhil</term> or the **Inner Threshold** <term>utba-dakhil</term> <note>Arabic: Qabd al-Dakhil and Utbat al-Dakhil.</note> appears, it will be found after some days; however, if you search for it with great haste, it will not be found. If **Outward Gain** <term>qabz-ud-kharij</term>, the **Outer Threshold** <term>utba-kharij</term>, or **The Beard** <term>lihyan</term> <note>Arabic: Qabd al-Kharij, Utbat al-Kharij, and Al-Lihyan.</note> appear, you will not find it. If **Joy** <term>farah</term>, **The Path** <term>tariq</term> <note>Arabic: Al-Farah and Al-Tariq.</note>, or a **favorable "Entering"** figure appears, you will find it. If **"Changeable"** <term>munqalib</term> figures appear, you will still not find it. Thus ends the section. || 46 ||

**Question—How many thieves are there, and in which direction did they go?**
Create the alignment and look at the figure in the **seventh house**. Count how many times that specific figure appears throughout the entire grid; that is the number of thieves. Then, look at all **sixteen houses** of the alignment. Collect the total number of **dots** <note>original: "shunya" (zeros/voids); referring to the dots that make up the geomantic figures.</note> from all the figures and divide that sum by four. If the remainder is **one**, the thief went **East**. If the remainder is **two**, he went **West**. If the remainder is **three**, he went **North**. If the remainder is **four** (zero), he went **South**. Thus ends the section. || 47 ||

<header>Questions on Hidden Objects and Silent Inquiries ] ( 159 )</header>

If someone asks: "Will I receive a loan from a specific person?" In this matter, the first and second **houses** <term>ghar</term> <note>In geomancy, houses represent different areas of life; the first and second usually represent the self and resources.</note> belong to the one asking, and the seventh and eighth **houses** belong to the person from whom they wish to borrow. Consider the auspicious or inauspicious nature of these, and then look at the sixth **house** of the **alignment** <term>prastara</term> <note>The grid or chart of sixteen geomantic figures.</note>. If a **favorable "Exiting"** <term>kharij</term> <note>original: "shubha kharij"; figures representing movement outward, here signifying the lender releasing the money.</note> figure appears there, the loan will be granted and there will be a profit. If an **unfavorable "Exiting"** figure appears, there will be a delay. If an **"Entering"** <term>dakhil</term> or **"Fixed"** <term>sabit</term> <note>original: "dakhil" and "sabit"; these figures suggest things staying where they are or moving inward, meaning the money stays with the lender.</note> figure appears, the loan will not be received and much hardship will be faced. Even if a **"Changeable"** <term>munqalib</term> figure appears, the loan will not be received. Thus ends the section. || 48 ||

## Regarding the Question of Hidden Objects

After casting the **geomantic figures** <term>ramal</term> and creating the **alignment** <term>prastara</term>, examine the seventh **house**. If a figure associated with the **Fire element** or the **East** appears, the object is made of metal. If it is a figure of the **Air element** or the **West**, the question concerns a living being <note>original: "jeeva-prashna"; inquiries regarding humans or animals.</note>. If the seventh house shows a figure of the **North**, mention grass, wood, fruit, and so on. If in the seventh house there is a figure of the **South** or the **Earth element**, state that there is a stone, gem, pearl, or coral in the **closed fist** <term>mushti</term>. || 49 ||

->Thus ends the section on hidden objects.<-

Now we consider the method for discerning a **silent question** <term>muka prashna</term> <note>A "silent question" is a demonstration of the diviner's skill where they identify what the seeker is thinking about without being told.</note>—After creating the **alignment**, look at the first **house**. If an **"Entering"** <term>dakhil</term> or **"Fixed"** <term>sabit</term> figure appears in the first house, the question is about profit, wealth, or the acquisition of a place. It may also be a question about meeting someone, or the recovery of an item that has gone missing. If a **favorable "Entering"** figure appears in the first [house], it indicates a question regarding anxiety, dependency, or distress; the seeker cannot say anything to anyone, or wishes to go somewhere but cannot go. If a **favorable "Exiting"** <term>kharij</term> figure falls in the first house of the **alignment**, the question is about obtaining an object that is far away; the seeker thinks of it constantly, [as if to say] "a good thing is far from me," or there is something from which...

...wishes to be released. If the seeker asks, "I am undertaking a certain task; will the outcome be good or bad?" and an **Inauspicious "Fixed"** <term>sabit</term> <note>A figure representing stability or lack of movement; here, it traps the seeker in a bad situation.</note> figure is proven, the question relates to the fear of an enemy, or fear of anxiety and imprisonment. ## A Second Method for Discerning the Silent Question

Now, another method for addressing the **Silent Question** <term>mook prashna</term> <note>A specialized divinatory feat where the practitioner identifies the seeker's unspoken thought before answering it.</note> is described. After creating the **alignment** <term>prastara</term> <note>The mathematical grid of sixteen positions used in geomancy.</note>:

1. First, examine the figure in the **first house**. 
2. If the figure in the first house belongs to the **Fire element** <term>agni tattva</term> and the "Fire point" is "open" <note>In geomancy, each figure consists of four levels (Fire, Air, Water, Earth). A single dot at a level is considered "open" or active, whereas two dots are "closed" or stable.</note>, then the question concerns wealth or property. If that same figure is repeated elsewhere in the alignment, the status of the matter should be judged by the house where it reappears.
3. If it is an **Air figure** and the "Air point" is open, the question concerns a living being <term>jiva</term>. If the figure is classified as female, the question is about a woman; if male, it is about a man.
4. If it is a **Water figure** and the "Water point" is open, the question concerns agricultural work, or planting a garden or orchard. 
5. If it is an **Earth figure** and the "Earth point" is open, the question concerns obtaining a house, a country, a village, or land.

If multiple points are open, the interpretation should be formed by combining the meanings of all those active elements. || 51 || Thus ends the section on the types of Silent Questions.

## Characteristics of the Sixteen Figures

Now, in proper order, we describe the names, forms, and natures—such as "Exiting," "Dual-natured" <term>dvisvabhava</term>, gender (male/female), strength during day or night, auspiciousness, direction, element, zodiac sign, planetary ruler, and whether they are "Movable" or "Fixed"—for all sixteen figures.

### 1. Lahyan Ξ
The figure **Lahyan** Ξ is:
*   **Nature:** Exiting <term>kharij</term>
*   **Quality:** Dual-natured
*   **Gender:** Male
*   **Strength:** Strong during the day
*   **Auspiciousness:** Auspicious <note>original: "shubha"</note>
*   **Direction:** East
*   **Element:** Fire <term>agni tattva</term>
*   **Zodiac:** Sagittarius <note>original: "Dhanu rashi"</note>
*   **Ruler:** Jupiter <note>original: "Brihaspati"</note>
*   **Classification:** Movable <term>char</term>

<header>[Names and Forms of the Figures]</header>

The **Kanjul Dakhil** <term>Kanjul Dakhil</term> <note>Arabic: *al-idkhilal* or *Acquisitio*. A figure signifying gain and "Entering."</note> figure is **⁛**. It is of the **Entering** <term>dakhil</term> nature, **dual-natured** <term>dvishvabhav</term> <note>Possessing qualities of both fixed and movable signs.</note>, and feminine. It is powerful at night, auspicious, and belongs to the South. Its element is **Earth**, its zodiac sign is **Leo**, its ruler is the **Sun**, and it is classified as **Fixed**. || 2 ||

The **Kanjul Kharij** <term>Kanjul Kharij</term> <note>Arabic: *al-kharij* or *Amissio*. A figure signifying loss or "Exiting."</note> figure is **-:**. It is **dual-natured**, masculine, and powerful during the day. It is inauspicious and belongs to the East. Its element is **Fire**, its zodiac sign is **Aquarius**, its ruler is **Rahu** <note>The North Node of the Moon, considered a "shadow planet" in Vedic astrology.</note>, and it is classified as **Movable**. || 3 ||

The **Jamaat** <term>Jamaat</term> <note>Arabic: *al-jama'ah* or *Populus*. Signifies a crowd or assembly.</note> figure is **≡**. It is **Fixed** <term>sabit</term>, stable, and neuter. It is powerful at the time of twilight and is of medium (neutral) quality. It belongs to the South. Its element is **Earth**, its zodiac sign is **Gemini**, its ruler is **Mercury**, and it is classified as **Fixed**. || 4 ||

The **Farha** <term>Farha</term> <note>Arabic: *al-farah* or *Laetitia*. Signifies joy or happiness.</note> figure is **-·**. It is **Changeable** <term>munqalib</term>, movable, and masculine. It is powerful at the time of twilight and is auspicious. It belongs to the West. Its element is **Air**, its zodiac sign is **Libra**, its ruler is **Venus**, and it is classified as **Movable**. || 5 ||

The **Ukda** <term>Ukda</term> <note>Arabic: *al-uqdah* or *Cauda Draconis*. Signifies a "Knot" or the Dragon's Tail.</note> figure is **·-**. It is **Changeable**, movable, and neuter. It is powerful at the time of twilight and is inauspicious. It belongs to the South. Its element is **Earth**, its zodiac sign is **Capricorn**, its ruler is **Saturn**, and it is classified as **Movable**. || 6 ||

The **Ankeesh** <term>Ankeesh</term> <note>Arabic: *al-ankis* or *Conjunction*. Often associated with complications.</note> figure is **≡**. It is of the **Entering** nature, **dual-natured**, and feminine. It is powerful at night and is inauspicious. It belongs to the South. Its element is **Earth**, its ruler is **Saturn**, its zodiac sign is **Aquarius**, and it is classified as **Fixed**. || 7 ||

The **Humra** <term>Humra</term> <note>Arabic: *al-humrah* or *Rubeus*. Meaning "The Red," often associated with blood or passion.</note> figure is **=:**. It is **Fixed**, stable, and masculine. It is powerful at twilight and is inauspicious. It belongs to the West. Its element is **Air**, its zodiac sign is **Aries**, and its ruler is **Mars**. It is classified as **Fixed**. || 8 ||

The **Bayaz** <term>Bayaz</term> <note>Arabic: *al-bayad* or *Albus*. Meaning "The White," signifying purity or peace.</note> figure is **≡**. It is **Fixed** and stable. It is powerful at twilight and belongs to the North. Its element is **Water**, its zodiac sign is **Cancer**, and its ruler is the **Moon**. It is classified as **Fixed**. || 9 ||

The **Nusrat Kharij** <term>Nusrat Kharij</term> <note>Arabic: *nasru al-kharij* or *Fortuna Minor*. Signifies "External Success" or fleeting victory.</note> figure is **·=**. It is **dual-natured**, masculine, and powerful during the day. It is auspicious and belongs to the East. Its element is **Fire**, its zodiac sign is **Leo**, its ruler is the **Sun**, and it is classified as **Movable**. || 10 ||
